p_{0}\in(0,1)
,
\delta_{0},H_{0}>0
, and
\widetilde{\xi}_{1}>0
there exist
b\in(1,2),\xi_{1}\in(0,(e^{-b}-e^{-2})/2)
,
\xi_{0}\in(0,H_{0}/2)
(depending only on
p_{0},\delta_{0}
and
H_{0}
) and
\widetilde{\xi}_{0}\in(0,H_{0}/2)
(depending only on
p_{0},\delta_{0},H_{0}
and
\widetilde{\xi}_{1}
) so that the following is true. Let
F_{z,k+1}^{1}
be the event that

(i)

\varphi_{z,k+1}(A^{*}_{z,k+1}\cap\psi_{z,k+1}^{-1}(B(0,e^{-b}-\xi_{1})\setminus B(0,e^{-b}-2\xi_{1})))\subseteq(0,1)\times(\xi_{0},H_{0}-\xi_{0})
and

(ii)

\varphi_{z,k+1}(\{w:\mathrm{dist}(w,\partial A_{z,k+1}^{*})\geq\widetilde{\xi}_{1}\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,k+1}),\psi_{z,k+1}(w)\in B(0,e^{-b}-\xi_{1})\setminus B(0,e^{-b}-2\xi_{1})\})\subseteq(\widetilde{\xi}_{0},1-\widetilde{\xi}_{0})\times(\widetilde{\xi}_{0},H_{0}-\widetilde{\xi}_{0}).

Then

\mathbf{P}\left[\tau_{z,k+1}<\infty,\ \inf_{\tau_{z,k}\leq t\leq\tau_{z,k+1}}S_{t}\geq\delta_{0},\ (F_{z,k+1}^{1})^{c}\,|\,\mathcal{G}_{\tau_{z,k}}\right]{\bf 1}_{\tau_{z,k}<\infty}\leq p_{0}{\bf 1}_{\tau_{z,k}<\infty}.

Proof.

On
\tau_{z,k+1}<\infty
, let
Q_{z,k+1}=\varphi_{z,k+1}(A^{*}_{z,k+1}\cap\psi_{z,k+1}^{-1}(B(0,e^{-b}-\xi_{1})\setminus B(0,e^{-b}-2\xi_{1})))
. Note that
\varphi_{z,k+1}
is a homeomorphism on
\tau_{z,k+1}<\infty
. Hence it follows that the probability of
Q_{z,k+1}\not\subseteq(0,1)\times(0,H_{0}/2)
tends to
0
by first taking
b\to 1
and then
\xi_{1}\to 0
uniformly given
\mathcal{G}_{\tau_{z,k}}
on
\tau_{z,k}<\infty
. Indeed, this follows from the same argument used to prove Lemma
4.2
. Similarly, for fixed
b\in(1,2),\xi_{1}\in(0,(e^{-b}-e^{-2})/2)
, the probability of
Q_{z,k+1}\subseteq(0,1)\times(0,H_{0}/2)
,
Q_{z,k+1}\not\subseteq(0,1)\times(\xi_{0},H_{0}-\xi_{0})
tends to
0
as
\xi_{0}\to 0
uniformly given
\mathcal{G}_{\tau_{z,k}}
on
\tau_{z,k}<\infty
. This proves (
i
). The case of (
ii
) follows from the same argument.
∎

Lemma 4.4
.

For every
p_{0}\in(0,1)
,
a\in(0,\frac{1}{2}),\delta_{0}>0
there exists
M_{0}\geq 1
so that the following is true. Let
F_{z,k+1}^{2}
be the event that for every
m\in\mathbf{N}
such that
2^{-m}\leq\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,k+1})
the Lebesgue measure of the
2^{-m}
-neighborhood of
\eta([\sigma_{z,k+1},\tau_{z,k+1}])
is at most
M_{0}2^{m(a-1/2)}\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,k+1})^{a+3/2}
. Then we have that

\mathbf{P}\left[\tau_{z,k+1}<\infty,\ \inf_{\tau_{z,k}\leq t\leq\tau_{z,k+1}}S_{t}\geq\delta_{0},\ (F_{z,k+1}^{2})^{c}\,|\,\mathcal{G}_{\tau_{z,k}}\right]{\bf 1}_{\tau_{z,k}<\infty}\leq p_{0}{\bf 1}_{\tau_{z,k}<\infty}.

Proof.

Let
\gamma
be an
{\rm SLE}_{4}
in
\mathbf{H}
from
0
to
\infty
and fix
K\subseteq\mathbf{H}
compact. For each
m\in\mathbf{N}
we let
N_{m}
be the number of points
z\in 2^{-m}\mathbf{Z}^{2}
with
\mathrm{dist}(z,K)\leq 2^{-m}
such that
\gamma\cap B(z,2^{-m})\neq\emptyset
. Then
[
5
, Proposition 4]
implies that
\mathbf{E}[N_{m}]\lesssim 2^{3m/2}
and so
\mathbf{P}[N_{m}\geq 2^{(3/2+a)m}]\lesssim 2^{-am}
where the implicit constant depends only
K
. Let
\Lambda_{m}
be the Lebesgue measure of the
2^{-m}
-neighborhood of
\gamma\cap K
. Then we have that
\mathbf{P}[\Lambda_{m}\geq 2^{(-1/2+a)m}]\lesssim 2^{-am}
where the implicit constant tends only on
K
. This implies that
\mathbf{P}[\Lambda_{\ell}\leq 2^{(-1/2+a)\ell}\ \forall\ell\geq m]\to 1
as
m\to\infty
. We let
f_{t}=g_{t}-W_{t}
be the centered Loewner flow of
\eta
and set
h_{t}(\cdot)=f_{t}(\cdot)/\mathrm{Im}(f_{t}(z))
. Then we can make the choice of
K\subseteq\mathbf{H}
(depending only on
\delta_{0}
) so that on
\tau_{z,k+1}<\infty
and
\inf_{\tau_{z,k}\leq t\leq\tau_{z,k+1}}S_{t}\geq\delta_{0}
we have that
h_{\tau_{z,k}}(A_{z,k+1})\subseteq K
. The result then follows since
h_{\tau_{z,k}}(\eta|_{[\tau_{z,k},\infty)})
has the law of an
{\rm SLE}_{4}
in
\mathbf{H}
from
0
to
\infty
given
\mathcal{G}_{\tau_{z,k}}
on
\tau_{z,k}<\infty
and
|(h_{\tau_{z,k}}^{-1})^{\prime}|
on
K
is comparable to
\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,{k+1}})
(with constants depending only on
\delta_{0}
) on
\tau_{z,k+1}<\infty
.
∎

4.3.
Regularity of the first crossing

Figure 2.
Illustration of the setup for Lemma
4.5
. Here,
b\in(1,2)
and
\xi_{2}>0
is small so that
B(0,e^{-b}-\xi_{2})\subseteq B(0,e^{-1})\setminus\mathrm{cl}(B(0,e^{-2}))
as shown. The orange arc of
\eta([\sigma_{z,k+1},\tau_{z,k+1}])
is
I
and
W
is shown in the case that the component of
B(z_{\mathrm{R}},\delta_{1}^{1/4}\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,k+1}))\setminus\eta([0,\tau_{z,k+1}])
containing
z_{\mathrm{R}}
is contained in
W
.

The purpose of this subsection is to establish a version of Lemmas
3.1
and
3.2
which holds for the first crossing of an
{\rm SLE}_{4}
across the conformal annulus
A_{z,k+1}
(Lemma
4.5
). As in Section
4.2
, we will need to truncate on the event that
S_{t}
is not too small so that we can make the comparison between the first crossing and a radial
{\rm SLE}_{4}(2)
in a uniform manner. Some care will be needed in both the setup and the proof because, in contrast to Lemma
3.1
, we will need to find a single arc along the first crossing of the radial
{\rm SLE}_{4}(2)
and points
z_{\mathrm{L}}
and
z_{\mathrm{R}}
which are respectively close to the left and right sides such that the fraction (measured using the natural parameterization) of points along the crossing so the hyperbolic geodesic only passes through Whitney squares
Q
satisfying the condition of Lemma
3.1
is at least
3/4
. Furthermore, we will need the statement to hold for the hyperbolic metric with respect to a general simply connected domain
W
which contains a neighborhood of
z_{\mathrm{L}}
(resp.
z_{\mathrm{R}}
) which is much larger than the distance of
z_{\mathrm{L}}
(resp.
z_{\mathrm{R}}
) to the arc.

We now turn to give the precise version of the main statement; see Figure
2
for an illustration. We fix
\delta_{0},\delta_{1},\delta_{2},a\in(0,1)
,
b\in(1,2)
, and
\xi_{2}\in(0,(e^{-b}-e^{-2})/2)
. For
M_{0}>1
large we let
C_{z,k+1}
be the event that there exists a segment
I
of
\eta([\sigma_{z,k+1},\tau_{z,k+1}])
which satisfies the following. Let
I^{\mathrm{L}}
(resp.
I^{\mathrm{R}}
) be the left (resp. right) side of
I
.

(i)

There exist
z_{\mathrm{L}},z_{\mathrm{R}}\in A_{z,k+1}^{*}
such that
\delta_{2}\leq\frac{\mathrm{dist}(z_{q},I^{q})}{\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,k+1})}\leq\delta_{1}
with
\psi_{z,k+1}(z_{q})\in\partial B(0,e^{-b}-\xi_{2})
for
q\in\{\mathrm{L},\mathrm{R}\}
and satisfy the following property. Suppose that
W\subseteq A_{z,k+1}^{*}
is any simply connected domain such that the component of
B(z_{\mathrm{R}},\delta_{1}^{1/4}\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,k+1}))\setminus\eta([0,\tau_{z,k+1}])
containing
z_{\mathrm{R}}
is contained in
W
and
\mathcal{W}
is a Whitney square decomposition of
W
. Let
G^{+}
be the set of points
w\in I
such that each square
Q\in\mathcal{W}
which is intersected by the hyperbolic geodesic in
W
from
z_{\mathrm{R}}
to
w
satisfies
\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{W}(z_{\mathrm{R}},\mathrm{cen}(Q))\leq M_{0}({\mathrm{len}}(Q)/\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,k+1}))^{-a}
. Then we have that

\mu_{\eta}^{\mathrm{nat}}(G^{+})\geq\frac{3}{4}\mu_{\eta}^{\mathrm{nat}}(I).

The same moreover holds with
z_{\mathrm{L}}
in place of
z_{\mathrm{R}}
in which case we denote by
G^{-}
the set of points in
I
.

(ii)

\mu_{\eta}^{\mathrm{nat}}(I)\geq M_{0}^{-1}\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,k+1})^{3/2}
.

(iii)

\mu_{\eta}^{\mathrm{nat}}(Y\cap I)\leq M_{0}\mathrm{diam}(Y)^{3/2-a}
for all
Y
Borel.

The reason for the precise form of condition (
i
) is that in the proof of Theorem
1.1
we will need that
\varphi_{z,k+1}(z_{\mathrm{L}})
and
\varphi_{z,k+1}(z_{\mathrm{R}})
are respectively close to the right and left sides of the rectangle
(0,1)\times(0,H_{z,k+1})
and we will need both to be close to the bottom of the rectangle. In particular, if
C_{z,k+1}
and the event
F_{z,k+1}^{1}
from Lemma
4.3
both occur then
\varphi_{z,k+1}(z_{\mathrm{L}})
,
\varphi_{z,k+1}(z_{\mathrm{R}})
will be mapped into the desired areas of the rectangle.

Lemma 4.5
.

Fix
\delta_{0},p_{0},a\in(0,1)
,
b\in(1,2)
, and
\xi_{2}\in(0,(e^{-b}-e^{-2})/2)
and suppose that we have the above setup. Then for all
\delta_{1}\in(0,1)
sufficiently small there exists
M_{0}>1
and
\delta_{2}\in(0,1)
such that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}[\tau_{z,k+1}<\infty,\inf_{\tau_{z,k}\leq t\leq\tau_{z,k+1}}S_{t}\geq\delta_{0},C_{z,k+1}^{c}\,|\,\mathcal{G}_{\tau_{z,k}}]{\bf 1}_{B_{z,k}}\leq p_{0}{\bf 1}_{B_{z,k}}

where
B_{z,k}=\{\tau_{z,k}<\infty,\ S_{\tau_{z,k}}\geq\delta_{0}\}
.

Before we give the proof of Lemma
4.5
, we state and prove the following upper bound for the probability that a radial
{\rm SLE}_{4}(2)
in
\mathbf{D}
gets close to a point which holds uniformly in the location of its force point.

Lemma 4.6
.

Fix
p\in(0,1)
,
0<r_{1}<r_{2}<1
,
\theta_{0}\in(0,\pi)
, and let
\eta
be a radial
{\rm SLE}_{4}(2)
in
\mathbf{D}
from
1
and to
0
with its force point located at
e^{i\theta}
for
\theta\in[\theta_{0},2\pi-\theta_{0}]
. Then there exists
\epsilon_{0}\in(0,1)
depending only on
p
,
r_{1}
,
r_{2}
, and
\theta_{0}
such that for every
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{0})
and every
w\in\mathbf{D}
with
r_{1}\leq|w|\leq r_{2}
we have that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}[\eta\cap B(w,\epsilon)\neq\emptyset]\leq p.

Proof.

Step 1. Result for fixed
\theta
.
Fix
\theta\in[\theta_{0},2\pi-\theta_{0}]
and suppose that
p
,
r_{1}
,
r_{2}
are as in the statement of the lemma. We claim that there exists
\epsilon_{0}\in(0,1)
depending only on
p
,
r_{1}
,
r_{2}
, and
\theta
such that for every
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{0})
and every
w\in\mathbf{D}
with
r_{1}\leq|w|\leq r_{2}
we have that
\mathbf{P}[\eta\cap B(w,\epsilon)\neq\emptyset]\leq p
. Indeed, suppose that this does not hold. Then there exists a sequence
(w_{k})
in
\mathbf{D}
with
r_{1}\leq|w_{k}|\leq r_{2}
for every
k
and a sequence
(\epsilon_{k})
of positive reals with
\epsilon_{k}\to 0
as
k\to\infty
such that
\mathbf{P}[\eta\cap B(w_{k},\epsilon_{k})\neq\emptyset]\geq p
for every
k
. By passing to a subsequence if necessary, we may assume that
(w_{k})
converges to a limit
w_{0}
. Fix
\epsilon>0
. Then as
B(w_{0},\epsilon)
contains
B(w_{k},\epsilon_{k})
for all
k
large enough, it follows that
\mathbf{P}[\eta\cap B(w_{0},\epsilon)\neq\emptyset]\geq p
. Since
\epsilon>0
was arbitrary, we obtain that
\mathbf{P}[w\in\eta]\geq p
. Since
\lim_{t\to\infty}\eta(t)=0
a.s., it follows that there exists
T\in(0,\infty)
so that
\mathbf{P}[w\in\eta([0,T])]\geq p/2
. This is a contradiction since the law of
\eta|_{[0,T]}
is absolutely continuous with respect to that of an
{\rm SLE}_{4}
in
\mathbf{D}
from
-1
to
1

[
51
, Theorem 3]
.

Step 2. Uniformity in
\theta\in[\theta_{0},2\pi-\theta_{0}]
.
We will now show that we can take the value of
\epsilon_{0}\in(0,1)
from the previous step to be uniform in
\theta\in[\theta_{0},2\pi-\theta_{0}]
. Let
\eta
be an
{\rm SLE}_{4}(2)
,
(W,O)
the driving pair for
\eta
, and
\theta_{t}=\arg W_{t}-\arg O_{t}
. Fix
r_{1}^{\prime}\in(0,r_{1})
and
r_{2}^{\prime}\in(r_{2},1)
. We claim that we can find
\delta\in(0,1)
sufficiently small depending only on
r_{1},r_{2},r_{1}^{\prime},r_{2}^{\prime}
such that
g_{t}(\{\frac{r_{1}+r_{1}^{\prime}}{2}\leq|z|\leq\frac{r_{2}+r_{2}^{\prime}}{2}\})\subset\{r_{1}^{\prime}\leq|z|\leq r_{2}^{\prime}\}
for each
t\in[0,\delta]
. Indeed we set
\mathbf{D}_{t}=\mathbf{D}\setminus\eta([0,t])
and recall from
[
26
, Section 3.5]
that
\log(g_{\delta}^{\prime}(0))=\delta=-\mathbf{E}_{0}[\log(|B_{\sigma}|)]
where
B=(B_{t})
is a Brownian motion starting from
0
and
\sigma
is the first time that it exits
\mathbf{D}_{\delta}
. Let
r\in(0,1)
be such that
\eta([0,\delta])\cap B(0,1-r)\neq\emptyset
and let
s
be the last time before
\delta
that
\eta
hits
\partial B(0,1-r/2)
. Then we have that
\eta((s,\delta])\subseteq B(0,1-r/2)
and
\mathrm{diam}(\eta((s,\delta]))\geq r/2
and so
[
26
, Exercise 2.7]
implies that with probability at least
c_{1}r
,
B
hits
\eta((s,\delta])
before exiting
B(0,1-r/2)
, where
c_{1}>0
is a universal constant. It follows that
\delta\geq-c_{1}r\log(1-r/2)
. Let
r(\delta)
be the largest
r\in(0,1)
such that
\delta\geq-c_{1}r\log(1-r/2)
. Note that
\eta([0,\delta])\subseteq\mathbf{D}\setminus\mathrm{cl}(B(0,1-2r(\delta)))
and
r(\delta)\to 0
as
\delta\to 0
. Thus
[
26
, Proposition 3.57]
implies that
|\log(g_{t}(z)/z)|\leq cr(\delta)(1-\log(1-|z|))
for each
z\in\mathrm{cl}(B(0,1-4r(\delta)))
and each
t\in[0,\delta]
, where
c<\infty
is a universal constant. The claim then follows by taking
\delta
sufficiently small since
|\log(|g_{t}(z)|/|z|)|\leq|\log(g_{t}(z)/z)|
.

Fix
n\in\mathbf{N}
and assume that
\theta_{0}=\theta_{1}<\ldots<\theta_{n}=2\pi-\theta_{0}
are equally spaced. Let
\tau=\inf\{t\geq 0:\theta_{t}\in\{\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{n}\}\}
. Note that we can choose
n
sufficiently large such that
\mathbf{P}[\tau\geq\delta]\leq p/2
. Indeed
[
22
, Section 2]
implies that the law of
\theta|_{[0,\delta]}
stopped at the first time it exits
[\theta_{0}/2,2\pi-\theta_{0}/2]
is absolutely continuous with respect to the law of a standard Brownian motion
B
starting from
\theta
in the time interval
[0,\delta]
and stopped at the first time it exits
[\theta_{0}/2,2\pi-\theta_{0}/2]
. Let
\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}_{\theta}
be the law of
B
and
\widetilde{\tau}
the corresponding stopping time. Then the claim follows since
\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}_{\theta}[\widetilde{\tau}\geq\delta]\to 0
as
n\to\infty
uniformly in
\theta
and the Radon-Nikodym derivative is bounded from above and below by constants depending only on
\theta_{0}
and
\delta
. We assume that
n
is sufficiently large so that
\mathbf{P}[\tau\geq\delta]\leq p/2
. On
\{\tau\leq\delta\}
, we have that
g_{\tau}(\{\frac{r_{1}+r_{1}^{\prime}}{2}\leq|z|\leq\frac{r_{2}+r_{2}^{\prime}}{2}\})\subset\{r_{1}^{\prime}\leq|z|\leq r_{2}^{\prime}\}
. Moreover, by Step 1 there exists
\epsilon_{0}\in(0,1)
such that for every
w\in\mathbf{D}
with
r_{1}^{\prime}\leq|w|\leq r_{2}^{\prime}
and
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{0})
the probability that
g_{\tau}(\eta([\tau,\infty)))
hits
B(w,\epsilon)
is at most
p/2
and
B(w,\epsilon)\subset\{\frac{r_{1}+r_{1}^{\prime}}{2}\leq|z|\leq\frac{r_{2}+r_{2}^{\prime}}{2}\}
. Combining with
[
26
, Theorem 3.21]
(and possibly decreasing
\epsilon_{0}>0
to account for the distortion of
g_{\tau}
) implies assertion of the lemma.
∎

Figure 3.
Illustration of the proof of Lemma
4.5
. The conformal map
\psi_{z,k+1}
,
\widetilde{\psi}_{\widetilde{\tau}_{R}}
are chosen so that
1=\psi_{z,k+1}(\eta(\tau_{z,k+1}^{*}))=\widetilde{\psi}_{\widetilde{\tau}_{R}}(\widetilde{\eta}(\widetilde{\tau}_{R}))
. In particular,
\widetilde{\phi}_{R}=\psi_{z,k+1}^{-1}\circ\widetilde{\psi}_{\widetilde{\tau}_{R}}
.

As in the proof of Lemma
4.2
, we have that the law of the first crossing of
\eta
across
A_{z,k+1}
is absolutely continuous with respect to the law of a radial
{\rm SLE}_{4}(2)
curve. This will allow us to work with the conformal image of part of a two-sided whole-plane
{\rm SLE}_{4}
curve from
\infty
to
\infty
through
0
(i.e., the setting of Lemma
3.1
) in place of an
{\rm SLE}_{4}
. Some care will be needed because we need to find a pair of points close to the left and right sides of the crossing and a single arc so that the condition of Lemma
3.1
holds for both points simultaneously. We will also need to control the part of the curve after it completes the crossing to argue that it does not affect the hyperbolic geodesics near the distinguished arc. See Figure
3
for an illustration of the setup of the proof.

The first step in proving Lemma
4.5
is to show that we can transfer the result from the case of a whole-plane
{\rm SLE}_{4}
to the radial
{\rm SLE}_{4}
. From radial
{\rm SLE}_{4}
it is then easy to deduce the result for chordal
{\rm SLE}_{4}
with an absolute continuity argument (see the last proof of this section). Recall the setup and above and consider the following. Let
\widetilde{\eta}_{1}
be a whole-plane
{\rm SLE}_{4}(2)
from
\infty
to
0
and given
\widetilde{\eta}_{1}
we let
\widetilde{\eta}_{2}
be a chordal
{\rm SLE}_{4}
in
\mathbf{C}\setminus\widetilde{\eta}_{1}
from
0
to
\infty
. Then the concatenation
\widetilde{\eta}
of
\widetilde{\eta}_{1}
and
\widetilde{\eta}_{2}
is a two-sided whole-plane
{\rm SLE}_{4}
from
\infty
to
\infty
passing through
0
and we parameterize it according to the natural parameterization with
\widetilde{\eta}(0)=0
. We assume that
\widetilde{\eta}
is independent of
\eta
so that
\widetilde{\eta}
and
\eta
are on the same probability space. For each
t<0
we let
\widetilde{\psi}_{t}
be the unique conformal transformation mapping
\mathbf{C}\setminus\widetilde{\eta}((-\infty,t])
to
\mathbf{D}
with
\widetilde{\psi}_{t}(0)=0
and
\widetilde{\psi}_{t}(\widetilde{\eta}(t))=1
. For each
R>0
we also consider the stopping times
\widetilde{\sigma}_{R}=\inf\{t\in\mathbf{R}:\widetilde{\eta}(t)\in\partial B(0,R)\}
and
\widetilde{\tau}_{R}=\inf\{t\geq\widetilde{\sigma}_{R}:\widetilde{\psi}_{t}(\infty)=\psi_{z,k+1}(\infty)\}
.

Lemma 4.7
.

Fix
p_{0}\in(0,1)
. There exists
R_{0}=R_{0}(p_{0})>1
such that for all
R\geq R_{0}
,

(4.2)

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}[\widetilde{\tau}_{R}\leq\widetilde{\sigma}_{1}\,|\,\mathcal{G}_{\tau_{z,k}}]{\bf 1}_{B_{z,k}}\geq\left(1-\frac{p_{0}}{100}\right){\bf 1}_{B_{z,k}}.

Proof.

Note that if
B_{z,k}
occurs, we have that
|\psi_{z,k+1}(\infty)-1|\gtrsim\delta_{0}
where the implicit constant is universal.

Fix
R>1
. Then
\widehat{\eta}=\widetilde{\psi}_{\widetilde{\sigma}_{R}}(\widetilde{\eta}|_{[\widetilde{\sigma}_{R},0]})
is a radial
{\rm SLE}_{4}(2)
in
\mathbf{D}
from
1
to
0
with the force point located at
\widetilde{\psi}_{\widetilde{\sigma}_{R}}(\infty)\in\partial\mathbf{D}
. We parameterize
\widehat{\eta}
by log conformal radius as seen from
0
, let
(\widehat{W},\widehat{O})
be its driving pair, and set
\widehat{\theta}_{t}=\arg(\widehat{O}_{t})-\arg(\widehat{W}_{t})
. If
\{\widetilde{\tau}_{R}>\widetilde{\sigma}_{1}\}
occurs, then
\widehat{\theta}
does not hit
\arg(\psi_{z,k+1}(\infty))
in the time interval
[0,\log R+c_{1}]
where
c_{1}\in\mathbf{R}
is a universal constant. Therefore it suffices to show that the latter event occurs with probability tending to
0
as
R\to\infty
. As explained at the beginning of the proof, there exists
\theta_{0}\in[0,\pi/4]
depending only on
\delta_{0}
such that
\arg(\psi_{z,k+1}(\infty))\in[\theta_{0},2\pi-\theta_{0}]
. Let
E_{R}
be the event that there exists
t\in[0,\log R+c_{1}-2]
such that
\widehat{\theta}|_{[t,t+2]}
hits both
(0,\theta_{0}]
and
[2\pi-\theta_{0},2\pi)
and note that if
E_{R}
occurs then
\widehat{\theta}
hits
\arg(\psi_{z,k+1}(\infty))
before time
\log R+c_{1}
. Set
\widetilde{\theta}_{t}=\widehat{\theta}_{t}/2
and let
q_{t}
be the transition density for
\widetilde{\theta}
. As in (
4.1
) in the proof of Lemma
4.2
,
[
27
, Proposition 4.4]
implies that there exists
q_{0}>0
depending only on
\theta_{0}
so that if
q_{t}(\widetilde{\theta}_{0},A)=\int_{A}q_{t}(\widetilde{\theta_{0}},s)ds
, then

(4.3)

q_{1}(\widetilde{\theta}_{0},(0,\theta_{0}/2])\geq q_{0}\quad\text{and}\quad q_{1}(\widetilde{\theta}_{0},[\pi-\theta_{0}/2,\pi))\geq q_{0}\quad\text{for all}\quad\widetilde{\theta}_{0}\in[0,\pi].

Note that (
4.3
) implies that

(4.4)

\mathbf{P}[\widehat{\theta}|_{[0,2]}\ \text{hits both}\ (0,\theta_{0}]\ \text{and}\ [2\pi-\theta_{0},2\pi)]\geq q_{0}^{2}\quad\text{uniformly in}\quad\widehat{\theta}_{0}\in[0,2\pi].

Applying the Markov property
\tfrac{1}{2}(\log R+c_{1})-1
times for
\widehat{\theta}
implies that there exists
q>0
depending only on
\theta_{0}
so that
\mathbf{P}[E_{R}]=1-O(R^{-q})
. This proves (
4.2
).
∎

Next, we shall show that with high probability, the crossing of
A_{z,k+1}
is separated from the rest of the curve. We begin by proving that a regularity event is very likely to occur, provided that we choose the parameters correctly.

Lemma 4.8
.

Fix
p_{0}\in(0,1)
,
R\geq R_{0}
and
\epsilon>0
. There exist
A,C>0
large and
\delta\in(0,1)
such that the following holds. Define the events
F_{0}=\{\widetilde{\tau}_{R}\leq\widetilde{\sigma}_{1},\ \widetilde{\tau}_{R}\geq-A,\ |\widetilde{\eta}(s)-\widetilde{\eta}(t)|\leq C|s-t|^{1/2}\ \forall s,t\in[-A,A]\}
and
F_{1}=F_{0}\cap\{\mathrm{dist}(\widetilde{\eta}([-A,s]),\widetilde{\eta}([s+\epsilon,\infty)))\geq\delta\ \text{for each}\ s\in[-A,0]\}
. Then,

(4.5)

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}[F_{1}\,|\,\mathcal{G}_{\tau_{z,k}}]{\bf 1}_{B_{z,k}}\geq\left(1-\frac{p_{0}}{25}\right){\bf 1}_{B_{z,k}}.

Proof.

We assume that
\widetilde{\eta}
has the natural parameterization with time normalized so that
\widetilde{\eta}(0)=0
. Then
[
58
, Corollary 4.7]
, the independence of
\widetilde{\eta}
and
\eta
, and (
4.2
) imply that there exist constants
A,C>0
such that

\mathbf{P}[F_{0}\,|\,\mathcal{G}_{\tau_{z,k}}]{\bf 1}_{B_{z,k}}\geq\left(1-\frac{p_{0}}{50}\right){\bf 1}_{B_{z,k}}.

Moreover, since
\widetilde{\eta}
is a simple curve and
\widetilde{\eta}_{1},\widetilde{\eta}_{2}
are transient
[
36
]
, it follows that we can find
\delta\in(0,1)
such that (
4.5
) holds.
∎

Before stating the next lemma, we define the following random times. Let
\widetilde{\phi}_{R}
be the unique conformal map from
\mathbf{C}\setminus\widetilde{\eta}((-\infty,\widetilde{\tau}_{R}])
to
\mathbf{H}_{\tau_{z,k+1}^{*}}
with
\widetilde{\phi}_{R}(0)=z
and
\widetilde{\phi}_{R}(\widetilde{\eta}(\widetilde{\tau}_{R}))=\eta(\tau_{z,k+1}^{*})
and for a small parameter
\xi\in(0,1)
, define

\displaystyle\widetilde{\tau}_{z,k+1}

\displaystyle=\inf\{t\in\mathbf{R}:\widetilde{\phi}_{R}(\widetilde{\eta}(t))\in\psi_{z,k+1}^{-1}(B(0,e^{-2}+\xi))\},

\displaystyle\widetilde{\sigma}_{z,k+1}

\displaystyle=\sup\{t\leq\widetilde{\tau}_{z,k+1}:\widetilde{\phi}_{R}(\widetilde{\eta}(t))\notin\psi_{z,k+1}^{-1}(B(0,e^{-1}-\xi))\},\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\widehat{\tau}_{z,k+1}

\displaystyle=\inf\{t\geq\widetilde{\tau}_{z,k+1}:|\widetilde{\phi}_{R}(\widetilde{\eta}(t))-\widetilde{\phi}_{R}(\widetilde{\eta}(\widetilde{\tau}_{z,k+1}))|\geq\xi\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,k+1})\}.

Moreover, we let
\widehat{I}=\widetilde{\phi}_{R}(\widetilde{\eta}([\widetilde{\sigma}_{z,k+1},\widetilde{\tau}_{z,k+1}]))
. It is from this segment that we will pick the segment
I
of the event
C_{z,k+1}
. The next lemma shows that on the event
F_{1}
, anything but the immediate future of the curve stays away from the set
\widehat{I}
.

Lemma 4.9
.

Consider the setup of the previous lemma. There exists
\epsilon,\zeta>0
small such that on
F_{1}
we have that
\mathrm{dist}(\widetilde{\phi}_{R}(\widetilde{\eta}([\widetilde{\sigma}_{z,k+1},\widetilde{\tau}_{z,k+1}])),\widetilde{\phi}_{R}(\widetilde{\eta}([\widehat{\tau}_{z,k+1},\infty))))\geq\zeta\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,k+1})
.

Proof.

Note that
\widetilde{\phi}_{R}(\widetilde{\eta}|_{[\widetilde{\tau}_{R},0]})
is a radial
{\rm SLE}_{4}(2)
in
\mathbf{H}_{\tau_{z,k+1}^{*}}
from
\eta(\tau_{z,k+1}^{*})
to
z
with the force point located at
\infty
. (Note that
\widetilde{\phi}_{R}=\psi_{z,k+1}^{-1}\circ\widetilde{\psi}_{\widetilde{\tau}_{R}}
.)

Suppose that we are working on
F_{0}
. We fix
\xi\in(0,1)
small (depending only on the implicit constants that we have considered so far). Note that
[
26
, Corollary 3.19 and Theorem 3.21]
imply that there exist universal constants
c_{1},c_{2}>0
and a constant
C_{R}>1
depending only on
R
such that
\psi^{-1}_{z,k+1}(B(0,e^{-1}+\xi))\subseteq B(z,c_{1}\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,k+1}))
,
B(z,c_{2}\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,k+1}))\subseteq\psi_{z,k+1}^{-1}(B(0,e^{-2}-2\xi))
when
\xi\in(0,1)
is sufficiently small and
C_{R}^{-1}\leq|(\widetilde{\phi}_{R}^{-1})^{\prime}(w)|\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,k+1})\leq C_{R}
for each
w\in A_{z,k+1}
. Combining this with
[
26
, Theorem 3.21]
we obtain that if
\xi>0
is sufficiently small, then we have that
|\widehat{\tau}_{z,k+1}-\widetilde{\tau}_{z,k+1}|\geq(\xi/2CC_{R})^{2}
. Choosing
\epsilon>0
sufficiently small, it follows that
\widetilde{\phi}_{R}(\widetilde{\eta})([\widehat{\tau}_{z,k+1},\infty))\subseteq\widetilde{\phi}_{R}(\widetilde{\eta}([\widetilde{\tau}_{z,k+1}+\epsilon,\infty)))
and
\widetilde{\phi}_{R}(\widetilde{\eta}([\widetilde{\tau}_{R},\widetilde{\tau}_{z,k+1}]))\subseteq\widetilde{\phi}_{R}(\widetilde{\eta}([-A,0]))
. Thus, assuming that we are on
F_{1}
, it follows that
\mathrm{dist}(\widetilde{\phi}_{R}(\widetilde{\eta}([\widetilde{\sigma}_{z,k+1},\widetilde{\tau}_{z,k+1}])),\widetilde{\phi}_{R}(\widetilde{\eta}([\widehat{\tau}_{z,k+1},\infty))))\geq\zeta\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,k+1})
, proving the lemma.
∎

We now turn our attention to the construction of
I
and the two points
z_{\mathrm{L}},z_{\mathrm{R}}
which are close to
I
. We fix
\widetilde{\delta}_{1}\in(0,1)
small and
\xi_{2}\in(0,(e^{-b}-e^{-2})/2)
(both to be chosen) such that
2\pi/\widetilde{\delta}_{1}\in\mathbf{N}
. We divide
\partial B(0,e^{-b}-\xi_{2})
into open and pairwise disjoint arcs
J_{1},\ldots,J_{n(\widetilde{\delta}_{1})}
of length
\widetilde{\delta}_{1}
such that
\partial B(0,e^{-b}-\xi_{2})=\cup_{m=1}^{n(\widetilde{\delta}_{1})}\mathrm{cl}(J_{m})
. Let
J
be the arc in
\{J_{1},\ldots,J_{n(\widetilde{\delta}_{1})}\}
so that its image under
\psi_{z,k+1}^{-1}
is last hit by
\widehat{I}
. Let also
z_{\mathrm{L}}
(resp.
z_{\mathrm{R}}
) be the two endpoints of
\psi_{z,k+1}^{-1}(J)
(where naturally
z_{\mathrm{R}}
is the image of the first point of
J
if it is ordered clockwise). Moreover, we let
w_{0}
be the last point (chronologically) on
\psi_{z,k+1}^{-1}(J)
that
\widehat{I}
hits and let
w_{1}
be the first point on
\partial B(w_{0},\delta_{1}\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,k+1}))
that
\widehat{I}
hits after it hits
w_{0}
. Finally, we let
t_{z,k+1}^{j}
,
j=0,1
be such that
\widetilde{\phi}_{R}(\widetilde{\eta}(t_{z,k+1}^{j}))=w_{j}
and define
I=\widetilde{\phi}_{R}(\widetilde{\eta}([t_{z,k+1}^{0},t_{z,k+1}^{1}]))
.

In the following lemma we provide the distance-diameter ratio bounds of (
i
).

Lemma 4.10
.

Fix
p_{0}\in(0,1)
,
\delta_{1}\in(0,1)
and the parameters
R,A,C,\delta,\epsilon
as above. Then for all
\widetilde{\delta}_{1},\delta_{2}\in(0,1)
small enough we have that if
F_{2}=F_{1}\cap\{\mathrm{dist}(\{z_{\mathrm{L}},z_{\mathrm{R}}\},\widetilde{\phi}_{R}(\widetilde{\eta}))\geq\delta_{2}\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,k+1})\ \cap\{\mathrm{dist}(\{z_{\mathrm{L}},z_{\mathrm{R}}\},I)\leq\delta_{1}\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,k+1})\}
, then

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}[F_{2}\,|\,\mathcal{G}_{\tau_{z,k}}]{\bf 1}_{B_{z,k}}\geq(1-p_{0}/20){\bf 1}_{B_{z,k}}.

Proof.

By construction we have that
\mathrm{dist}(z_{\mathrm{L}},I^{\mathrm{L}})\lesssim\widetilde{\delta}_{1}\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,k+1})
and
\mathrm{dist}(z_{\mathrm{R}},I^{\mathrm{R}})\lesssim\widetilde{\delta}_{1}\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,k+1})
with universal implicit constant, where
I^{\mathrm{L}}
and
I^{\mathrm{R}}
denote the left and right side of
I
, respectively. Thus, if we fix
\delta_{1}\in(0,1)
then we can pick
\widetilde{\delta}_{1}\in(0,1)
to be sufficiently small so that
\mathrm{dist}(z_{\mathrm{L}},I^{\mathrm{L}})\leq\delta_{1}\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,k+1})
and
\mathrm{dist}(z_{\mathrm{R}},I^{\mathrm{R}})\leq\delta_{1}\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,k+1})
.

Next, noting that an argument similar to that of the proof of Lemma
4.9
implies that we can choose
\delta_{1}>0
so small that
\mathrm{dist}(\psi_{z,k+1}^{-1}(J),\widetilde{\phi}_{R}(\widetilde{\eta}([\widetilde{\tau}_{z,k+1},\infty))))\geq\delta_{1}\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,k+1})
. From now on, we assume that
\delta_{1}
is taken to be that small. We next claim that we can choose
\delta_{2}>0
sufficiently small so that it is likely that we also have that
\mathrm{dist}(\{z_{\mathrm{L}},z_{\mathrm{R}}\},\widetilde{\phi}_{R}(\widetilde{\eta}([\widetilde{\tau}_{R},\widetilde{\tau}_{z,k+1}])))\geq\delta_{2}\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,k+1})
. Indeed, let
x_{m-1}
and
x_{m}
be the endpoints of
\psi_{z,k+1}^{-1}(J_{m})
in clockwise order for
m=1,\ldots,n(\widetilde{\delta}_{1})
, where
x_{0}=z_{\mathrm{L}}
and
x_{n(\widetilde{\delta}_{1})}=z_{\mathrm{R}}
. Then Lemma
4.6
applied to
\psi_{z,k+1}\circ\widetilde{\phi}_{R}(\widetilde{\eta}_{1}|_{[\widetilde{\tau}_{R},0]})
(note
\psi_{z,k+1}\circ\widetilde{\phi}_{R}=\widetilde{\psi}_{\widetilde{\tau}_{R}}
) implies that we can find
\delta_{2}\in(0,1)
sufficiently small such that conditional on
\mathcal{G}_{\tau_{z,k}}
and on the event
B_{z,k}
we have that
\widetilde{\phi}_{R}(\widetilde{\eta}([\widetilde{\tau}_{R},0]))\cap B(x_{m},\delta_{2}\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,k+1}))=\emptyset
for each
m=0,\ldots,n(\widetilde{\delta}_{1})
with probability at least
1-p_{0}/100
. In particular, we have that
\mathrm{dist}(\{z_{\mathrm{L}},z_{\mathrm{R}}\},\widetilde{\phi}_{R}(\widetilde{\eta}))\geq\delta_{2}\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,k+1})
. Combining this with Lemma
4.8
gives the result.
∎

The next lemma states that
I
is likely to satisfy conditions (
ii
) and (
iii
).

Lemma 4.11
.

Fix
a,p_{0}\in(0,1)
and let
R,A,C,\delta,\epsilon,\delta_{1},\delta_{2}
be as above. There exists
M_{0}^{*}\geq 1
such that for all
M_{0}\geq M_{0}^{*}
, the following holds. Define the event

\displaystyle F_{3}=F_{2}\cap\{\mu_{\widetilde{\phi}_{R}(\widetilde{\eta})}^{\mathrm{nat}}(I)\geq M_{0}^{-1}\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,k+1})^{3/2}\}\cap\{\mu_{\widetilde{\phi}_{R}(\widetilde{\eta})}^{\mathrm{nat}}(Y\cap I)\leq M_{0}\mathrm{diam}(Y)^{3/2-a},\forall Y\ \text{Borel}\}.

Then,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}[F_{3}\,|\,\mathcal{G}_{\tau_{z,k}}]{\bf 1}_{B_{z,k}}\geq(1-p_{0}/10){\bf 1}_{B_{z,k}}.

Proof.

By an argument to the one used in Lemma
4.9
and noting that
\mu_{\widetilde{\phi}_{R}(\widetilde{\eta})}^{\mathrm{nat}}(dw)=|\widetilde{\phi}_{R}^{\prime}(\widetilde{w})|^{3/2}\mu_{\widetilde{\eta}}^{\mathrm{nat}}(d\widetilde{w})
, we obtain that the conditional probability of
F_{1}
and the event that

(4.6)

\mu_{\widetilde{\phi}_{R}(\widetilde{\eta})}^{\mathrm{nat}}(I)\gtrsim\delta_{1}^{2}\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,k+1})^{3/2}

given
\mathcal{G}_{\tau_{z,k}}
and on
B_{z,k}
is at least
1-p_{0}/24
, where the implicit constant depends only on
C
and
C_{R}
(recall the proof of Lemma
4.9
). Thus, choosing
M_{0}
large enough so that
M_{0}^{-1}
is at most
\delta_{1}^{2}
times the implicit constant we need only care about the event about the upper bound.

Moreover, by arguing as in the proof of Lemma
4.4
and using Lemma
3.2
, we have (by possibly increasing the value of
M_{0}
) that on
F_{2}\cap\{\mu_{\widetilde{\phi}_{R}(\widetilde{\eta})}^{\mathrm{nat}}(I)\geq M_{0}^{-1}\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,k+1})^{3/2}\}
, the inequality
\mu_{\widetilde{\phi}_{R}(\widetilde{\eta})}^{\mathrm{nat}}(Y\cap I)\leq M_{0}\mathrm{diam}(Y)^{3/2-a}
holds for all Borel sets
Y
. Thus the result follows.
∎

For the next lemma, we consider the following. Let
\widetilde{\mathbf{H}}_{\mathrm{L}}
(resp.
\widetilde{\mathbf{H}}_{\mathrm{R}}
) be the connected component of
\mathbf{H}\setminus(\eta([0,\tau_{z,k+1}^{*}])\cup\widetilde{\phi}_{R}(\widetilde{\eta}))
to the left (resp. right) of
\eta([0,\tau_{z,k+1}^{*}])\cup\widetilde{\phi}_{R}(\widetilde{\eta})
and let
\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{q}
be a Whitney square decomposition of
\widetilde{\mathbf{H}}_{q}
,
q\in\{\mathrm{L},\mathrm{R}\}
, chosen in some measurable way.

Lemma 4.12
.

Fix
a,p_{0}\in(0,1)
and let
R,A,C,\delta,\epsilon,\delta_{1},\delta_{2}
be as above. There exists
M_{0}>0
such that the following holds for all
M\geq M_{0}
. Let
F_{4}
be the intersection of
F_{3}
and the event that there exists a set
I_{0}\subset I
such that
\mu_{\widetilde{\phi}_{R}(\widetilde{\eta})}^{\mathrm{nat}}(I\setminus I_{0})\leq\mu_{\widetilde{\phi}_{R}(\widetilde{\eta})}^{\mathrm{nat}}(I)/100
such that for
q\in\{\mathrm{L},\mathrm{R}\}
and for each
w\in I_{0}
and each
\widetilde{Q}
which is intersected by
\gamma_{z_{q},w}^{\widetilde{\mathbf{H}}_{q}}
(recall Section
2.1
), the following holds

(4.7)

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\widetilde{\mathbf{H}}_{q}}(z_{q},\mathrm{cen}(\widetilde{Q}))\leq M({\mathrm{len}}(\widetilde{Q})/\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,k+1}))^{-a}.

Then

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}[F_{4}\,|\,\mathcal{G}_{\tau_{z,k}}]{\bf 1}_{B_{z,k}}\geq(1-p_{0}/8){\bf 1}_{B_{z,k}}.

Proof.

Assume that we are working on
F_{3}
. We can find
r>0
large (depending only on
R
) and by decreasing the value of
\delta_{2}\in(0,1)
if necessary we have that
\widetilde{\phi}_{R}^{-1}(B(z,c_{1}\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,k+1})))\subseteq B(0,r)
and
\mathrm{dist}(\widetilde{\phi}_{R}^{-1}(z_{\mathrm{L}}),\widetilde{\eta})\geq C_{R}^{-1}\delta_{2}
,
\mathrm{dist}(\widetilde{\phi}_{R}^{-1}(z_{\mathrm{R}}),\widetilde{\eta})\geq C_{R}^{-1}\delta_{2}
(where
c_{1}
and
C_{R}
are as in the proof of Lemma
4.9
).

We let
\mathbf{C}_{\mathrm{L}}
(resp.
\mathbf{C}_{\mathrm{R}}
) denote the connected of
\mathbf{C}\setminus\widetilde{\eta}
which is to the left (resp. right) of
\widetilde{\eta}
and let
\mathcal{W}_{q}
,
q\in\{\mathrm{L},\mathrm{R}\}
, be a Whitney square decomposition of
\mathbf{C}_{q}
chosen in some fixed measurable way. We fix some
\mathfrak{f}\in(0,1)
(to be determined later) and
p_{1}\in(0,1)
and note that the proof of Lemma
3.1
combined with the translation invariance property of
\widetilde{\eta}
when parameterized according to the natural parameterization implies that there exists
M>0
such that conditional on
\mathcal{G}_{\tau_{z,k}}
and on the event
B_{z,k}
, with probability at least
1-p_{1}
the following holds for sufficiently small
\delta_{2}
. Fix a point
z^{\prime}
in
B(0,r)\setminus B(0,r_{0})
for some
r_{0}\in(0,r)
. Then
\mathrm{dist}(z^{\prime},\widetilde{\eta})\geq C_{R}^{-1}\delta_{2}
, and if we let
q\in\{\mathrm{L},\mathrm{R}\}
be such that
z^{\prime}\in\mathbf{C}_{q}
and
X
be the set of
t\in[-A,0]
such that

(4.8)

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\mathbf{C}_{q}}(\mathrm{cen}(Q),z^{\prime})\leq M{\mathrm{len}}(Q)^{-a},

for each
Q\in\mathcal{W}_{q}
which is intersected by the hyperbolic geodesic in
\mathbf{C}_{q}
from
z^{\prime}
to
\widetilde{\eta}(t)
. Then
\mathcal{L}_{1}(X)\geq(1-\mathfrak{f})A
. Thus, picking
p_{1}>0
extremely small,
M>0
appropriately large, considering a fine grid of points
(z_{j})
in
B(0,r)
and taking a union bound, it holds that conditional on
\mathcal{G}_{\tau_{z,k}}
and on the event
B_{z,k}
, we have that with probability at least
1-p_{0}/100
, we can find points
z_{\mathrm{L}}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{C}_{\mathrm{L}}\cap B(0,r)
and
z_{\mathrm{R}}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{C}_{\mathrm{R}}\cap B(0,r)
such that
\mathrm{dist}(\{z_{\mathrm{L}}^{\prime},z_{\mathrm{R}}^{\prime}\},\widetilde{\eta})\geq C_{R}^{-1}\delta_{2}
, so that if
X_{q}
is the set of
t\in[-A,0]
such that for each
Q\in\mathcal{W}_{q}
intersected by
\gamma_{z_{q}^{\prime},\widetilde{\eta}(t)}^{\mathbf{C}_{q}}
, (
4.8
) holds with
z^{\prime}=z_{q}^{\prime}
, then
\mathcal{L}_{1}(X_{q})\geq(1-\mathfrak{f})A
.

Thus, letting
X=X_{\mathrm{L}}\cap X_{\mathrm{R}}
, we may choose
\mathfrak{f}\in(0,1)
so that
\mu_{\widetilde{\phi}_{R}(\widetilde{\eta})}^{\mathrm{nat}}(I\cap\widetilde{\phi}_{R}(\widetilde{\eta}([-A,0]\setminus X)))\leq\mu_{\widetilde{\phi}_{R}(\widetilde{\eta})}^{\mathrm{nat}}(I)/100
and applying Lemma
A.6
, we have that (
4.8
) holds with
z^{\prime}=z_{\mathrm{L}}
and
z^{\prime}=z_{\mathrm{R}}
by possibly increasing
M
by a universal constant. Hence we are done if we can show that (
4.7
) follows from (
4.8
), so that we can set
I_{0}=I\cap\widetilde{\phi}_{R}(\widetilde{\eta}(X))
.

Suppose that the intersection of
F_{3}
and the above event occurs (the conditional probability of this given
\mathcal{G}_{z,k}
and the event
B_{z,k}
is at least
1-p_{0}/8
). Note that
z_{\mathrm{R}}\in\widetilde{\mathbf{H}}_{\mathrm{R}}
since in order for
z_{\mathrm{R}}
to lie in
\widetilde{\mathbf{H}}_{\mathrm{L}}
, the curve
\widetilde{\phi}_{R}(\widetilde{\eta})([\widetilde{\tau}_{z,k+1},\infty))
must separate
z_{\mathrm{R}}
from the right side of
\widehat{I}
and the latter can only happen when
\widetilde{\phi}_{R}(\widetilde{\eta})([\widetilde{\tau}_{z,k+1},\infty))
comes within distance
\delta_{1}\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,k+1})
of
z_{\mathrm{R}}
since
\mathrm{dist}(z_{\mathrm{R}},\widehat{I})\leq\delta_{1}\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,k+1})
but the choice of
\delta_{1}
implies that this cannot happen. Similarly, we have that
z_{\mathrm{L}}\in\widetilde{\mathbf{H}}_{\mathrm{L}}
. Fix
\widetilde{w}_{0}\in I\cap\widetilde{\phi}_{R}(\widetilde{\eta}(X))
and let
\widetilde{Q}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{R}}
,
\widetilde{z}\in\widetilde{Q}
be such that
\gamma_{z_{\mathrm{R}},\widetilde{w}_{0}}^{\widetilde{\mathbf{H}}_{\mathrm{R}}}(s)=\widetilde{z}
for some
s\geq 0
. Note that
\mathrm{diam}(\gamma_{z_{\mathrm{R}},\widetilde{w}_{0}}^{\widetilde{\mathbf{H}}_{\mathrm{R}}})\leq c_{1}\delta_{1}\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,k+1})
for some universal constant
c_{1}>0
and so by taking
\delta_{1}>0
sufficiently small we have that
\gamma_{z_{\mathrm{R}},\widetilde{w}_{0}}^{\widetilde{\mathbf{H}}_{\mathrm{R}}}\subseteq A_{z,k+1}
. This implies that
{\mathrm{len}}(\widetilde{Q})\leq\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,k+1})
and so we can assume that
\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\widetilde{\mathbf{H}}_{\mathrm{R}}}(z_{\mathrm{R}},\widetilde{Q})\geq 1
. Let
Q\in\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{R}}
be such that
\widetilde{w}=\widetilde{\phi}_{R}^{-1}(\widetilde{z})\in Q
. Then we have that
\widetilde{w}\in\gamma_{\widetilde{\phi}_{R}^{-1}(z_{\mathrm{R}}),w_{0}}^{\mathbf{C}_{\mathrm{R}}}
where
w_{0}=\widetilde{\phi}_{R}^{-1}(\widetilde{w}_{0})\in\widetilde{\eta}(X)
and so
\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\mathbf{C}_{\mathrm{R}}}(\widetilde{\phi}_{R}^{-1}(z_{\mathrm{R}}),\mathrm{cen}(Q))\leq M{\mathrm{len}}(Q)^{-a}
. This implies that

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\widetilde{\mathbf{H}}_{\mathrm{R}}}(z_{\mathrm{R}},\widetilde{Q})

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\widetilde{\mathbf{H}}_{\mathrm{R}}}(z_{\mathrm{R}},\widetilde{z})=\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\mathbf{C}_{\mathrm{R}}}(\widetilde{\phi}_{R}^{-1}(z_{\mathrm{R}}),\widetilde{w})

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\mathbf{C}_{\mathrm{R}}}(\widetilde{\phi}_{R}^{-1}(z_{\mathrm{R}}),\mathrm{cen}(Q))+1\leq M{\mathrm{len}}(Q)^{-a}+1

since
\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\mathbf{C}_{\mathrm{R}}}(\widetilde{x},\widetilde{y})\leq 1
for each
\widetilde{x},\widetilde{y}\in Q
. It follows that
\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\widetilde{\mathbf{H}}_{\mathrm{R}}}(z_{\mathrm{R}},\mathrm{cen}(\widetilde{Q}))\lesssim{\mathrm{len}}(Q)^{-a}
. Moreover (
2.6
) implies that
{\mathrm{len}}(\widetilde{Q})/{\mathrm{len}}(Q)\asymp\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,k+1})
. It follows that

\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\widetilde{\mathbf{H}}_{\mathrm{R}}}(z_{\mathrm{R}},\mathrm{cen}(\widetilde{Q}))\lesssim({\mathrm{len}}(\widetilde{Q})/\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,k+1}))^{-a}

where the implicit constant depends only on the previously chosen constants. Analogously, the same holds with
z_{\mathrm{L}}
and
\widetilde{\mathbf{H}}_{\mathrm{L}}
in place of
z_{\mathrm{R}}
and
\widetilde{\mathbf{H}}_{\mathrm{R}}
and thus the proof is done.
∎

The final ingredient in the proof of Lemma
4.5
will be the following, which transfers the result of Lemma
4.12
to domains
W
as in (
i
).

Lemma 4.13
.

Fix
a,p_{0}\in(0,1)
and let
R,A,C,\delta,\epsilon,\delta_{1},\delta_{2}
be as above. There exists
M_{0}>0
such that if
F_{5}
is the intersection of
F_{4}
and the event that (
4.7
) holds with any
W
as in (
i
) in place of
\widetilde{\mathbf{H}}_{q}
. Then,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}[F_{5}\,|\,\mathcal{G}_{\tau_{z,k}}]{\bf 1}_{B_{z,k}}\geq(1-p_{0}/5){\bf 1}_{B_{z,k}}.

Proof.

The entirety of this proof is dedicated to showing that with sufficiently high probability, we are in the setting to use Lemma
A.6
.
We fix a simply connected domain
W\subseteq A^{*}_{z,k+1}
as in condition (
i
) in the case of
z_{\mathrm{R}}
and suppose that
\mathcal{W}
is a Whitney square decomposition of
W
. We will now apply Lemma
A.6
in order to argue that after possibly increasing the value of
M
(independently of
W
), the hyperbolic geodesics in
W
from
z_{\mathrm{R}}
to
I
intersect only squares
Q
satisfying
\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{W}(\mathrm{cen}(Q),z_{\mathrm{R}})\leq M({\mathrm{len}}(Q)/\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,k+1}))^{-a}
. First, we introduce one more event for
\widetilde{\eta}
. We let
\mathcal{S}
be the set of squares whose top left corner is in
(\delta_{1}^{1/2}/4)\mathbf{Z}^{2}
, have side length equal to
\delta_{1}^{1/2}
and are contained in
B(0,r)
. Note that
\mathcal{S}=\{S_{1},\ldots,S_{m}\}
where
m
depends only on
r
and
\delta_{1}
. For
1\leq j\leq m
we let
U^{j}_{1},\ldots,U^{j}_{k_{j}}
be the connected components of
S_{j}\setminus\widetilde{\eta}
which contain a point whose distance to
\partial S_{j}\setminus\widetilde{\eta}
is at least
C_{R}^{-1}\delta_{2}
and their boundaries also contain an arc of
\widetilde{\eta}
of diameter
C_{R}^{-1}\delta_{2}
. Note that for each
1\leq j\leq m
,
k_{j}
is almost surely bounded by a constant depending only on
R,\delta_{1},\delta_{2}
. For
p\in(0,1)
fixed, we let
\widetilde{F}_{5}
be the event that
F_{4}
occurs and the following holds. For each
1\leq j\leq m
and
1\leq i\leq k_{j}
, there is a point in
U_{i}^{j}
from which the harmonic measure in
U_{i}^{j}
of any arc of
\widetilde{\eta}
contained in
\partial U^{j}_{i}
of diameter at least
C_{R}^{-1}\delta_{2}
, is at least
p
. We claim that for
p\in(0,1)
sufficiently small, we have that
\mathbf{P}[\widetilde{F}_{5}\,|\,\mathcal{G}_{\tau_{z,k}}]{\bf 1}_{B_{z,k}}\geq(1-p_{0}/7){\bf 1}_{B_{z,k}}
. Indeed, we consider conformal transformations
\psi^{j}_{i}\colon U_{i}^{j}\to\mathbf{D}
for each
i,j
chosen in some fixed measurable way. Then the claim follows by combining the fact that the
\psi^{j}_{i}
extend to homeomorphisms mapping
\mathrm{cl}(U^{j}_{i})
onto
\mathrm{cl}(\mathbf{D})
with the explicit form of the Poisson kernel in
\mathbf{D}
. Next we let
1\leq j\leq m
be such that
\widetilde{\phi}_{R}^{-1}(z_{\mathrm{R}})\in S_{j}
and
\mathrm{dist}(\widetilde{\phi}_{R}^{-1}(z_{\mathrm{R}}),\partial S_{j})\geq\delta_{1}^{1/2}/4
. Let
\widetilde{U}
be the connected component of
S_{j}\setminus\widetilde{\eta}
containing
\widetilde{\phi}_{R}^{-1}(z_{\mathrm{R}})
. Note that
\mathrm{dist}(\widetilde{\phi}_{R}^{-1}(z_{\mathrm{R}}),\widetilde{\eta})\geq C_{R}^{-1}\delta_{2}
and so
\mathrm{dist}(\widetilde{\phi}_{R}^{-1}(z_{\mathrm{R}}),\partial\widetilde{U})\geq C_{R}^{-1}\delta_{2}
for
\delta_{2}>0
sufficiently small. We set
\widetilde{J}=\partial\widetilde{U}\setminus\widetilde{\eta}
and
\widehat{J}=\widetilde{\phi}_{R}(\widetilde{J})
. For
\delta_{1}>0
sufficiently small, we have that
\widehat{J}\subseteq A^{*}_{z,k+1}
,
\mathrm{dist}(z_{\mathrm{R}},\widehat{J})\asymp\delta_{1}^{1/2}\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,k+1})
,
I\subseteq\widetilde{\phi}_{R}(\widetilde{\eta}\cap\partial\widetilde{U})
and that
\widehat{J}
separates
z_{\mathrm{R}}
from
\infty
. By possibly taking
\delta_{2}
to be smaller, we have that for every arc
K\subseteq\widehat{I}
such that
\mathrm{diam}(K)\geq\delta_{2}\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,k+1})
, it holds that
\mathrm{diam}(\widetilde{\phi}_{R}^{-1}(K))\geq C_{R}^{-1}\delta_{2}
. We fix
w\in I
such that
\gamma_{z_{\mathrm{R}},w}^{\widetilde{\mathbf{H}}_{\mathrm{R}}}
intersects only squares
Q
satisfying
\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\widetilde{\mathbf{H}}_{\mathrm{R}}}(\mathrm{cen}(Q),z_{\mathrm{R}})\leq M({\mathrm{len}}(Q)/\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,k+1}))^{-a}
and we let
\phi_{w}\colon\mathbf{H}_{\tau_{z,k+1}}\to\mathbf{H}
be the conformal transformation such that
\phi_{w}(w)=0
and
\phi_{w}(z_{\mathrm{R}})=i
. We claim that for
c\in(0,1)
sufficiently small, we have that
B(0,c)\cap\phi_{w}(\widehat{J})=\emptyset
. Indeed, suppose that
B(0,c)\cap\phi_{w}(\widehat{J})\neq\emptyset
and let
\widetilde{x}
and
\widetilde{y}
be the endpoints of
\phi_{w}(\widehat{J})
, assume by symmetry that
\widetilde{y}
is the smallest positive of the two (so that either
\widetilde{x}<0<\widetilde{y}
or
0<\widetilde{y}<\widetilde{x}
) and let
\widetilde{X}=[-\widetilde{x},0]
if
\widetilde{x}<0
and
\widetilde{X}=(-\infty,0]\cup[\widetilde{x},\infty)
if
\widetilde{x}>0
. Let also
\widetilde{W}
be the connected component of
\mathbf{H}\setminus\phi_{w}(\widehat{J})
which contains
i
. Since
\widetilde{F}_{5}
occurs and the clockwise arc of
\partial\widetilde{\phi}_{R}(\widetilde{U})
connecting
\widetilde{\phi}_{R}(\partial\widetilde{U}\setminus\widetilde{J})
to
w
has diameter at least
\delta_{1}\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,k+1})
, it follows that the probability that a Brownian motion starting from
i
exits
\widetilde{W}
in
[0,\widetilde{y}]
is at least
p
and the same holds for
\widetilde{X}
. Furthermore, since
B(0,c)\cap\phi_{w}(\widehat{J})\neq\emptyset
, it follows that either
\widetilde{X}
or
[0,\widetilde{y}]
is separated from
i
by
\phi_{w}(\widetilde{J})\cup\partial B(0,c)
. Consequently, in order for a Brownian motion from
i
to exit
W
in that particular set, it has to first intersect
B(0,c)
without exiting
\mathbf{H}
. The probability of this, however, tends to
0
as
c\to 0
, which contradicts the fact that the probability of exiting in said set is at least
p
. Moreover, we have that
\mathrm{dist}(\phi^{-1}_{w}(ic/4),\eta)\gtrsim\delta_{2}\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,k+1})
where the implicit constant depends only on
c
and
\delta_{1}
and so we can find
\zeta>0
sufficiently small (depending only on
c
and
\delta_{1}
) such that
\mathrm{dist}(\widetilde{z},\widetilde{\eta})\geq\zeta
, where
\widetilde{z}=\widetilde{\phi}^{-1}_{R}(\phi^{-1}_{w}(ic/4))
. By possibly taking
\zeta>0
to be smaller, we have that
\mathrm{dist}(\widetilde{z},\partial\widetilde{U})\geq\zeta
.

Now we let
\widetilde{U}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{U}_{n}
be the connected components of
S_{k}\setminus\widetilde{\eta}
for some
1\leq k\leq m
which contain some point whose distance from
\partial S_{k}\setminus\widetilde{\eta}
is at least
\zeta
. Note that
n<\infty
a.s. Let
\widetilde{F}_{6}
be the event that
\widetilde{F}_{5}
occurs and the following holds. For each
1\leq i\leq n
and each
x_{1},x_{2}\in\widetilde{U}_{i}
such that
\mathrm{dist}(\{x_{1},x_{2}\},\partial\widetilde{U}_{i})\geq\zeta
, we have that
\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\widetilde{U}_{i}}(x_{1},x_{2})\leq C
. Arguing as before, we can choose
C>0
(depending only on the above implicit constants) such that
\mathbf{P}[\widetilde{F}_{6}\,|\,\mathcal{G}_{\tau_{z,k}}]{\bf 1}_{B_{z,k}}\geq(1-p_{0}/5){\bf 1}_{B_{z,k}}
. Suppose that we are working on
\widetilde{F}_{6}
. Then the conformal invariance of the hyperbolic metric implies that
\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\widetilde{W}}(i,ic/4)\leq C
. Consider the conformal automorphism of
\mathbf{H}
given by
\widetilde{\psi}(w)=2w/c
. Then
\mathrm{inrad}(\widetilde{\psi}(\widetilde{W}))\geq 2
and so Lemma
A.6
implies that there exists a constant
\widetilde{C}>0
depending only on
C
such that
\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\phi_{w}(\widetilde{\mathbf{H}}_{\mathrm{R}})}(\gamma_{i,0}^{\phi_{w}(\widetilde{\mathbf{H}}_{\mathrm{R}})}(t),\gamma_{ic/4,0}^{\mathbf{H}}(t))\leq\widetilde{C}
for each
t\geq 0
. Since
\widetilde{\mathbf{H}}_{\mathrm{R}}\subseteq\mathbf{H}_{\tau_{z,k+1}}
, it follows that
\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\mathbf{H}_{\tau_{z,k+1}}}(\gamma_{z_{\mathrm{R}},w}^{\widetilde{\mathbf{H}}_{\mathrm{R}}}(t),\gamma^{\mathbf{H}_{\tau_{z,k+1}}}_{\phi^{-1}_{w}(ic/4),w}(t))\leq\widetilde{C}
for each
t\geq 0
. Let
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}
be a Whitney square decomposition of
\mathbf{H}_{\tau_{z,k+1}}
and fix
\widehat{Q}\in\widehat{\mathcal{W}}
such that
\gamma_{\phi^{-1}_{w}(ic/4),w}^{\mathbf{H}_{\tau_{z,k+1}}}(t)\in\widehat{Q}
for some
t\geq 0
. Note that
\mathrm{dist}(\phi^{-1}_{w}(ic/4),I)\lesssim\delta_{1}^{1/2}\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,k+1})
and so for
\delta_{1}
small enough we obtain that
\mathrm{dist}(\widehat{Q},\partial\mathbf{H}_{\tau_{z,k+1}})=\mathrm{dist}(\widehat{Q},\partial\widetilde{\mathbf{H}}_{\mathrm{R}})
. Let
\widetilde{Q}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{R}
be such that
\gamma_{z_{\mathrm{R}},w}^{\widetilde{\mathbf{H}}_{\mathrm{R}}}(t)\in\widetilde{Q}
. Then we have that
\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\mathbf{H}_{\tau_{z,k+1}}}(\widehat{Q},\widetilde{Q})\leq\widetilde{C}
and so
{\mathrm{len}}(\widehat{Q})\asymp{\mathrm{len}}(\widetilde{Q})
, where the implicit constants depend only on
\widetilde{C}
. Also,
{\mathrm{len}}(\widehat{Q})\leq\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,k+1})
. Therefore, arguing as the proof of Lemma
4.12
, we obtain that
\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\mathbf{H}_{\tau_{z,k+1}}}(\phi_{w}^{-1}(ic/4),w)\lesssim M({\mathrm{len}}(\widehat{Q})/\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,k+1}))^{-a}
. Note that
\widetilde{W}\subseteq\phi_{w}(W)
and so
\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\phi_{w}(W)}(i,ic/4)\leq C
. Hence arguing as above by applying Lemma
A.6
with
D=\mathbf{H}_{\tau_{z,k+1}},\widetilde{D}=W,z_{0}=z_{\mathrm{R}}
and
w_{0}=\phi_{w}^{-1}(ic/4)
, we obtain that
\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{W}(z_{\mathrm{R}},\mathrm{cen}(Q))\lesssim M({\mathrm{len}}(Q)/\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,k+1}))^{-a}
for each
Q\in\mathcal{W}_{z,k+1}
such that
\gamma_{z_{\mathrm{R}},w}^{W}\cap Q\neq\emptyset
.
∎

We now conclude this section with the proof of Lemma
4.5
. This will be done by comparing the laws of radial and chordal
{\rm SLE}
.

Proof of Lemma
4.5
.

Let us now compare the laws of radial
{\rm SLE}_{4}(2)
and chordal
{\rm SLE}_{4}
. Let
\delta_{0},\delta_{1},\delta_{2},p_{0},a\in(0,1)
,
b\in(1,2)
, and
\xi_{2}\in(0,(e^{-b}-e^{2})/2)
be fixed and conditional on
\mathcal{G}_{\tau_{z,k}}
we let
\mathbf{P}_{z,k}^{*}
be the law of a radial
{\rm SLE}_{4}(2)
process
\widehat{\eta}
in
\mathbf{H}\setminus\eta([0,\tau_{z,k}])
from
\eta(\tau_{z,k})
to
z
with the force point located at
\infty
and stopped at the first time
\widehat{\tau}_{z,k+1}
that it hits
\partial^{\mathrm{in}}A_{z,k+1}
. Let also
\mathbf{E}_{z,k}^{*}
denote expectation with respect to
\mathbf{P}_{z,k}^{*}
. Then by Lemmas
4.8
-
4.13
, (replacing
p_{0}
with
\delta_{0}p_{0}
) implies that we can find
M>1
large and
\delta_{1}\in(0,1)
small such that
\mathbf{P}_{z,k}^{*}[C_{z,k+1}^{c}]{\bf 1}_{B_{z,k}}\leq p_{0}\delta_{0}{\bf 1}_{B_{z,k}}
. Let
M_{t}
be as in the proof of Lemma
4.2
and let
E_{z,k+1}
is the event that
\tau_{z,k+1}<\infty
,
\inf_{\tau_{z,k}\leq t\leq\tau_{z,k+1}}S_{t}\geq\delta_{0}
and
C_{z,k+1}^{c}
holds. As in the proof of Lemma
4.2
, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}[E_{z,k+1}\,\,|\,\mathcal{G}_{\tau_{z,k}}]{\bf 1}_{B_{z,k}}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\frac{M_{\tau_{z,k+1}}}{M_{\tau_{z,k}}}\right)^{-1}\left(\frac{M_{\tau_{z,k+1}}}{M_{\tau_{z,k}}}\right){\bf 1}_{E_{z,k+1}}\,\,|\,\mathcal{G}_{\tau_{z,k}}\right]{\bf 1}_{B_{z,k}}

\displaystyle=S_{\tau_{z,k}}\mathbf{E}_{z,k}^{*}\left[S_{\tau_{z,k+1}}^{-1}\left(\frac{\Upsilon_{\tau_{z,k+1}}}{\Upsilon_{\tau_{z,k}}}\right)^{1/2}{\bf 1}_{E_{z,k+1}}\right]{\bf 1}_{B_{z,k}}

\displaystyle\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\mathbf{P}_{z,k}^{*}[E_{z,k+1}]{\bf 1}_{B_{z,k}}\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\mathbf{P}_{z,k}^{*}[C_{z,k+1}^{c}]{\bf 1}_{B_{z,k}}\leq p_{0}{\bf 1}_{B_{z,k}},

which completes the proof.
∎

5.
Exploration of rectangle crossings

5.1.
Definition of the exploration

Figure 4.
Illustration of the first stage of the exploration in the case that
\eta_{1}
exits
\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
in
\partial^{\mathrm{D}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
. Note that the boundary data for
h
along the left side of
\eta_{1}
is
-\lambda+u
.
Left:
Shown in red is
\widetilde{\eta}_{1}
in the case that it exits
\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
in
\partial^{\mathrm{U}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
. Since
\widetilde{\eta}_{1}
is a level line of
-h+u
, the boundary data for
h
along its right side is
-\lambda+u
. Altogether, the exploration has discovered a crossing
L_{1}
so that the field boundary data on its right side (going from
\partial^{\mathrm{D}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
to
\partial^{\mathrm{U}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
) is
-\lambda+u
.
Right:
Shown in red is
\widetilde{\eta}_{1}
in the case that it exits
\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
in
\partial^{\mathrm{D}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
and the next level line in the exploration exits
\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
in
\partial^{\mathrm{U}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
. Altogether, the exploration has discovered a crossing
L_{1}
so that the field boundary data on its right side (going from
\partial^{\mathrm{D}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
to
\partial^{\mathrm{U}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
) is
\lambda+u
. In both cases, the starting point of the part of the exploration which will discover
L_{2}
is shown as a green dot.

Fix
H>0
and let
\mathcal{R}=(0,1)\times(0,H)
. Fix
0<a<b<H
and let
\mathcal{R}_{a,b}=(0,1)\times(a,b)
. We let
\partial^{\mathrm{D}}\mathcal{R}
,
\partial^{\mathrm{U}}\mathcal{R}
,
\partial^{\mathrm{L}}\mathcal{R}
and
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}
be the lower, upper, left and right boundary of
\mathcal{R}
, respectively. We use the same notation with
\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
in place of
\mathcal{R}
. Let
h
be a GFF in
\mathcal{R}
with boundary conditions given by
\lambda
on
\partial^{\mathrm{L}}\mathcal{R}
,
-\lambda
on
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}
, and
0
on
\partial^{\mathrm{U}}\mathcal{R}
and
\partial^{\mathrm{D}}\mathcal{R}
. We shall consider an exploration of level lines of
h
which form crossings of
\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
; see Figure
4
for an illustration. Fix
u>0
small and let
\eta_{1}
be the level line of
h
of height
u
(i.e., the level line of
h-u
) started from the midpoint
i(a+b)/2
of
\partial^{\mathrm{L}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
. Then, there are two possible outcomes:

(i)

\eta_{1}
exits
\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
in
\partial^{\mathrm{D}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
. If this occurs, then we start to explore the level line
\widetilde{\eta}_{1}
of
-h+u
from the point of
\partial^{\mathrm{L}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
which has the largest imaginary part among the points which have been visited by the exploration. If
\widetilde{\eta}_{1}
hits
\partial^{\mathrm{U}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
before
\partial^{\mathrm{D}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
or
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
, then the first stage of the exploration is concluded and we continue the exploration by iterating as described below. If
\widetilde{\eta}_{1}
hits
\partial^{\mathrm{D}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
before
\partial^{\mathrm{U}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
, then we explore the level line of height
u
from the point of
\partial^{\mathrm{L}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
which has the largest imaginary part among the points which have been visited by the exploration at this point in time. Repeat this step until the newly generated level line of height
u
hits
\partial^{\mathrm{U}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
before
\partial^{\mathrm{D}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
and
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
, which concludes the first stage of the exploration.

(ii)

\eta_{1}
exits
\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
in
\partial^{\mathrm{U}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
. If this occurs, then we start to explore the level line
\widetilde{\eta}_{1}
of
-h+u
from the point of
\partial^{\mathrm{L}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
which has the smallest imaginary part among the points which have been visited by the exploration. If
\widetilde{\eta}_{1}
hits
\partial^{\mathrm{D}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
before
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
and
\partial^{\mathrm{U}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
, then the first stage of the exploration is concluded and next we iterate the exploration as described below. If
\widetilde{\eta}_{1}
hits
\partial^{\mathrm{U}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
before
\partial^{\mathrm{D}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
and
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
, then we explore the level line of height
u
from the point of
\partial^{\mathrm{L}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
which has the smallest imaginary part among the points which have been visited by the exploration at this point in time. Repeat this step until the newly generated level line of height
u
hits
\partial^{\mathrm{D}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
before
\partial^{\mathrm{U}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
and
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
, concluding the first stage of the exploration.

Let
K_{1}
be the union of
\partial^{\mathrm{L}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
and the set discovered by the first stage of the exploration. The exploration a.s. does not discover a level line which hits
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
(as this is only possible if the height is in
(-2\lambda,0)
) so we let
U_{1}
be the component of
\mathcal{R}_{a,b}\setminus K_{1}
which has
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
as part of its boundary and we let
L_{1}=\mathrm{cl}(\partial U_{1}\setminus\partial\mathcal{R}_{a,b})
. Note that
L_{1}
consists of two level lines, one of
h-u
and one of
h+u
, from
\partial^{\mathrm{L}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
which exit
\partial^{\mathrm{U}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
and
\partial^{\mathrm{D}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
. This implies that the boundary conditions of
h
along
L_{1}
are either
-\lambda+u
or
\lambda+u
. We say that
L_{1}
is a crossing of
\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
of height
u
.

We inductively define successive stages of the exploration after
j
steps as follows, assuming that it has not yet discovered a level line which hits
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
(which is not possible in the first stage of the iteration, but may happen later, as we will explain). Let
K_{j}
be the union of
\partial^{\mathrm{L}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
and the exploration after
j
steps,
L_{j}
the
j
th crossing,
u_{j}
its height, and
U_{j}
the component of
\mathcal{R}_{a,b}\setminus K_{j}
with
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
on its boundary. For the iteration step, we make the following definition.

•

If the boundary conditions of
h
in
U_{j}
on
L_{j}
are equal to
\lambda+u_{j}
, then let
\eta_{j+1}
be the level line of
h
of height
u_{j+1}=u_{j}+u
starting from the leftmost intersection of
L_{j}
with the line
\{z:\mathrm{Im}(z)=(a+b)/2\}
.

•

If the boundary conditions of
h
in
U_{j}
on
L_{j}
are equal to
-\lambda+u_{j}
, then let
\eta_{j+1}
be the level line of
h
of height
u_{j+1}=u_{j}-u
starting from the leftmost intersection of
L_{j}
with the line
\{z:\mathrm{Im}(z)=(a+b)/2\}
.

We repeat the steps described above, replacing
\partial^{\mathrm{L}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
with
L_{j}
and
\eta_{1}
by
\eta_{j+1}
as follows. If
u_{j+1}\notin(-2\lambda,0)
, then
\eta_{j+1}
a.s. does not hit
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
, so we proceed as above. In the choice of where to start the next level line, one replaces the point of intersection with the largest (resp. smallest) imaginary part by last point with respect to the upward (resp. downward) exploration of
L_{j}
which was intersected by the previous level line. However, if
u_{j+1}\in(-2\lambda,0)
, then
\eta_{j+1}
, or any of the subsequent level lines in the exploration steps described above, may exit
\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
in
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
and if one of them does, then we stop the exploration at that point. From this exploration we obtain a sequence of crossings
(L_{j})
of
\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
with the property that
L_{j}\cap L_{j+1}\neq\emptyset
for each
j
.

The purpose of the remainder of this section is to establish two estimates for the exploration that we have defined. In Section
5.2
, we will prove a tail bound for
\sup_{j}|u_{j}|
. Then, in Section
5.3
, we will show that the number of crossings that the exploration discovers is a.s. finite. (One can see that the exploration a.s. discovers at most finitely many level lines at each stage using the same argument used to prove Lemma
5.5
.)

5.2.
Maximum height

Lemma 5.1
.

For each
H>0
and
0<a<b<H
there exist constants
c_{0},M_{0}>0
so that

\mathbf{P}\!\left[\sup_{j\geq 1}|u_{j}|\geq M\right]\leq\exp(-c_{0}M^{1/3}\log(M))\quad\text{for all}\quad M\geq M_{0}.

The strategy to prove Lemma
5.1
is as follows. We will fix
M\in\mathbf{N}
and for each
1\leq m\leq M-1
let
v_{m}=\tfrac{m}{M}+\tfrac{i(a+b)}{2}
. We will then consider the conditional expectation of
h
evaluated at the points
v_{m}
in
\mathcal{R}
given the exploration parameterized by
\log
-conformal radius as seen from
v_{m}
. By
[
35
, Proposition 6.5]
, such a conditional expectation evolves as a standard Brownian motion and its value is dominated by the boundary values of
h
along those crossings which are close to
v_{m}
and before disconnecting
v_{m}
from
\partial^{\mathrm{L}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
. Each time the exploration discovers a new crossing of
\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
which is close to
v_{m}
and before disconnecting
v_{m}
from
\partial^{\mathrm{L}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
this gives rise to a change in the boundary values of the GFF hence of the aforementioned Brownian motion. In particular, in order to see a crossing with a large height there must be one of the points
v_{m}
near which crossings are observed where the height increases dramatically. On this event, the associated Brownian motion will change values by a large amount in a small amount of time and the probability of this is bounded by the standard tail estimate for the maximum of a Brownian motion. We do not believe that the stretched exponential decay that we obtain in the statement of Lemma
5.1
is optimal, but it will suffice for our purposes.

Fix a point
z_{0}\in\mathcal{R}\setminus\mathrm{cl}(\mathcal{R}_{a,b})
and let
(K_{s})_{s\geq 0}
denote the exploration parameterized by
\log
-conformal radius as seen from
z_{0}
. The particular choice of time parameterization here is not important, as we mentioned just above we shall reparameterize it according to
\log
-conformal radius as seen from a number of different points. More precisely, we set
\sigma=\log({\rm CR}(z_{0},\mathcal{R}))-\log({\rm CR}(z_{0},\widehat{D}))
where
K=\mathrm{cl}(\cup_{j\geq 1}K_{j})
and
\widehat{D}
is the connected component of
\mathcal{R}\setminus K
containing
z_{0}
. We continuously parameterize the union of
\partial^{\mathrm{L}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
with the level lines discovered in
\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
by
(\widetilde{K}_{s})_{0\leq s<1}
in the order that they are discovered. Then for all
0\leq t<\sigma
, we set

\displaystyle\widetilde{s}(t)=\inf\{s\geq 0:t=\log({\rm CR}(z_{0},\mathcal{R}))-\log({\rm CR}(z_{0},\widetilde{D}_{s}))\}

where
\widetilde{D}_{s}
is the connected component of
\mathcal{R}\setminus\widetilde{K}_{s}
containing
z_{0}
. We set
K_{t}=\widetilde{K}_{\widetilde{s}(t)}
for
0\leq t<\sigma
. Observe that under the above parameterization we have that

t=\log({\rm CR}(z_{0},\mathcal{R}))-\log({\rm CR}(z_{0},D_{t}))

where
D_{t}=\widetilde{D}_{\widetilde{s}(t)}
.

In proving Lemma
5.1
, we consider the following setup. Fix
1\leq m\leq M-1
and let
\sigma_{m}
be the first time
t
that
K_{t}
disconnects
v_{m}
from
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
or
\sigma_{m}=\sigma
if that does not occur. We set
s_{m}=\lim_{t\to\sigma_{m}}(\log({\rm CR}(v_{m},\mathcal{R}))-\log({\rm CR}(v_{m},D_{t})))
. We also set
s_{m}(t)=\inf\{s\geq 0:\log({\rm CR}(v_{m},\mathcal{R}))-\log({\rm CR}(v_{m},D_{s}))=t\}
for
0\leq t\leq s_{m}
. Thus, writing
D^{m}_{t}=D_{s_{m}(t)}
for
0\leq t\leq s_{m}
we have that
t=\log({\rm CR}(v_{m},\mathcal{R}))-\log({\rm CR}(v_{m},D^{m}_{t}))
. Furthermore, we write
K^{m}_{t}=K_{s_{m}(t)}
. We let
\zeta_{m}
be the first time
t
that
K^{m}_{t}
disconnects
v_{m}
from
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
and set
\xi_{m}=\zeta_{m}\wedge s_{m}
. Finally, we let
T_{m}
be the first time
t
that
K^{m}_{t}
discovers a crossing
L_{j}
with height outside of
[-M,M]
such that
\mathrm{dist}(v_{m},L_{j})\in[M^{-100},2M^{-1}]
and all the previously discovered crossings have height in
[-M,M]
.

We begin with the following lemma.

Lemma 5.2
.

Consider the setting above and for each
1\leq i\leq M-1
, let
L_{j(i)}
denote the first crossing that separates
v_{i}
from
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
(if it exists). Let
E_{M}
be the event that there is no crossing
L_{k}
,
k<j(i)
with height outside
[-M,M]
such that
\mathrm{dist}(v_{i},L_{k})\geq M^{-100}
. There exist constants
c_{0}>0
and
M_{0}\geq 0
such that
\mathbf{P}[E_{M}^{c}]\leq\exp(-c_{0}M^{2}/\log(M))
for all
M\geq M_{0}
.

Proof.

Step 1. It is unlikely that the first crossing with large height is of distance in
[M^{-100},2M^{-1}]
from some
v_{i}
.
Suppose that the event
\{T_{m}<\xi_{m}\}
occurs and let
L^{*}
be the crossing observed at time
T_{m}
. Observe that there exists a constant
p>0
depending only on
H
,
a
, and
b
such that if we start a Brownian motion from
v_{m}
, then with probability at least
p
it does not exit
D^{m}_{t}
in
\partial^{\mathrm{D}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}\cup\partial^{\mathrm{U}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
for all
0\leq t<\xi_{m}
. For any simply connected domain
D
and
z\in D
, we have by the Schwarz lemma and the Koebe-
1/4
theorem that
\mathrm{dist}(z,\partial D)\leq{\rm CR}(z,D)\leq 4\mathrm{dist}(z,\partial D)
. It follows that
{\rm CR}(v_{m},D_{T_{m}}^{m})\geq M^{-100}
and
{\rm CR}(v_{m},\mathcal{R})\leq 2
. Since
T_{m}=\log({\rm CR}(v_{m},\mathcal{R}))-\log({\rm CR}(v_{m},D^{m}_{T_{m}}))
it follows that
T_{m}\leq\log(2)+100\log(M)\leq 200\log(M)
for all
M\geq 2
. We set
\mathcal{G}_{t}^{m}=\sigma(K^{m}_{s\wedge s_{m}}:0\leq s\leq t)
and
\mathfrak{h}_{t}^{m}(v_{m})=\mathbf{E}[h(v_{m})\,\,|\,\,\mathcal{G}_{t}^{m}]
. Note that
\mathfrak{h}^{m}_{0}
has boundary values given by
\lambda
(resp.
-\lambda
) on
\partial^{\mathrm{L}}\mathcal{R}
(resp.
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}
) and zero on
\partial^{\mathrm{D}}\mathcal{R}\cup\partial^{\mathrm{U}}\mathcal{R}
. In particular the boundary values are bounded uniformly and since
\mathfrak{h}^{m}_{0}
is harmonic in
\mathcal{R}
it follows that there exists universal constant
c_{1}>0
such that
\mathfrak{h}^{m}_{0}(v_{m})\in[-c_{1},c_{1}]
. Next, we claim that
\mathfrak{h}^{m}_{T_{m}}(v_{m})-\mathfrak{h}_{0}^{m}(v_{m})\notin[-cM,cM]
where
c\in(0,1)
is a universal constant. Indeed, there is a universal constant
p_{0}>0
such that with probability at least
p_{0}
, a Brownian motion started from
v_{m}
exits
D_{T_{m}}^{m}
in
L^{*}
. To see this, note that a Brownian motion has a positive probability (independent of
M,a,b
) of hitting
\{\mathrm{Re}(z)=v_{m}-3/M\}
before exiting the
M^{-1}
-neighborhood of
[v_{m}-3/M,v_{m}]
. Upon doing so, there is a universal constant
p_{*}>0
such that the Brownian motion travels clockwise around
B(v_{m},2/M)
before exiting
B(v_{m},4/M)
and before hitting
[v_{m}-i4/M,v_{m}-i2/M]
in the counterclockwise direction. By symmetry the corresponding holds in the counterclockwise direction. At least one of these events amount to hitting
L^{*}
before exiting
D_{T_{m}}^{m}
. Moreover, the Beurling estimate implies that the probability that the same Brownian motion exits
D^{m}_{T_{m}}\cap\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
in
\partial^{\mathrm{D}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}\cup\partial^{\mathrm{U}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
is
O(M^{-1/2})
where the implicit constant depends only on
H
,
a
and
b
. Therefore the crossing
L^{*}
gives a contribution to
|\mathfrak{h}_{T_{m}}^{m}(v_{m})|
of at least
p_{0}(M-\lambda)
while the crossings discovered before
L^{*}
give a contribution of at most
O(M^{1/2})
. Similarly, the parts of
\partial D_{T_{m}}^{m}
lying outside of
\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
give a contribution of order
O(M^{-1/2})
. Combining it follows that
|\mathfrak{h}_{T_{m}}^{m}(v_{m})-\mathfrak{h}_{0}^{m}(v_{m})|\geq p_{0}M/2
for all
M
sufficiently large and this proves the claim. Note that
M^{-1}\geq M^{-100}
and that
\mathfrak{h}_{t}^{m}(v_{m})-\mathfrak{h}_{0}^{m}(v_{m})
evolves as a standard Brownian motion up to time
s_{m}

[
35
, Proposition 6.5]
. Hence, for a standard Brownian motion
B
the probability that
T_{m}<\xi_{m}
is at most
\mathbf{P}_{0}[\sup_{0\leq t\leq 200\log(M)}|B_{t}|>cM]
and the latter is at most
\exp(-c_{1}M^{2}/\log(M))
for some constant
c_{1}>0
. Summing over
1\leq m\leq M-1
, we obtain that the probability that there exists
1\leq m\leq M-1
such that we can find a crossing with distance in
[M^{-100},2M^{-1}]
of
v_{m}
which does not disconnect
v_{m}
from
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
and with height outside of
[-M,M]
and such that all of the previously discovered crossings have height in
[-M,M]
is at most
M\exp(-c_{1}M^{2}/\log(M))\leq\exp(-c_{2}M^{2}/\log(M))
.

Step 2. It is unlikely that there exists a crossing with large height and with distance at least
M^{-100}
of some
v_{i}
.
Suppose that there exist
1\leq i\leq M-1
such that there is a crossing
L^{\prime}
with height outside of
[-M,M]
,
\mathrm{dist}(v_{i},L^{\prime})\in[M^{-100},2M^{-1}]
and which does not disconnect
v_{i}
from
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
, let
1\leq k\leq M-1
be the smallest such
i
and let
L_{*}^{k}
be the first crossing satisfying the above with
v_{k}
. Let
L
be the leftmost crossing which is discovered by the exploration in
\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
with height outside of
[-M,M]
. We want to show that
L=L_{*}^{k}
. If this is not the case, then
L
has to be to the left of
L_{*}^{k}
and
\mathrm{dist}(v_{k},L)\geq M^{-100}
. Let
v_{m}
be the leftmost point of the
v_{\ell}
’s such that
L
is to the left of
v_{m}
and
\mathrm{dist}(v_{m},L)\geq M^{-100}
. Then
m\leq k
and
\mathrm{dist}(v_{m},L)\leq 2M^{-1}
(otherwise
v_{m-1}
would be to the right of
L
and
\mathrm{dist}(v_{m-1},L)>M^{-100}
, contradicting the choice of
m
). Moreover, the height of
L
lies outside of
[-M,M]
and thus
m=k
and all of the crossings discovered before
L
have height in
[-M,M]
. This shows that
L=L_{*}^{k}
. Combining, it follows that off an event with probability at most
\exp(-c_{2}M^{2}/\log(M))
the following holds. For each
1\leq i\leq M-1
, if we explore towards
v_{i}
, then we cannot find a crossing with height outside of
[-M,M]
which does not disconnect
v_{i}
from
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
and whose distance from
v_{i}
lies between
M^{-100}
and
2M^{-1}
.
∎

Next we shall see that if a crossing gets very close to a point
v_{m}
, then the next crossing is very likely to disconnect
v_{m}
from
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
. We introduce the following notation. Let
n_{1}
be the first
j
such that
L_{j}
is within distance
M^{-100}
of some
v_{k}
,
k\leq M/2
which it does not disconnect from
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
and let
m_{1}
be the smallest
k
such that this occurs. Furthermore, we let
N_{1}
be the first
j\geq n_{1}+1
such that
L_{j}
disconnects
v_{m_{1}}
from
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
. Next, we define inductively the sequences
(n_{\ell})_{\ell\geq 1}
,
(m_{\ell})_{\ell\geq 1}
and
(N_{\ell})_{\ell\geq 1}
as follows. We let
n_{\ell+1}
be the first
j\geq N_{\ell}
such that
L_{j}
is within distance
M^{-100}
of some
v_{k}
,
k\leq M/2
which it does not disconnect from
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
,
m_{\ell+1}
be the smallest such
k
and
N_{\ell+1}
be the first
j\geq n_{\ell+1}+1
such that
L_{j}
disconnects
v_{m_{\ell+1}}
from
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
. Finally, we let
\mathcal{F}_{k}
be the
\sigma
-algebra generated by the level lines which make up
K_{1},\ldots,K_{k}
. Then we have the following result.

Lemma 5.3
.

There exist a constant
c>0
such that the following is true. Let
F_{\ell}
be the event that
n_{\ell}<\infty
and
\max_{1\leq j\leq n_{\ell}}|u_{j}|\leq M^{3}
. Then

\mathbf{P}[N_{\ell}-n_{\ell}\geq M\,|\,\mathcal{F}_{n_{\ell}}]{\bf 1}_{F_{\ell}}\leq\exp(-cM\log M){\bf 1}_{F_{\ell}}.

Proof.

Step 1. Setup.
We will prove that there exist constants
a,c>0
so that

\mathbf{P}[N_{\ell}-n_{\ell}\geq 2\,|\,\mathcal{F}_{n_{\ell}}]{\bf 1}_{F_{\ell}}\leq cM^{-a}{\bf 1}_{F_{\ell}}.

The result will then follow by iteratively applying this bound. The strategy of the proof is to show that by generating level lines of the GFF starting from
L_{n_{\ell}}
sufficiently close to
v_{m_{\ell}}
, we are very likely to find one level line which cuts off
v_{m_{\ell}}
from
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
so that the next crossing cannot travel between
L_{n_{\ell}}
and
v_{m_{\ell}}
.

We let
D_{n_{\ell}}
be the connected component of
\mathcal{R}\setminus\cup_{i=1}^{n_{\ell}}K_{i}
containing
v_{m_{\ell}}
and let
x_{\ell}
(resp.
y_{\ell}
) be the point on
\partial^{\mathrm{U}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
(resp.
\partial^{\mathrm{D}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
) which is visited by
L_{n_{\ell}}
. We then let
\psi_{\ell}
be the conformal transformation mapping
D_{n_{\ell}}
onto
\mathbf{H}
such that
\psi_{\ell}(x_{\ell})=-1
,
\psi_{\ell}(y_{\ell})=1
and
\psi_{\ell}(1)=\infty
. Note that
\psi_{\ell}
is
\mathcal{F}_{n_{\ell}}
-measurable and that the field
h\circ\psi_{\ell}^{-1}
on
\mathbf{H}
can be expressed as
h^{0}+\mathfrak{h}_{\ell}
, where
h^{0}
is a zero boundary GFF on
\mathbf{H}
and
\mathfrak{h}_{\ell}
is a function which is harmonic on
\mathbf{H}
and has piecewise constant boundary conditions that change only a finite number of times. Next we observe that
\mathrm{dist}(v_{m_{\ell}},\partial^{\mathrm{U}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}\cup\partial^{\mathrm{D}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b})=\tfrac{b-a}{2}
and
\mathrm{dist}(v_{m_{\ell}},\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b})\geq\tfrac{1}{M}
. Hence the Beurling estimate implies that the probability that a Brownian motion starting from
v_{m_{\ell}}
exits
D_{n_{\ell}}
either in
\cup_{i=1}^{n_{\ell}-1}K_{j}
or in parts of
\partial D_{n_{\ell}}
lying outside of
\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
is
O(M^{-50})
. Those parts give a contribution to
|\mathfrak{h}_{\ell}\circ\psi_{\ell}(v_{m_{\ell}})|
at most
O(M^{-50}(M^{3}+\lambda))=O(M^{-47})
. Similarly, the probability that the Brownian motion exits
D_{n_{\ell}}
in
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
is
O(M^{-99/2})
so that its contribution to
|\mathfrak{h}_{\ell}\circ\psi_{\ell}(v_{m_{\ell}})|
is
O(M^{-99/2})
. It follows that
\mathfrak{h}_{\ell}(\psi_{\ell}(v_{m_{\ell}}))=u_{n_{\ell}}+O(1)
. Note that
h
has boundary values on the right side of
L_{n_{\ell}}
given by either
\lambda+u_{n_{\ell}}
or
-\lambda+u_{n_{\ell}}
. In the former case, we have that
u_{n_{\ell}+1}=u_{n_{\ell}}+u
while in the latter case we have that
u_{n_{\ell}+1}=u_{n_{\ell}}-u
. Without loss of generality, we can assume that the former case holds. Then
h\circ\psi_{\ell}^{-1}
has boundary values given by
\lambda+u_{n_{\ell}}
on
[-1,1]
. Set
\psi_{\ell}(v_{m_{\ell}})=a_{\ell}+ib_{\ell}
and
d_{\ell}=\mathrm{dist}(\psi_{\ell}(v_{m_{\ell}}),\mathbf{R}\setminus[-1,1])
. The above imply that the probability that a Brownian motion starting from
\psi_{\ell}(v_{m_{\ell}})
exits
\mathbf{H}
in
\partial\mathbf{H}\setminus[-1,1]
is at most
O(M^{-50})
. This implies that
\arg(\psi_{\ell}(v_{m_{\ell}})+1)=O(M^{-50})
and
\arg(\psi_{\ell}(v_{m_{\ell}})-1)=\pi-O(M^{-50})
which in turn implies that
b_{\ell}/d_{\ell}=O(M^{-50})
.

Step 2. Half-annuli and separation of
\psi_{\ell}(v_{m_{\ell}})
.
Let
B_{k,\ell}=\mathbf{H}\cap B(a_{\ell},Mb_{\ell}e^{-k})
and consider the half-annuli
A_{k,\ell}=\mathbf{H}\cap(B(a_{\ell},Mb_{\ell}e^{-k})\setminus\mathrm{cl}(B(a_{\ell},Mb_{\ell}e^{-k}/2)))
for
0\leq k\leq N
where
N=\lfloor\log(M)\rfloor-1
. Note that
A_{k,\ell}
disconnects
\psi_{\ell}(v_{m_{\ell}})
from
\infty
for all
0\leq k\leq N
. For each
0\leq k\leq N
we let
\eta_{k}
be the level line of
h\circ\psi_{\ell}^{-1}
of height
u_{n_{\ell}+1}=u_{n_{\ell}}+u
starting from the midpoint
c_{k}
of the interval of
\partial A_{k}\cap\mathbf{R}
to the left of
a_{\ell}
. We draw each
\eta_{k}
up until the first time
\tau_{k}
that it exits
A_{k,\ell}
and let
P_{k}
be the event that
\eta_{k}
disconnects
\psi_{\ell}(v_{m_{\ell}})
from
\infty
before time
\tau_{k}
. Let also
\mathcal{G}_{k,n_{\ell}}
be the
\sigma
-algebra generated by
\mathcal{F}_{n_{\ell}}
and all of
\eta_{0},\ldots,\eta_{k}
and let
D_{k,\ell}
be the unbounded connected component of
\mathbf{H}\setminus\cup_{i=0}^{k}\eta_{i}
.

In order to see that the event
\cup_{k=0}^{N}P_{k}
is likely to occur, we shall compare with a GFF with nicer boundary data. Let
\widehat{h}_{\ell}
be a GFF on
\mathbf{H}
with boundary data
\lambda+u_{n_{\ell}}
and for each
k\leq N
, let
\widehat{\eta}_{k}
be the height
u_{n_{\ell}+1}
level line from
c_{k}
stopped upon exiting
A_{k,\ell}
. Furthermore, denote by
\widehat{\mu}_{k,\ell}
the law of
\widehat{h}_{\ell}
restricted to
B_{k,\ell}
and let
\widehat{P}_{k}
be the event that
\widehat{\eta}_{k}
disconnects
\psi_{\ell}(v_{m_{\ell}})
from
\infty
before exiting
A_{k,\ell}
. Note that
\widehat{P}_{k}
is determined by the restriction of
\widehat{h}_{\ell}
to
B_{k,\ell}
. Since
\widehat{\eta}_{k}\sim{\rm SLE}_{4}(-2+u/\lambda,-u/\lambda)
, that is, both force points have weight in
(-2,0)
it follows by scale invariance and
[
41
, Lemma 2.5]
that there is some
p>0
such that
\widehat{\mu}_{k,\ell}(\widehat{P}_{k})\geq p
for all
k
.

Thus, if we for each
k
let
\mu_{k,\ell}
denote the conditional law of
h\circ\psi_{\ell}^{-1}
restricted to
B_{k,\ell}
given
\mathcal{G}_{k,n_{\ell}}
and prove that there exist constants
C>0
and
q>0
such that conditional on
\cap_{j=1}^{k}P_{j}^{c}
,

(5.1)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mu}_{k+1,\ell}(\widehat{P}_{k+1})^{q}\leq C\mu_{k+1,\ell}(P_{k+1}),

then it follows that
\sum_{k=1}^{N}{\bf 1}_{P_{k}}
stochastically dominates a geometric random variable with success probability
p_{0}=p^{q}/C>0
and hence the result follows since level lines of the same height cannot cross each other.

Step 3. Absolute continuity and the proof of (
5.1
).

In this step we shall prove that (
5.1
) holds. We will begin by showing that on
\cap_{i=0}^{k}P_{i}^{c}
, the restrictions of
h\circ\psi_{\ell}^{-1}
and
\widehat{h}_{\ell}
to
\mathbf{H}\cap B(a_{\ell},Mb_{\ell}e^{-k-1})
can be coupled together so that their zero-boundary and harmonic parts are equal and independent, respectively.

Suppose that we are working on the event
\cap_{i=0}^{k}P_{i}^{c}
for
0\leq k\leq N
fixed. Then the Markov property implies that the restriction of
h\circ\psi_{\ell}^{-1}
to
D_{k,\ell}
can be expressed as
h^{0,k}+\mathfrak{h}_{k,\ell}
conditional on
\mathcal{G}_{k,n_{\ell}}
, where
h^{0,k}
is a zero-boundary GFF on
D_{k,\ell}
and
\mathfrak{h}_{k,\ell}
is harmonic in
D_{k,\ell}
,
\mathcal{G}_{k,n_{\ell}}
-measurable, and independent of
h^{0,k}
. Set
B_{k,\ell}^{*}=\mathbf{H}\cap B(a_{\ell},Mb_{\ell}e^{-k}(\tfrac{1}{4}+\tfrac{1}{2e}))
and note that
B_{k+1,\ell}\subseteq B_{k,\ell}^{*}
and
B_{k,\ell}^{*}\cap A_{k,\ell}=\emptyset
. Similarly,
h^{0,k}
can be expressed as
\widetilde{h}^{0,k}+\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{k,\ell}
where
\widetilde{h}^{0,k}
is a zero boundary GFF on
B_{k+1,\ell}^{*}
and
\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{k,\ell}
is harmonic in
B_{k+1,\ell}^{*}
. In the same way,
\widehat{h}_{\ell}
can be decomposed as
\widehat{h}^{0}+\lambda+u_{n_{\ell}}
where
\widehat{h}^{0}
is a zero-boundary GFF on
\mathbf{H}
. Also
\widehat{h}^{0}
can be expressed as
\widehat{h}^{0,k}+\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}_{k,\ell}
where
\widehat{h}^{0,k}
is a zero boundary GFF on
B_{k+1,\ell}
and
\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}_{k,\ell}
is harmonic on
B_{k+1,\ell}
. Hence we can couple
h\circ\psi_{\ell}^{-1}
and
\widehat{h}_{\ell}
on the same probability space such that
\widetilde{h}^{0,k}=\widehat{h}^{0,k}
and that
\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}_{k,\ell}
,
\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{k,\ell}
are independent. Let
g\in C_{0}^{\infty}(B(0,1))
be fixed and such that
|g(z)|\leq 1
,
|\nabla g(z)|\leq C
for all
z
(where
C>0
is universal) and
g|_{B(0,e^{-1}(1/4+1/2e)^{-1})}\equiv 1
. Then we set
g_{k,\ell}(w)=g(M^{-1}b_{\ell}^{-1}e^{k}(\tfrac{1}{4}+\tfrac{1}{2e})^{-1}(w-a_{\ell}))
and so
g_{k,\ell}\in C_{0}^{\infty}(B_{k,\ell}^{*})
and
g_{k,\ell}|_{B_{k+1,\ell}}\equiv 1
. It follows from the proof of
[
35
, Proposition 3.4]
that conditional on
\mathcal{G}_{k,n_{\ell}}
and on the event
\cap_{i=0}^{k}P_{i}^{c}
, the law of the restriction of
h\circ\psi_{\ell}^{-1}
to
B_{k+1,\ell}
weighted by
\mathcal{Z}^{-1}\exp((\widetilde{h}^{0,k},f_{k,\ell})_{\nabla})
with
\mathcal{Z}=\exp(\|f_{k,\ell}\|_{\nabla}^{2}/2)
is equal to the law of
\widehat{h}_{\ell}
restricted to
B_{k+1,\ell}
, where
f_{k,\ell}=g_{k,\ell}((\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}_{k,\ell}-\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{k,\ell})-(\mathfrak{h}_{k,\ell}-\lambda-u_{n_{\ell}}))
.

With the Radon-Nikodym derivative between laws of
h\circ\psi_{\ell}^{-1}
and
\widehat{h}_{\ell}
restricted to
B_{k+1,\ell}
at our hands, we shall prove (
5.1
). We fix
p>1
(to be taken very close to
1
) and let
q>1
be such that
\tfrac{1}{p}+\tfrac{1}{q}=1
. Then Hölder’s inequality implies that

\displaystyle\widehat{\mu}_{k+1,\ell}(\widehat{P}_{k+1})\leq\mathbf{E}_{\mu_{k+1,\ell}}[\exp((p^{2}-p)\|f_{k,\ell}\|_{\nabla}^{2}/2)]^{\frac{1}{p}}\mu_{k+1,\ell}(P_{k+1})^{\frac{1}{q}}.

We claim that
\mathbf{E}_{\mu_{k+1,\ell}}[\exp((p^{2}-p)\|f_{k,\ell}\|_{\nabla}^{2}/2)]\lesssim 1
if
p
is chosen sufficiently close to
1
where the implicit constant depends only on
p
. Indeed, first we note that
g_{k,\ell}(\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}_{k,\ell}-\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{k,\ell})
and
g_{k,\ell}(\mathfrak{h}_{k,\ell}-\lambda-u_{n_{\ell}})
are independent and that
