\|f_{k,\ell}\|_{\nabla}^{2}\leq 2\|g_{k,\ell}(\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}_{k,\ell}-\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{k,\ell})\|_{\nabla}^{2}+2\|g_{k,\ell}(\mathfrak{h}_{k,\ell}-\lambda-u_{n_{\ell}})\|_{\nabla}^{2}
. It follows from the argument used to prove
[
34
, Lemma 4.1]
that
\mathbf{E}_{\mu_{k+1,\ell}}[\exp((p^{2}-p)\|g_{k,\ell}(\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}_{k,\ell}-\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{k,\ell})\|_{\nabla}^{2})]\lesssim 1
if
p
is chosen sufficiently close to
1
, where the implicit constant depends only on
p
. We now focus on the term
\|g_{k,\ell}(\mathfrak{h}_{k,\ell}-\lambda-u_{n_{\ell}})\|_{\nabla}^{2}
. Observe that

\displaystyle\|g_{k,\ell}(\mathfrak{h}_{k,\ell}-\lambda-u_{n_{\ell}})\|_{\nabla}^{2}\lesssim\|\nabla\mathfrak{h}_{k,\ell}\|_{2L^{2}(B_{k,\ell}^{*})}^{2}+\sup_{w\in B_{k,\ell}^{*}}|\mathfrak{h}_{k,\ell}(w)-\lambda-u_{n_{\ell}}|^{2},

since
\|\nabla g_{k,\ell}\|^{2}\lesssim 1
. We begin by bounding the supremum. Note that the boundary values of
\mathfrak{h}_{k,\ell}
on
\cup_{i=0}^{k}\eta_{i}
are
u_{n_{\ell}}+O(1)
. Fix
w\in B(a_{\ell},4Mb_{\ell}e^{-k}/9)
. Observe that
\mathrm{dist}(w,\partial\mathbf{H})\lesssim Mb_{\ell}
and so
\mathrm{dist}(w,\partial\mathbf{H})\leq\mathrm{dist}(w,\partial\mathbf{H}\setminus[-1,1])O(M^{-49})
. The Beurling estimate implies that
\omega(w,\partial\mathbf{H}\setminus[-1,1],D_{k,\ell})=O(M^{-49/2})
and so this gives a contribution to
\mathfrak{h}_{k,\ell}(w)
of order
O(M^{-43/2})
from the images under
\psi_{\ell}
of the parts of
\partial D_{n_{\ell}}
in
\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
except for the right side of
L_{n_{\ell}}
. Overall, it follows that
\mathfrak{h}_{k,\ell}(w)-\lambda-u_{n_{\ell}}=O(1)
for
w\in\mathbf{H}\cap B(a_{\ell},4Mb_{\ell}e^{-k}/9)
, where the implicit constant is universal. As for the other term, we note that
\mathfrak{h}_{k,\ell}-\lambda-u_{n_{\ell}}
can be extended by the reflection principle to a harmonic function in
B(a_{\ell},4Mb_{\ell}e^{-k}/9)
since
\mathfrak{h}_{k,\ell}-\lambda-u_{n_{\ell}}\equiv 0
in
B(a_{\ell},4Mb_{\ell}e^{-k}/9)\cap\partial\mathbf{H}
. Moreover, standard estimates for harmonic functions imply that
|\nabla\mathfrak{h}_{k,\ell}(w)|\lesssim M^{-1}b_{\ell}^{-1}e^{k}
for all
w\in B_{k,\ell}^{*}
, where the implicit constant is universal. Therefore we have that
\|\nabla\mathfrak{h}_{k,\ell}\|_{L^{2}(B_{k,\ell}^{*})}^{2}\lesssim 1
. Therefore, there exists a universal constant
C>1
such that
\widehat{\mu}_{k+1,\ell}(\widehat{P}_{k+1})\leq C\mu_{k+1,\ell}(P_{k+1})
conditional on
\mathcal{G}_{k,n_{\ell}}
and on the event
\cap_{i=0}^{k}P_{i}^{c}
. Thus we have proved that (
5.1
) holds and thus by the discussion in Step 2, the proof is complete.
∎

In the following lemma we will make sure that the crossings have bounded height, even when they get within distance
M^{-100}
of the points
(v_{m})
.

Lemma 5.4
.

There exist constants
c>0
and
M_{0}\in\mathbf{N}
such that the following holds. Let
G_{M}
be the event that there exists a crossing of height outside of
[-M^{3},M^{3}]
discovered before
v_{\lfloor M/2\rfloor}
is disconnected from
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
(or before the exploration terminates if
v_{\lfloor M/2\rfloor}
is never disconnected). Then
\mathbf{P}[G_{M}]\leq\exp(-cM\log(M))
for all
M\geq M_{0}
.

Proof.

Step 1. Setup.
Let
E_{M}
be the event of Lemma
5.2
and suppose that we are working on
E_{M}\cap G_{M}
. Let
J
be the first
i
such that
n_{i}<\infty
,
N_{i}-n_{i}\geq M
and the exploration does not stop after
M
steps of the discovery of
L_{n_{i}}
. In what follows, we are first going to show that
G_{M}\cap E_{M}\subseteq\{J<\infty\}\cap E_{M}
(Step 2) and then combine this with Lemma
5.2
to prove the desired upper bound on
\mathbf{P}[G_{M}]
(Step 3).

Step 2.
G_{M}\cap E_{M}\subseteq\{J<\infty\}\cap E_{M}
.
We first consider the case that there is some
L_{j^{*}}
separating
v_{1},\dots,v_{\lfloor M/2\rfloor-1}
but not
v_{\lfloor M/2\rfloor}
from
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
. Let
L_{j}
be a crossing of height outside of
[-M^{3},M^{3}]
which is discovered before
v_{\lfloor M/2\rfloor}
is disconnected from
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
(or before the exploration terminates if
v_{\lfloor M/2\rfloor}
is never disconnected). First, we we will show that
n_{1}\neq\infty
. Indeed, suppose that
n_{1}=\infty
. Let
v_{i}
be the leftmost point that is not disconnected from
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
by
L_{j}
. Then
1\leq i\leq M/2
and since
n_{1}=\infty
, we must have that
\mathrm{dist}(v_{i},L_{j})>M^{-100}
. But then, since
E_{M}
occurs,
L_{j}
must have height in
[-M,M]
which is a contradiction. Thus
n_{1}<\infty
and
\ell=\max\{i\in\mathbf{N}:n_{i}<\infty\}
is hence well-defined. Note that by the definition of the
n_{i}
’s, we must have that
1\leq\ell\leq M/2
.

Suppose that
m_{\ell}<\lfloor M/2\rfloor
. Then we must have that
N_{\ell}<\infty
(since
v_{m_{\ell}}
is disconnected from
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
by
L_{j^{*}}
),
n_{\ell+1}=\infty
and that
v_{\lfloor M/2\rfloor}
is not disconnected by
L_{N_{\ell}}
. Since
E_{M}
occurs, all of the crossings
L_{1},\ldots,L_{n_{1}-1}
have heights in
[-M,M]
and so
|u_{i}|\leq M
for each
1\leq i\leq n_{1}-1
and
|u_{n_{1}}|\leq|u_{n_{1}-1}|+u\leq M+u
, since every time that we discover a new crossing the height either decreases or increases by
u
. It follows that
|u_{i}|\leq M+u+u\sum_{k=1}^{\ell}(N_{k}-n_{k})
for each
1\leq i\leq N_{\ell}
. Suppose that
N_{k}-n_{k}\leq M
for each
1\leq k\leq\ell
. Then
|u_{i}|\leq M+u+uM^{2}\leq M^{3}
for each
1\leq i\leq N_{\ell}
. Since
n_{\ell+1}=\infty
and
E_{M}
occurs, it follows that every crossing discovered after
L_{N_{\ell}}
and up until
v_{\lfloor M/2\rfloor}
is disconnected from
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
(or up until the end of the exploration if no such time exists) has height in
[-M^{3},M^{3}]
, but that is a contradiction. Therefore there exists
1\leq k\leq\ell
such that
N_{k}-n_{k}>M
and
N_{k}<\infty
. Therefore in this case we have that
J<\infty
.

Suppose that
m_{\ell}=\lfloor M/2\rfloor
. If
N_{k}-n_{k}\leq M
for each
1\leq k\leq\ell
, then
|u_{i}|\leq M+u+u\sum_{k=1}^{\ell}(N_{k}-n_{k})\leq M+u+uM^{2}\leq M^{3}
for each
1\leq i\leq N_{\ell}
. This is a contradiction because then
v_{\lfloor M/2\rfloor}
will be disconnected from
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
by the time
L_{N_{\ell}}
is discovered hence the exploration will not discover a crossing with height outside
[-M^{3},M^{3}]
before disconnecting
v_{\lfloor M/2\rfloor}
. Hence there exists
1\leq k\leq\ell
such that
N_{k}-n_{k}>M
. Suppose that
N_{k}-n_{k}\leq M
for each
1\leq k\leq\ell-1
. Then either we have that
N_{\ell}-n_{\ell}>M
and
N_{\ell}<\infty
or
N_{\ell}=\infty
. Suppose also that
N_{\ell}=\infty
and the exploration stops after at most
M
steps after
L_{n_{\ell}}
is discovered. Again we have that
|u_{i}|\leq M+u+uM^{2}
for each
1\leq i\leq N_{\ell-1}
. If
N_{\ell-1}=n_{\ell}
, then it follows that every crossing discovered after
L_{n_{\ell}}
has height whose absolute value is at most
M+u+uM^{2}+uM\leq M^{3}
, but that is a contradiction. If
N_{\ell-1}<n_{\ell}
, then every crossing discovered after
L_{N_{\ell-1}}
and before
L_{n_{\ell}}
has height in
[-M,M]
and so
L_{n_{\ell}}
has height in
[-M-u,M+u]
. Consequently, every crossing discovered after
L_{n_{\ell}}
has height in
[-M-u(M+1),M+u(M+1)]
, and so again we have a contradiction. Therefore in this case we have that
J<\infty
.

We next consider the case that there does not exist a crossing
L_{j}^{*}
as described at the beginning of the step. We let
v_{n}
be the leftmost of the
v_{i}
’s for
1\leq i\leq\lfloor M/2\rfloor
which is not disconnected from
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
in the step just before the step at which
v_{\lfloor M/2\rfloor}
is disconnected from
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
if such disconnection occurs or the leftmost point of the
v_{i}
’s for
1\leq i\leq\lfloor M/2\rfloor
which is not disconnected from
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
up until the end of the exploration if no such disconnection occurs. It follows that
m_{\ell}\leq n
since
m_{\ell}<\lfloor M/2\rfloor
. Therefore a similar argument to the one given in the previous paragraphs implies that
J<\infty
if
G_{M}\cap E_{M}
occurs.

Combining everything we obtain that in every case
G_{M}\cap E_{M}\subseteq\{J<\infty\}\cap E_{M}
.

Step 3. Upper bound on
\mathbf{P}[G_{M}]
.
By Lemma
5.2
, there exists
c_{1}>0
such that
\mathbf{P}[E_{M}^{c}]\leq\exp(-c_{1}M^{2}/\log(M))
and thus

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}[G_{M}]\leq\exp(-c_{1}M^{2}/\log(M))+\mathbf{P}[\{J<\infty\}\cap E_{M}]

for all
M
sufficiently large. Let
B_{1}
be the event that
n_{1}<\infty
and
|u_{i}|\leq M
for each
1\leq i\leq n_{1}
such that
\mathrm{dist}(v_{m_{1}},L_{i})\geq M^{-100}
. Let also
B_{\ell}
be the event that
n_{\ell}<\infty
,
N_{i}-n_{i}\leq M
for each
1\leq i\leq\ell-1
and
|u_{i}|\leq M
for each
1\leq i\leq n_{\ell}
such that
\mathrm{dist}(v_{m_{\ell}},L_{i})\geq M^{-100}
for
2\leq\ell\leq M-1
. Writing
N_{\ell}-n_{\ell}\geq M
to mean that either
N_{\ell}<\infty
and
N_{\ell}-n_{\ell}\geq M
or
N_{\ell}=\infty
and the exploration has not ended after
M
steps of the discovery of
L_{n_{\ell}}
, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}[\{J<\infty\}\cap E_{M}]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{m=1}^{M-1}\mathbf{P}[\{J=m\}\cap E_{M}]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\ell=1}^{M-1}\mathbf{E}[\mathbf{P}[N_{\ell}-n_{\ell}\geq M\,\,|\,\,\mathcal{F}_{n_{\ell}}]{\bf 1}_{B_{\ell}}].

For each
1\leq\ell\leq M-1
we let
C_{\ell}
be the event that
n_{\ell}<\infty
and
|u_{i}|\leq M^{3}
for each
1\leq i\leq n_{\ell}
. Observe that the
B_{\ell}
’s and
C_{\ell}
’s are all
\mathcal{F}_{n_{\ell}}
-measurable and
B_{\ell}\subseteq C_{\ell}
for each
1\leq\ell\leq M-1
. Therefore it follows that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}[G_{M}]\leq\exp(-c_{1}M^{2}/\log(M))+\sum_{\ell=1}^{M-1}\mathbf{E}[\mathbf{P}[N_{\ell}-n_{\ell}\geq M\,\,|\,\mathcal{F}_{n_{\ell}}]{\bf 1}_{C_{\ell}}].

But Lemma
5.3
implies that
\mathbf{P}[N_{\ell}-n_{\ell}\geq M\,\,|\,\,\mathcal{F}_{n_{\ell}}]{\bf 1}_{C_{\ell}}\leq\exp(-c_{2}M\log(M)){\bf 1}_{C_{\ell}}
for all
M
sufficiently large, where
c_{2}>0
is a fixed constant. Combining we obtain that
\mathbf{P}[G_{M}]\leq\exp(-c_{1}M^{2}/\log(M))+M\exp(-c_{2}M\log(M))
which concludes the proof.
∎

The final lemma we need in order to prove Lemma
5.1
is the following, which states that we cannot have an infinite number of crossings in the exploration without disconnecting the points
v_{i}
from
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
in
\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
.

Lemma 5.5
.

Fix
M\in\mathbf{N}
and for each
1\leq i\leq M-1
let
C_{i,M}
be the event that either
v_{i}
is disconnected from
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
in
\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
by some crossing of the exploration or the exploration hits
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
after finitely many crossings. Then we have that
\mathbf{P}[C_{i,M}^{c}]=0
.

Proof.

Step 1. Setup.

Fix
1\leq i\leq M-1
. For each
j\in\mathbf{N}
, we let
K_{j}
be as in Section
5.1
and let
D_{j}
be the connected component of
\mathcal{R}\setminus K_{j}
whose boundary contains
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
. We set
K=\mathrm{cl}(\cup_{j\geq 1}K_{j})
and let
D
be the connected component of
\mathcal{R}\setminus K
whose boundary contains
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
. Note that
K_{j}
and
K
are local sets for
h
which are also determined by
h
. Let
\mathfrak{h}
and
\mathfrak{h}_{j}
be the associated harmonic functions. Let
c_{0},M_{0}>0
be the constants of Lemma
5.2
and
a>0
be the constant from Lemma
5.3
. For
m\in\mathbf{N}_{0}
we let
J_{m}
be the first
j\in\mathbf{N}
such that
L_{j}
does not disconnect
v_{i}
from
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
and
\mathrm{dist}(v_{i},L_{j})\leq m^{-100}
. Let also
N
be the minimum of the number of crossings discovered by the exploration and the first
j
such that
L_{j}
disconnects
v_{i}
from
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
.

Step 2.
v_{i}
is disconnected from
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
whenever the exploration discovers infinitely many crossings and gets arbitrarily close to
v_{i}
.

We claim that
\mathbf{P}[N=\infty,v_{i}\in K]=0
. Indeed, first we note that
\mathbf{P}[N=\infty,v_{i}\in K]=\lim_{m\to\infty}\mathbf{P}[N=\infty,J_{m}<\infty]
. Also Lemma
5.3
implies that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}[N=\infty\,|\,\mathcal{F}_{J_{m}}]{\bf 1}_{J_{m}<\infty}{\bf 1}_{\max_{1\leq\ell\leq J_{m}}|u_{\ell}|\leq m+u}\leq O(m^{-a}).

Let
E_{m}
be as in Lemma
5.2
and note that if
E_{m}
occurs, then we have that
\max_{1\leq\ell\leq J_{m}}|u_{\ell}|\leq m+u
and this implies that
\mathbf{P}[E_{m},N=\infty,J_{m}<\infty]\leq O(m^{-a})
. Combining with Lemma
5.2
we obtain that
\mathbf{P}[N=\infty,J_{m}<\infty]\leq O(m^{-a})+\exp(-c_{0}m^{2}/\log(m))
. Thus the claim follows by taking
m\to\infty
, and so it suffices to show that
\mathbf{P}[N=\infty,v_{i}\notin K]=0
.

Step 3.
v_{i}
is disconnected from
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
whenever the exploration discovers infinitely many crossings and does not accumulate at
v_{i}
.

Step 3a. The accumulation points of the exploration do not disconnect
v_{i}
from
\partial\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
.

Fix
m\in\mathbf{N}_{0}
. We claim that
\mathbf{P}[N=\infty,J_{m}=\infty,E_{m}]=0
. Indeed, suppose that we are working on the event
E_{m}\cap\{N=\infty\}\cap\{J_{m}=\infty\}
. Suppose first that
v_{i}\in D
and let
U
be the connected component of
\mathcal{R}_{a,b}\setminus K
containing
v_{i}
. For each
j\in\mathbf{N}
, we let
U_{j}
be the connected component of
\mathcal{R}_{a,b}\setminus K_{j}
containing
v_{i}
. We assume that
\partial U\nsubseteq K
and fix
z_{0}\in\partial U\setminus K
. We let
\phi
(resp.
\phi_{j}
) be the unique conformal transformation mapping
U
(resp.
U_{j}
) onto
\mathbf{D}
such that
\phi(v_{i})=0
(resp.
\phi_{j}(v_{i})=0
) and
\phi(z_{0})=1
(resp.
\phi_{j}(z_{0})=1
). Let
x_{j}
(resp.
y_{j}
) be the rightmost intersection point of
K_{j}
with
\mathcal{R}_{a,b}^{\mathrm{D}}
(resp.
\mathcal{R}_{a,b}^{\mathrm{U}}
). Note that for each
j\in\mathbf{N}
, the harmonic measure of of the clockwise (resp. counterclockwise) arc of
\partial\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
from
z_{0}
to
x_{j}
(resp.
y_{j}
) in
U_{j}
as seen from
v_{i}
is at least the harmonic measure of the clockwise (resp. counterclockwise) arc of
\partial\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
from
z_{0}
to
x_{j+1}
(resp.
y_{j+1}
) in
U_{j+1}
as seen from
v_{i}
. This implies that
I_{j}\subseteq I_{j+1}
where
I_{j}=\phi_{j}(K_{j}^{\mathrm{R}})
and so
\mathrm{length}(I\setminus I_{j})\to 0
as
j\to\infty
where
I=\cup_{j\geq 1}I_{j}
. We claim that
\mathfrak{h}_{j}\to\mathfrak{h}
as
j\to\infty
locally uniformly in
D
. Indeed, let
(q_{m})_{m\geq 1}
be an enumeration of the points in
\mathcal{R}
with rational coordinates. Then the martingale convergence theorem implies that
\mathfrak{h}_{j}(q_{\ell})\to\mathfrak{h}(q_{\ell})
as
j\to\infty
a.s. on the event that
q_{\ell}\notin K
for each
\ell\in\mathbf{N}
. The claim then follows since the
\mathfrak{h}_{j}
’s and
\mathfrak{h}
are harmonic functions and their boundary values lie in
[-m,m]
(since
E_{m}
occurs) hence are equicontinuous on compact sets.

Next we claim that
\phi_{j}^{-1}\to\phi^{-1}
as
j\to\infty
locally uniformly in
\mathrm{cl}(\mathbf{D})\setminus\mathrm{cl}(I)
. Indeed, let
x_{0}
be the lower left corner of
\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
and let
x_{1}
(resp.
x_{2}
) be the lower (resp. upper) right corner of
\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
. Let
F
be the unique conformal transformation mapping
\mathbf{H}
onto
\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
such that
F(\infty)=x_{0},F(-1)=x_{1},F(1)=x_{2}
and set
\psi_{j}=\phi_{j}^{-1}
. Note that
\psi_{j}=F\circ\widetilde{\psi}_{j}
where
\widetilde{\psi}_{j}
is the unique conformal transformation mapping
\mathbf{D}
onto
\mathbf{H}\setminus F^{-1}(K_{j})
such that
\widetilde{\psi}_{j}(0)=F^{-1}(v_{i})
and
\widetilde{\psi}_{j}(1)=F^{-1}(z_{0})
. Note that there exists
\epsilon>0
such that
B(z_{0},\epsilon)\cap\mathcal{R}_{a,b}\subseteq U
and
\omega(v_{i},\partial U_{j}\cap B(z_{0},\epsilon),U_{j})\geq p=\omega(v_{i},\partial U\cap B(z_{0},\epsilon),U)>0
and so
\mathrm{length}(\phi_{j}(\partial U_{j}\cap B(z_{0},\epsilon)))\geq 2\pi p
for each
j\in\mathbf{N}
. Since
\phi_{j}(\partial U_{j}\cap B(z_{0},\epsilon))
is an arc of
\partial\mathbf{D}
such that
I_{j}\cap\phi_{j}(\partial U_{j}\cap B(z_{0},\epsilon))=\emptyset
, it follows that
1\notin\mathrm{cl}(I)
. Thus
\widetilde{\psi}_{j}(\partial\mathbf{D}\setminus I)
is a non-trivial interval of
\mathbf{R}\cap\partial\mathbf{H}\setminus F^{-1}(K_{j})
such that
\mathrm{dist}(\widetilde{\psi}_{j}(\partial\mathbf{D}\setminus I),F^{-1}(K_{j}))>0
. Hence the reflection principle implies that
\widetilde{\psi}_{j}
extends to a conformal mapping defined on
\mathbf{D}_{I}^{*}
, where
\mathbf{D}_{I}^{*}
is the reflection of
\partial\mathbf{D}
with respect to
\partial\mathbf{D}\setminus\mathrm{cl}(I)
. Fix a set
X
which is compact in
\mathrm{cl}(\mathbf{D})\setminus\mathrm{cl}(I)
. Note that there exists
R>0
such that
\mathbf{H}\setminus F^{-1}(K_{j})\subseteq B(0,R)
for each
j
and so standard estimates for conformal maps imply that
\sup_{w\in X}|\widetilde{\psi}_{j}^{\prime}(w)|\lesssim 1
and
\sup_{w\in\mathbf{D}}|\widetilde{\psi}_{j}(w)|\leq R
where the implicit constant does not depend on
j
. Thus by the Arzela-Ascoli theorem we obtain that if we fix a subsequence
(\widetilde{\psi}_{j^{\prime}})_{j\geq 1}
, then we can find a further subsequence
(\widetilde{\psi}_{j^{\prime\prime}})_{j\geq 1}
and a conformal map
\widetilde{\psi}
such that
\widetilde{\psi}_{j^{\prime\prime}}\to\widetilde{\psi}
as
j\to\infty
locally uniformly in
\mathrm{cl}(\mathbf{D})\setminus\mathrm{cl}(I)
. Note that
|\widetilde{\psi}_{j}^{\prime}(0)|\geq m^{-100}|(F^{-1})^{\prime}(v_{i})|/4
for each
j
which implies that
\widetilde{\psi}^{\prime}(0)\neq 0
and so
\widetilde{\psi}
is a univalent function. Note that
F
is a homeomorphism away from
\infty
and so
\psi_{j^{\prime\prime}}\to\psi
as
j\to\infty
locally uniformly in
\mathrm{cl}(\mathbf{D})\setminus\mathrm{cl}(I)
where
\psi=F\circ\widetilde{\psi}
. Note that
\psi(\mathbf{D})\subseteq U
. To see this, fix
w\in\mathbf{D}
and
r\in(0,1)
such that
w\in B(0,r)
. Then standard estimates for conformal maps imply that
|\psi_{j}^{\prime}(z)|\gtrsim 1
for each
z\in B(0,r)
where the implicit constant does not depend on
j
or
z
. It follows that
\mathrm{dist}(\psi_{j}([0,w]),\partial U_{j})\gtrsim 1
and so
\mathrm{dist}(\psi([0,w]),\partial U)\gtrsim 1
. Since
U
is connected,
\psi(0)=v_{i}\in U
and
\psi([0,w])
is a path connecting
v_{i}
to
\psi(w)
, it follows that
\psi(w)\in U
and so
\psi(\mathbf{D})\subseteq U
. Note that
\psi(1)=z_{0}
and so to prove the claim we need to show that
U\subseteq\psi(\mathbf{D})
since the subsequence
(\psi_{j^{\prime}})_{j\geq 1}
was arbitrary. To show the latter, by applying again as before the Arzela-Ascoli theorem, we can assume that
\phi_{j^{\prime\prime}}\to\widetilde{\phi}
as
j\to\infty
locally uniformly in
U
for some univalent function
\widetilde{\phi}
taking values in
\mathbf{D}
. Then the claim follows since
\psi_{j^{\prime\prime}}(\phi_{j^{\prime\prime}}(w))=w
and so taking
j\to\infty
gives that
\psi(\widetilde{\phi}(w))=w
for each
w\in U
.

Now we observe that
\phi_{j+1}^{-1}(I_{j})\subseteq K_{j+1}^{\mathrm{R}}
since
I_{j}\subseteq I_{j+1}
and so
\mathfrak{h}_{j+1}\circ\phi_{j+1}^{-1}-\mathfrak{h}_{j}\circ\phi_{j}^{-1}
has boundary values on
I_{j}
given by
d_{j}
, where
d_{j}
is the difference between the boundary values of
\mathfrak{h}_{j+1}
on
K_{j+1}^{\mathrm{R}}
and
\mathfrak{h}_{j}
on
K_{j}^{\mathrm{R}}
. Thus we have that

(5.2)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{h}_{j+1}\circ\phi_{j+1}^{-1}(0)-\mathfrak{h}_{j}\circ\phi_{j}^{-1}(0)=\frac{d_{j}\mathrm{length}(I_{j})}{2\pi}+\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\partial\mathbf{D}\setminus I_{j}}(\mathfrak{h}_{j+1}\circ\phi_{j+1}^{-1}(w)-\mathfrak{h}_{j}\circ\phi_{j}^{-1}(w))dw.

Note that
\mathfrak{h}_{j+1}\circ\phi_{j+1}^{-1}(w)-\mathfrak{h}_{j}\circ\phi_{j}^{-1}(w)\to 0
as
j\to\infty
for Lebesgue a.e.
w\in\partial\mathbf{D}\setminus\mathrm{cl}(I)
since
\psi_{j}\to\psi
as
j\to\infty
locally uniformly in
\mathrm{cl}(\mathbf{D})\setminus\mathrm{cl}(I)
and
\mathfrak{h}_{j}\to\mathfrak{h}
as
j\to\infty
locally uniformly in
D
. Hence the second term of the right side of (
5.2
) tends to
0
as
j\to\infty
since
\|\mathfrak{h}_{j}\|_{\infty}\leq m
and
\mathrm{length}(I\setminus I_{j})\to 0
as
j\to\infty
. Moreover we have that
\liminf_{j\to\infty}|d_{j}|\mathrm{length}(I_{j})/2\pi\geq c\mathrm{length}(I)/2\pi>0
where
c=\min\{u,2\lambda-u\}
. Hence (
5.2
) implies that
\liminf_{j\to\infty}|\mathfrak{h}_{j+1}\circ\phi_{j+1}^{-1}(w)-\mathfrak{h}_{j}\circ\phi_{j}^{-1}(w)|=\liminf_{j\to\infty}|\mathfrak{h}_{j+1}(v_{i})-\mathfrak{h}_{j}(v_{i})|>0
. This is a contradiction since
\mathfrak{h}_{j+1}(v_{i})-\mathfrak{h}_{j}(v_{i})\to 0
as
j\to\infty
.

Step 3b. The limiting points of the exploration fill in open neighborhoods of
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
.

Now suppose that either
K
disconnects
v_{i}
from
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
or
v_{i}
is not disconnected but
\partial U\subseteq K
. In both cases we let
\phi_{j}
be the unique conformal transformation mapping
U_{j}
onto
\mathbf{D}
such that
\phi_{j}(v_{i})=0
and
\phi_{j}(x_{j})=-i
. Again if we set
I_{j}=\phi_{j}(K_{j}^{\mathrm{R}})
, we have that
I_{j}\subseteq I_{j+1}
for each
j\in\mathbf{N}
and
\mathrm{length}(I_{j})\to 2\pi
as
j\to\infty
. It follows that (
5.2
) still holds and the second term in the right side of (
5.2
) tends to
0
as
j\to\infty
. Hence we have that
\liminf_{j\to\infty}|\mathfrak{h}_{j+1}(v_{i})-\mathfrak{h}_{j}(v_{i})|>0
. Again the martingale convergence theorem implies that
\mathfrak{h}_{j}(v_{i})
converges as
j\to\infty
a.s. in both cases, and so we obtain a contradiction. Therefore we have that
\mathbf{P}[N=\infty,J_{m}=\infty,E_{m}]=0
.

Step 3c. Conclusion of the proof.

Finally, the previous analysis implies that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}[N=\infty,J_{m}=\infty]\leq\mathbf{P}[E_{m}^{c}]\leq\exp(-c_{0}m\log(m)).

Since the event
v_{i}\notin K
implies that
J_{m}=\infty
for all
m
sufficiently large, taking a limit as
m\to\infty
in the above implies that
\mathbf{P}[N=\infty,v_{i}\notin K]=0
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
5.1
.

We consider the case that
M
is even. By Lemma
5.4
we see that if we explore the crossings of
\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
from left to right, we are very unlikely to find any crossing with height outside of
[-M^{3},M^{3}]
before either disconnecting
v_{\lfloor M/2\rfloor}
from
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
or terminating the exploration. By symmetry, we may define the analogous exploration from right to left, and we obtain the same bound on the probability that this new exploration discovers a crossing with height outside
[-M^{3},M^{3}]
before disconnecting
v_{\lfloor M/2\rfloor}
from
\partial^{\mathrm{L}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
. Denote this event by
G_{M}^{*}
. Let
C_{M}
be the event from Lemma
5.5
in the case that
i=\lfloor M/2\rfloor
. Also, by arguing in the same way and using Lemma
5.5
, we obtain that a.s. the exploration from right to left discovers finitely many crossings in the case that it does not disconnect
v_{\lfloor M/2\rfloor}
from
\partial^{\mathrm{L}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
. Denote this event by
C_{M}^{*}
.

Suppose that we are working on the event
G_{M}^{c}\cap C_{M}\cap(G_{M}^{*})^{c}\cap C_{M}^{*}
. If the left to right exploration does not disconnect
v_{\lfloor M/2\rfloor}
from
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
, then all of the crossings of that exploration have heights in
[-M^{3},M^{3}]
(since
G_{M}^{c}
occurs). Suppose that the left to right exploration disconnects
v_{\lfloor M/2\rfloor}
from
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
.
Note that the right to left exploration either disconnects
v_{\lfloor M/2\rfloor}
from
\partial^{\mathrm{L}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
or it discovers finitely many crossings (since
C_{M}^{*}
occurs). Therefore, since both of the explorations are connected, if we are in the first case, then every crossing discovered by the left to right exploration after it discovers the crossing disconnecting
v_{\lfloor M/2\rfloor}
from
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
disconnects the left and right sides of
\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
and so the only way that the right to left exploration can disconnect
v_{\lfloor M/2\rfloor}
from
\partial^{\mathrm{L}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
is by hitting the crossing. Similarly, if we are in the second case, then the only way that the right to left exploration can hit
\partial^{\mathrm{L}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
is by hitting every crossing discovered by the left to right exploration. It follows that in both cases, every crossing discovered by the left to right exploration after it discovers the crossing disconnecting
v_{\lfloor M/2\rfloor}
from
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
is hit by a crossing of the right to left exploration discovered before the latter disconnects
v_{\lfloor M/2\rfloor}
from
\partial^{\mathrm{L}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
. We note that two level lines can intersect only if their height difference is less than
2\lambda
(Theorem
2.4
). Moreover every crossing discovered by the right to left exploration before disconnecting
v_{\lfloor M/2\rfloor}
has height in
[-M^{3},M^{3}]
(since
(G_{M}^{*})^{c}
occurs). It follows that every crossing discovered by the left to right exploration after it disconnects
v_{\lfloor M/2\rfloor}
from
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
has height in
[-M^{3}-2\lambda,M^{3}+\lambda]
and so the same is true for every crossing discovered from that exploration. Consequently there exists a constant
c>0
such that off an event with probability at most
\exp(-cM\log(M))
every crossing in the left to right exploration has height in
[-M^{3},M^{3}]
. This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

5.3.
Number of crossings

Lemma 5.6
.

For each
H>0
and
0<a<b<H
we have that the exploration a.s. discovers only finitely many crossings.

We shall now bound the probability that we discover a crossing
L
which separates
v_{M-1}
from
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
but does not intersect
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
. With this at hand we can use Lemma
5.5
to get that the number of crossings discovered up until
v_{M-1}
is disconnected from
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
or the exploration hits
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
is finite. In the former case, the crossing disconnecting
v_{M-1}
from
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
is the last crossing of the exploration, which will conclude the proof of Lemma
5.6
.

Lemma 5.7
.

Let
J_{M}
be the first
j
so that
K_{j}
disconnects
v_{M-1}
from
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
in
\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
. There exists a constant
a>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}[J_{M}<\infty,L_{J_{M}}\cap\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}=\emptyset,E_{M}]=O(M^{-a}).

Proof.

The proof is essentially the same as that of Lemma
5.3
. We let
v_{*}=1+i(a+b)/2
,
N_{*}=\lceil 1-\log_{2}(a+b)\rceil
,
N^{*}=\lfloor\log M\rfloor
and
A_{k}=B(v_{*},2^{-k})\setminus B(v_{*},\tfrac{3}{4}\cdot 2^{-k})
. Then for all
N_{*}\leq k\leq N^{*}
(and all
M
sufficiently large)
A_{k}
separates
v_{M-1}
from
\partial\mathcal{R}_{a,b}\setminus\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
and
\mathrm{dist}(A_{k},A_{k+1})=2^{-k-2}
. For such
k
, we let
x_{k}
be the midpoint of the lower interval of
\partial A_{k}\cap\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
and
\eta_{k}
be the level line of
h
of height
\widetilde{u}\in(-2\lambda,0)
starting from
x_{k}
. Moreover, we run
\eta_{k}
up until the first time
\tau_{k}
that it exits
A_{k}
and note that it may intersect
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
away from
x_{k}
. Let
\widetilde{E}_{k}
be the event that
\eta_{k}
separates
v_{M-1}
from
\partial\mathcal{R}_{a,b}\setminus\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
before exiting
A_{k}
and
\widetilde{E}=\cup_{k=N_{*}}^{N^{*}}\widetilde{E}_{k}
. By the same strategy as Lemma
5.3
we have that there exists
a>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}[\widetilde{E}^{c}\cap E_{M}]=O(M^{-a}).

Since two level lines can never cross (Theorem
2.4
), we have that on
\widetilde{E}\cap\{{J_{M}}<\infty\}\cap E_{M}
, the level line
L_{J_{M}}
must hit
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
in order to pass into the regions of
\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
which are cut off by
\cup_{k}\eta_{k}
. Thus, on the event
\widetilde{E}\cap\{{J_{M}}<\infty\}\cap E_{M}
,
L_{J_{M}}
must intersect
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
in order to separate
v_{M-1}
from
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
. Thus the lemma is proved.
∎

Proof of Lemma
5.6
.

Let
J_{M}
be as in the statement of Lemma
5.7
. Lemma
5.5
implies that a.s. either
J_{M}<\infty
or the exploration hits
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
after discovering a finite number of crossings and before disconnecting
v_{M-1}
from
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
in
\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
. Note that on the event
J_{M}<\infty
and
L_{J_{M}}\cap\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}\neq\emptyset
the exploration hits
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{a,b}
after discovering only finitely many crossings. The result thus follows by combining this observation with Lemmas
5.2
and
5.7
and taking a limit as
M\to\infty
.
∎

6.
Exploration of annulus crossings

6.1.
Setup and definition of the exploration

Figure 5.
Illustration of the setup for the exploration of the crossings across the annulus
A_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}
. Note that by the definition of
{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}
we have that
H_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}\geq H_{0}
so that the rectangles
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{D}}
,
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{I}}
,
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{U}}
are contained in
(0,1)\times(0,H_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}})
. The crossings that the exploration in
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{D}}
,
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{U}}
discovers are colored alternating in dark green and orange. We remark that the exploration discovers many strands which are not part of a crossing, but for the sake of convenience only the crossings are shown.

Throughout, we let
h
be a GFF on
\mathbf{H}
with boundary conditions
\lambda
(resp.
-\lambda
) on
\mathbf{R}_{+}
(resp.
\mathbf{R}_{-}
) and let
\eta
be the level line of
\mathbf{H}
from
0
to
\infty
. Then
\eta
is an
{\rm SLE}_{4}
in
\mathbf{H}
from
0
to
\infty
. We denote by
\eta^{\mathrm{L}}
(resp.
\eta^{\mathrm{R}}
) the collection of prime ends corresponding to
\eta
in the boundary of the component of
\mathbf{H}\setminus\eta
which is to the left (resp. right) of
\eta
. See Figure
5
for an illustration of the setup (in the context of Section
6.2
described just below).

We define the annuli and stopping times for
\eta
as in Section
4.1
. Fix
H_{0}>0
and
\xi\in(0,H_{0}/10)
. Let
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{D}}=(0,1)\times(2\xi,3\xi)
,
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{U}}=(0,1)\times(H_{0}-3\xi,H_{0}-2\xi)
, and
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{I}}=(0,1)\times(3\xi,H_{0}-3\xi)
. As in Section
5
, for
q\in\{\mathrm{D},\mathrm{U}\}
, we let
\partial^{\mathrm{D}}\mathcal{R}^{q}
,
\partial^{\mathrm{U}}\mathcal{R}^{q}
,
\partial^{\mathrm{L}}\mathcal{R}^{q}
and
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}^{q}
be the lower, upper, left and right boundary of
\mathcal{R}^{q}
, respectively. For each
z\in\mathbf{H}
and
k\in\mathbf{N}
with
H_{z,k}\geq H_{0}
we perform the exploration as described in Section
5
using the GFF
h\circ\varphi_{z,k}^{-1}
in each of
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{D}}
and
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{U}}
. For
q\in\{\mathrm{D},\mathrm{U}\}
, we let
(L_{j}^{q})
,
(u_{j}^{q})
, respectively, be the crossings and heights discovered by the exploration.

Part of this reason that we have defined the exploration in this way comes from the following lemma.

Lemma 6.1
.

On
\tau_{z,k}<\infty
and
H_{z,k}\geq H_{0}
, the number of times that
\eta
crosses
A_{z,k}
is at most the minimal number of
j
such that
u_{j}^{q}=0
for
q\in\{\mathrm{D},\mathrm{U}\}
.

Proof.

Suppose that
\eta
makes a crossing
L
across
A_{z,k}
. Then by Theorem
2.4
the level lines which make up the exploration in
\varphi_{z,k}^{-1}(\mathcal{R}^{q})
for
q\in\{\mathrm{D},\mathrm{U}\}
cannot cross
L
.
∎

6.2.
Iterating the exploration across annuli

We are now going to describe how to iterate the exploration from Section
6.1
across successive annuli. Suppose that we have fixed
z\in\mathbf{H}
. We let
{{\mathbf{k}}}_{0}
be the smallest
k\in\mathbf{N}
so that
A_{z,k}\subseteq\mathbf{H}
. We fix
a\in(0,1)
,
H_{0},\delta_{0}>0
,
b\in(1,2)
,
\xi_{1}\in(0,(e^{-b}-e^{-2})/2)
,
\xi_{0}\in(0,H_{0}/2)
,
\widetilde{\xi}_{1}>0
,
\delta_{1}\in(0,1)
,
\widetilde{\xi}_{0}\in(0,H_{0}/2)
,
\xi_{2}>0
and
M_{0}>1
. We also fix
\xi\in(0,H_{0}/10)
such that
\xi<\xi_{0}/3
and perform the explorations in
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{U}}
and
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{D}}
as in Section
6.1
. We let
{{\mathbf{k}}}_{1}
be the smallest
\ell\geq{{\mathbf{k}}}_{0}
so that

(i)

\tau_{z,\ell}<\infty
,
H_{z,\ell}\geq H_{0}
,
\inf_{\tau_{z,\ell-1}\leq t\leq\tau_{z,\ell}}S_{t}\geq\delta_{0}
, and

(ii)

the events
F_{z,\ell}^{1},F_{z,\ell}^{2}
, and
C_{z,\ell}
of Lemmas
4.3
,
4.4
, and
4.5
respectively occur with the above choice of constants, where
\xi_{2}=3\xi_{1}/2
and
\widetilde{\xi}_{1}=\delta_{2}
with
\delta_{2}
as in Lemma
4.5
.

If there is no such
\ell
, then we let
{{\mathbf{k}}}_{1}=\infty
. On the event
\tau_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{1}}<\infty
, we perform the exploration as in Section
6.1
in
A_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{1}}
. Let
M_{1}\in\mathbf{N}
be the smallest
n\in\mathbf{N}
so that the height of all of the crossings discovered in
A_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{1}}
is in
[-n,n]
. Suppose that we have defined
{{\mathbf{k}}}_{1},\ldots,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}
and
M_{1},\ldots,M_{j}
. If
{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}=\infty
, we set
{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}=\infty
and
M_{j+1}=0
. If
{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}<\infty
, we set
{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}^{*}=\lceil 2\log_{2}M_{j}\rceil=\lceil 2\log M_{j}/\log 2\rceil
and let
{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}
be the smallest
\ell\geq{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}+{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}^{*}+4
so that (
i
), (
ii
) hold. If there is no such
\ell
, we set
{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}=\infty
. If
{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}<\infty
, we perform the exploration from Section
6.1
in
A_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}
and let
M_{j+1}
be the smallest
n\in\mathbf{N}
so that the height of all of the crossings discovered in
A_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}
is in
[-n,n]
. We let
\mathcal{F}_{z,j}
be the
\sigma
-algebra generated by
\eta|_{[0,\tau_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}]}
and the exploration in each of
A_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{\ell}}
for
1\leq\ell\leq j
. We also let
\mathcal{F}_{z,j}^{-}
be the
\sigma
-algebra generated by
\eta|_{[0,\tau_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}]}
and the exploration in each of
A_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{\ell}}
for
1\leq\ell\leq j-1
.

The purpose of the following lemma is to illustrate why we have defined the successive scales in the manner that we have.

Lemma 6.2
.

There exists a constant
c_{0}>0
so that the following is true. On
\tau_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}<\infty
, we let
\mathfrak{h}_{z,j}
be the conditional expectation of
h
given
\mathcal{F}_{z,j}^{-}
. Then

(6.1)

\sup_{w\in A^{*}_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}}|\mathfrak{h}_{z,j}(w)|\leq c_{0}.

Let
\mathfrak{h}_{0}
be the harmonic function on
(0,1)\times(0,H_{0})
with boundary conditions equal to
0
on
[0,1]\times\{0,H_{0}\}
and
\lambda
(resp.
-\lambda
) on
\{0\}\times[0,H_{0}]
(resp.
\{1\}\times[0,H_{0}]
). Then for each
\delta\in(0,H_{0}/2)
there exists a constant
c_{1}>0
so that with
\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{z,j}=\mathfrak{h}_{z,j}\circ\varphi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}^{-1}
and
\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}_{z,j}=\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{z,j}-\mathfrak{h}_{0}
we have

(6.2)

\int_{(0,1)\times(\delta,H_{0}-\delta)}|\nabla\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}_{z,j}(w)|^{2}d\mathcal{L}_{2}(w)\leq c_{1}.

Proof.

Given
\mathcal{F}_{z,j}^{-}
and
\tau_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}<\infty
, let
D_{z,j}
be the component containing
z
in the complement of
\mathbf{H}
of the union of
\eta([0,\tau_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}])
and the level lines discovered by the exploration in
A_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{i}}
for
1\leq i\leq j-1
. Let
\mathfrak{h}^{+}
be the function which is harmonic in
D_{z,j}
with boundary values given by
-\lambda
(resp.
\lambda
) on the part of
\eta^{\mathrm{L}}([0,\tau_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}])
(resp.
\eta^{\mathrm{R}}([0,\tau_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}])
) in
\partial D_{z,j}
and
M_{j-1}+\lambda
on the rest of
\partial D_{z,j}
. We define
\mathfrak{h}^{-}
in the same way except we take the boundary values on the rest of
\partial D_{z,j}
to be given by
-M_{j-1}-\lambda
. Note that the exploration in
A_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j-1}}
disconnects
D_{z,j}
from the exploration in
A_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{i}}
for
1\leq i\leq j-2
. Also, combining
[
26
, Theorem 3.21]
with the fact that
|\psi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j-1}}^{\prime}(z)|=e^{4{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j-1}}
, we obtain that there exist universal constants
c_{1},c_{2}>0
such that
\mathrm{dist}(z,\partial^{\text{in}}A_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j-1}})\geq c_{1}e^{-4{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j-1}}
and
A_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}\subseteq B(z,c_{2}e^{-4{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}})\subseteq B(z,c_{1}e^{-4{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j-1}})
. It follows from the definition of
M_{j-1}
that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{h}^{-}(w)\leq\mathfrak{h}_{z,j}(w)\leq\mathfrak{h}^{+}(w)\quad\text{for all}\quad w\in B(z,c_{2}e^{-4{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}})\setminus\eta([0,\tau_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}]).

Note that that if
\mathbf{E}^{w}
denotes the expectation with respect to the law of a Brownian motion
B
, started from
w
, which is independent of
h
and
\eta
, then conditionally on
\mathcal{F}_{z,j}^{-}
, we have that
\mathfrak{h}_{z,j}(w)=\mathbf{E}^{w}[\mathfrak{h}_{z,j}(B_{\tau})\,|\,\mathcal{F}_{z,j}^{-}]
, where
\tau
is the first time that
B
exits
D_{z,j}
. By the Beurling estimate, there exists a constant
c_{0}>0
such that for all
w\in B(z,c_{2}e^{-4{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}})
the probability that a Brownian motion started from
w
exits
D_{z,j}
without first hitting
\eta([0,\tau_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}]))
is at most
c_{0}e^{-2{{\mathbf{k}}}^{*}_{j-1}}\leq c_{0}M_{j-1}^{-1}
. Consequently, the contribution to
\mathfrak{h}^{+}(w)
coming from the boundary values of
\mathfrak{h}^{+}
on the part of
\partial D_{z,j}
not in
\eta([0,\tau_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}]))
is at most
c_{0}(1+\lambda/M_{j-1})\leq c_{0}(1+\lambda)
whereas the contribution from the boundary values on
\eta([0,\tau_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}])
is at most
\lambda
. Altogether, this gives the desired upper bound for
\mathfrak{h}^{+}
. Applying the same argument for
\mathfrak{h}^{-}
gives the analogous lower bound for
\mathfrak{h}^{-}
, which completes the proof of (
6.1
).

We will now deduce (
6.2
) from (
6.1
). Fix
\delta\in(0,\frac{H_{0}}{2})
. We note that
\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}_{z,j}
is harmonic in
(0,1)\times(0,H_{0})
and its boundary values on
\{0,1\}\times(0,H_{0})
are equal to zero and, by (
6.1
), its boundary values on
(0,1)
and
(0,1)+H_{0}i
are at most
c_{0}
. We can extend
\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}_{z,j}
to be a harmonic function on
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}=(-1,2)\times(0,H_{0})
by reflection as follows. In
(-1,0]\times(0,H_{0})
we set
\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}_{z,j}(x+iy)=-\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}_{z,j}(-x+iy)
and in
[1,2)\times(0,H_{0})
we set
\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}_{z,j}(x+iy)=-\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}_{z,j}(2-x+iy)
. Then the extension of
\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}_{z,j}
(which we also refer to as
\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}_{z,j}
) is harmonic in
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}
with boundary values given by zero on
\{-1,2\}\times(0,H_{0})
and bounded by
c_{0}
on
[-1,2]\times\{0,H_{0}\}
. Note that

\displaystyle|\nabla\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}_{z,j}(w)|\lesssim\frac{\|\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}_{z,j}\|_{\infty}}{\mathrm{dist}(w,\partial\widetilde{\mathcal{R}})}\quad\text{for all}\quad w\in\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}

where the implicit constant is universal. In particular,
|\nabla\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}(w)|\lesssim c_{0}\delta^{-1}
for each
w\in(0,1)\times(\delta,H_{0}-\delta)
. This completes the proof of (
6.2
).
∎

Let
\mathcal{R}=(0,1)\times(0,H_{0})
,
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{in}}
be the interior of
\mathrm{cl}(\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{D}}\cup\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{I}}\cup\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{U}})
, and let
\mathbf{P}_{H_{0}}
denote the law of a GFF on
\mathcal{R}
with boundary conditions equal to
\lambda
(resp.
-\lambda
) on
\partial^{\mathrm{L}}\mathcal{R}
(resp.
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}
) and by
0
on
\partial^{\mathrm{D}}\mathcal{R}
and
\partial^{\mathrm{U}}\mathcal{R}
. Finally, let
\mathbf{P}_{H_{0}}^{\mathrm{in}}
denote the law of the restriction to
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{in}}
of a sample from
\mathbf{P}_{H_{0}}
.

Lemma 6.3
.

There exists a constant
c_{0}<\infty
depending only on
H_{0}
,
\xi
and a universal constant
p>1
so that the following is true. Let
\mathcal{Z}
be the Radon-Nikodym derivative of the conditional law of
h\circ\varphi^{-1}_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}|_{\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{in}}}
given
\mathcal{F}_{z,j}^{-}
and on
\tau_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}<\infty
with respect to
\mathbf{P}_{H_{0}}^{\mathrm{in}}
. Then
\mathbf{E}[\mathcal{Z}^{p}\,|\,\mathcal{F}_{z,j}^{-}]{\bf 1}_{\tau_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}<\infty}\leq c_{0}
.

Proof.

Throughout, we shall assume that we are working on the event that
\tau_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}<\infty
. As in the proof of Lemma
6.2
, let
D_{z,j}
be the component containing
z
of the complement in
\mathbf{H}
of the union of
\eta([0,\tau_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}])
and the level lines discovered in the exploration of
A_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{i}}
for
1\leq i\leq j-1
. First we note that conditional on
\mathcal{F}_{z,j}^{-}
we can write
h|_{D_{z,j}}=h^{0}+\mathfrak{h}_{z,j}^{0}
where
h^{0}
is a GFF in
D_{z,j}
with boundary values given by
-\lambda
(resp.
\lambda
) on the part of the left (resp. right) side of
\eta
contained in
\partial D_{z,j}
and
0
on the remaining parts of
\partial D_{z,j}
and
\mathfrak{h}_{z,j}^{0}
is a harmonic function in
D_{z,j}
which is measurable with respect to
\mathcal{F}_{z,j}^{-}
with
0
boundary values on the part of the left (resp. right) side of
\eta
in
\partial D_{z,j}
. Similarly, we can write
h^{0}=h^{1}+\mathfrak{h}_{z,j}^{1}
where
h^{1}
is a GFF in
\varphi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}^{-1}(\mathcal{R})
with boundary values given by
-\lambda
(resp.
\lambda
) on the part of the left (resp. right) side of
\eta
contained in
\partial\varphi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}^{-1}(\mathcal{R})
and
0
on the remaining part of
\partial\varphi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}^{-1}(\mathcal{R})
and
\mathfrak{h}_{z,j}^{1}
is harmonic in
\varphi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}^{-1}(\mathcal{R})
and conditionally independent of
h^{1}
given
\mathcal{F}_{z,j}^{-}
. Note that
\mathfrak{h}_{z,j}^{1}
has zero boundary values on the left and right sides of
\eta
.

For each
\epsilon>0
set
\mathcal{R}_{\epsilon}=(0,1)\times(\epsilon,H_{0}-\epsilon)
; note that
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{in}}=\mathcal{R}_{2\xi}
. Let
\phi\in C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathcal{R}_{\xi})
be such that
\phi|_{\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{in}}}\equiv 1
. Let
\phi_{z,j}=\phi\circ\varphi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}
and

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}=\mathbf{E}\!\left[\exp\left((h^{1},\phi_{z,j}(\mathfrak{h}_{z,j}^{0}+\mathfrak{h}_{z,j}^{1}))_{\nabla}-\frac{1}{2}\|\phi_{z,j}(\mathfrak{h}_{z,j}^{0}+\mathfrak{h}_{z,j}^{1})\|^{2}_{\nabla}\right)\,\middle|\,h^{1}|_{\varphi^{-1}_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}(\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{in}})},\mathcal{F}_{z,j}^{-}\right].

Note that if we weight the conditional law of
h^{1}|_{\varphi^{-1}_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}(\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{in}})}
given
\mathcal{F}_{z,j}^{-}
by
\mathcal{Z}
then we obtain the conditional law of
h|_{\varphi^{-1}_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}(\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{in}})}
given
\mathcal{F}_{z,j}^{-}
. Note also that the field
h^{1}\circ\varphi^{-1}_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}|_{\mathcal{R}}
has the law of a GFF in
\mathcal{R}
with boundary values given by
\lambda
(resp.
-\lambda
) on
\partial^{\mathrm{L}}\mathcal{R}
(resp.
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}
) and
0
on
\partial^{\mathrm{U}}\mathcal{R}
,
\partial^{\mathrm{D}}\mathcal{R}
. Fix
p>1
. We need to give an upper bound on
\mathbf{E}[\mathcal{Z}^{p}\,|\,\mathcal{F}_{z,j}^{-}]
. By Jensen’s inequality, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}[\mathcal{Z}^{p}\,|\,\mathcal{F}_{z,j}^{-}]\leq\mathbf{E}[\exp((p^{2}-p)/2\|\phi_{z,j}(\mathfrak{h}_{z,j}^{0}+\mathfrak{h}_{z,j}^{1})\|^{2}_{\nabla})\,|\,\mathcal{F}_{z,j}^{-}].

Here, we used that
\mathfrak{h}_{z,j}^{0}
and
\mathfrak{h}_{z,j}^{1}
are conditionally independent given
\mathcal{F}_{z,j}^{-}
and that
h^{1}
is conditionally independent of
(\mathfrak{h}_{z,j}^{0},\mathfrak{h}_{z,j}^{1})
given
\mathcal{F}_{z,j}^{-}
. Next, we have that

\displaystyle\|\phi(\mathfrak{h}_{z,j}^{1}\circ\varphi^{-1}_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}})\|_{\nabla}\lesssim\|\phi\|_{\infty}\|\mathfrak{h}_{z,j}^{1}\circ\varphi^{-1}_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}\|_{\nabla}+\|\mathfrak{h}_{z,j}^{1}\circ\varphi^{-1}_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}\|_{\infty}\|\phi\|_{\nabla}

where the norms are computed on
\mathcal{R}_{\xi}
and the implicit constants are universal. First, we focus on the term
I=\|\mathfrak{h}_{z,j}^{1}\circ\varphi^{-1}_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}\|_{\infty}
. Arguing as in the proof of Lemma
6.2
, we can extend
\mathfrak{h}_{z,j}^{1}\circ\varphi^{-1}_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}|_{\mathcal{R}}
to a function
\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}^{1}
which is harmonic in
(-1,2)\times(0,H_{0})
. For each
\epsilon>0
we let
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{\epsilon}=(-1,2)\times(\epsilon,H_{0}-\epsilon)
. Let
\widetilde{\mathfrak{p}}
be the Poisson kernel in
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{\xi/2}
and let
\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{1}
denote Lebesgue measure on
\partial\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{\xi/2}
normalized to have unit mass. For each
w\in\mathcal{R}_{\xi}
we have that
\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}^{1}(w)=\int_{\partial\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{\xi/2}}\widetilde{\mathfrak{p}}(w,u)\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}^{1}(u)d\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{1}(u)
and so
|\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}^{1}(w)|\leq\int_{\partial\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{\xi/2}}\widetilde{\mathfrak{p}}(w,u)|\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}^{1}(u)|d\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{1}(u)
. Also, Harnack’s inequality implies that there exist
w_{0}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{\xi}
and
c_{0}<\infty
depending only on
H_{0}
and
\xi
such that
\widetilde{\mathfrak{p}}(w,u)\leq c_{0}\widetilde{\mathfrak{p}}(w_{0},u)
for each
w\in\mathcal{R}_{\xi}
and
u\in\partial\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{\xi/2}
. It follows that

\displaystyle I\leq c_{0}\int_{\partial\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{\xi/2}}\widetilde{\mathfrak{p}}(w_{0},u)|\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}^{1}(u)|d\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{1}(u)\leq c_{1}\int_{\partial\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{\xi/2}}|\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}^{1}(u)|d\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{1}(u)

where
c_{1}<\infty
depends only on
H_{0}
and
\xi
. Hence Jensen’s inequality implies that

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}[\exp(aI^{2})\,|\,\mathcal{F}_{z,j}^{-}]\leq\int_{\partial\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{\xi/2}}\mathbf{E}[\exp(ac_{1}^{2}|\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}^{1}(u)|^{2})\,|\,\mathcal{F}_{z,j}^{-}]d\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{1}(u).

Fix
u\in\partial\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{\xi/2}
. Then there exists
v\in\mathcal{R}_{\xi/2}
such that either
\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}^{1}(u)=\mathfrak{h}^{1}_{z,j}(\varphi^{-1}_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}(v))
or
\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}^{1}(u)=-\mathfrak{h}^{1}_{z,j}(\varphi^{-1}_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}(v))
. Let
\mathfrak{g}_{z,j}^{0}
(resp.
\mathfrak{g}_{z,j}^{1}
) be the harmonic function in
D_{z,j}
(resp.
\varphi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}^{-1}(\mathcal{R})
) with boundary values given by
-\lambda
(resp.
\lambda
) on the part of the left (resp. right) side of
\eta
contained in
\partial D_{z,j}
(resp.
\partial\varphi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}^{-1}(\mathcal{R})
) and zero on the remaining parts of
\partial D_{z,j}
(resp.
\partial\varphi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}^{-1}(\mathcal{R})
). In particular, the boundary values of
\mathfrak{g}_{z,j}^{0}
and
\mathfrak{g}_{z,j}^{1}
are in
[-\lambda,\lambda]
. Note that

\mu:=\mathbf{E}[\mathfrak{h}_{z,j}^{1}(\varphi^{-1}_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}(v))\,|\,\mathcal{F}_{z,j}^{-}]=\mathfrak{g}_{z,j}^{0}(\varphi^{-1}_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}(v))-\mathfrak{g}_{z,j}^{1}(\varphi^{-1}_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}(v)).

We also have that

\sigma^{2}:=\mathrm{var}(\mathfrak{h}_{z,j}^{1}(\varphi^{-1}_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}(v))\,|\,\mathcal{F}_{z,j}^{-})=\log({\rm CR}(\varphi^{-1}_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}(v),D_{z,j}))-\log({\rm CR}(\varphi^{-1}_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}(v),\varphi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}^{-1}(\mathcal{R}))).

By the Koebe-1/4 theorem,
{\rm CR}(\varphi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}^{-1}(v),D_{z,j})\asymp\mathrm{dist}(\varphi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}^{-1}(v),\partial D_{z,j})
and
{\rm CR}(\varphi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}^{-1}(v),\varphi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}^{-1}(\mathcal{R}))\asymp\mathrm{dist}(\varphi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}^{-1}(v),\partial\varphi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}^{-1}(\mathcal{R}))
, where the implicit constants are universal. Moreover, we claim that

\mathrm{dist}(\varphi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}^{-1}(v),\partial D_{z,j})\asymp\mathrm{dist}(\varphi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}^{-1}(v),\partial\varphi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}^{-1}(\mathcal{R})),

where the implicit constants depend only on
\xi
and
H_{0}
. Indeed, first we note that there exists
q\in(0,1)
depending only on
\xi
and
H_{0}
such that if we start a Brownian motion from
v
, then with probability at least
q
it exits
\mathcal{R}
either in the left or the right side of
\mathcal{R}
. Conformal invariance implies that if we start a Brownian motion from
\varphi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}^{-1}(v)
, then with probability at least
q
it exits
\varphi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}^{-1}(\mathcal{R})
in
\eta([0,\tau_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}])
. Thus the Beurling estimate implies that
\mathrm{dist}(\varphi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}^{-1}(v),\eta([0,\tau_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}]))\lesssim\mathrm{dist}(\varphi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}^{-1}(v),\partial\varphi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}^{-1}(\mathcal{R}))
. Also note there exists a constant
c_{2}\geq 1
so that
\mathrm{dist}(\varphi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}^{-1}(v),A_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j-1}})\geq c_{2}e^{-4{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}\geq\mathrm{dist}(\varphi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}^{-1}(v),\eta([0,\tau_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}]))
and so

\mathrm{dist}(\varphi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}^{-1}(v),\partial D_{z,j})=\mathrm{dist}(\varphi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}^{-1}(v),\eta([0,\tau_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}]))\lesssim\mathrm{dist}(\varphi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}^{-1}(v),\partial\varphi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}^{-1}(\mathcal{R}))
. On the other hand, we have that
\mathrm{dist}(\varphi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}^{-1}(v),\partial\varphi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}^{-1}(\mathcal{R}))\leq\mathrm{dist}(\varphi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}^{-1}(v),\eta([0,\tau_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}]))=\mathrm{dist}(\varphi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}^{-1}(v),\partial D_{z,j})
and so this proves the claim. It follows that
\mu=O(1)
and
\sigma^{2}=O(1)
where the implicit constants depend only on
H_{0}
and
\xi
. We thus have for
a>0
sufficiently small (depending only on
H_{0}
and
\xi
) that
\mathbf{E}[\exp(aI^{2})\,|\,\mathcal{F}_{z,j}^{-}]=O(1)
. Therefore arguing as in the proof of Lemma
6.2
and observing that
\|\phi\|_{\nabla}\lesssim 1
,
\|\phi\|_{\infty}\lesssim 1
with the implicit constants depending only on
H_{0}
and
\xi
, we obtain (possibly decreasing
a>0
) that

(6.3)

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}[\exp(a\|\phi(\mathfrak{h}_{z,j}^{1}\circ\varphi^{-1}_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}})\|^{2}_{\nabla})\,|\,\mathcal{F}_{z,j}^{-}]=O(1).

Furthermore, arguing as in the proof of Lemma
6.2
gives that
\mathfrak{h}_{z,j}^{0}\circ\varphi^{-1}_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}
extends to a function
\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}
which is harmonic in
(-1,2)\times(0,H_{0})
and such that
\sup_{w\in(-1,2)\times(0,H_{0})}|\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}(w)|\lesssim 1
where the implicit constant is universal since
\mathfrak{h}_{z,j}^{0}+\mathfrak{g}_{z,j}^{0}
has the same boundary values as
h
on
\partial D_{z,j}
. It follows that
\|\phi(\mathfrak{h}_{z,j}^{0}\circ\varphi^{-1}_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}})\|_{\nabla}\lesssim 1
where the implicit constant depends only on
H_{0}
and
\xi
. The proof is then complete by combining with (
6.3
) and the conformal invariance of
(\cdot,\cdot)_{\nabla}
.
∎

6.3.
Crossing bounds

Lemma 6.4
.

For each
H_{0}>0
and
\xi\in(0,H_{0}/10)
there exist constants
c_{0},M_{0}>0
so that the following is true. On
\tau_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}<\infty
, let
u^{*}
be the maximum height of a crossing discovered by the exploration in
A_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}^{*}
, otherwise let
u^{*}=0
. Then

\mathbf{P}[u^{*}\geq M\,|\,\mathcal{F}_{z,\tau_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}}]{\bf 1}_{\tau_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}<\infty}\leq\exp(-c_{0}M^{1/3}\log(M))\quad\text{for all}\quad M\geq M_{0}.

Proof.

This follows by combining Lemma
5.1
with Lemma
6.3
.
∎

Lemma 6.5
.

Suppose that
H_{0}>0
and
\xi\in(0,H_{0}/10)
. On
\tau_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}<\infty
, it is a.s. the case that the exploration in
A_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}
discovers only finitely many crossings.

Proof.

This follows by combining Lemma
5.6
with Lemma
6.3
.
∎

7.
{\rm SLE}_{4}
satisfies the hypotheses of the removability theorem

The purpose of this section is to complete the proof of Theorem
1.1
by showing that
{\rm SLE}_{4}
satisfies the hypotheses of Theorem
8.1
. Throughout, we will assume that we have the same setup and use the same notation as in Sections
6.1
and
6.2
.

Figure 6.
Illustration of the setup for the proof of Theorem
1.1
in the setting of the rectangle. Shown is the rectangle
\mathcal{R}_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}=(0,1)\times(0,H_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}})
and the explorations of
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{D}}
and
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{U}}
. The crossings with height zero are shown in blue and the other crossings are shown in orange. The continuations of these zero height crossings into
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{I}}
are shown in dark blue and the ones which connect
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{D}}
to
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{U}}
are
\widetilde{\eta}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{\eta}_{n}
(with
n=4
in the illustration); the left and right sides of
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{I}}
are
\widetilde{\eta}_{0}
and
\widetilde{\eta}_{n+1}
. Those zero height crossings which do not cross
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{I}}
are unlabeled. The conditional law of each
\widetilde{\eta}_{i}
given all of the other paths is absolutely continuous with respect to the law of an
{\rm SLE}_{4}
curve. As in the case of Figure
5
, the explorations discover other strands which are not crossings, but only crossings are shown.

7.1.
Setup and main statement

See Figure
6
for an illustration of the setup and the notation. Suppose
j\in\mathbf{N}
and
z\in\mathbf{H}
are such that
\tau_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}<\infty
. Let
\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{q}
for
i=1,\dots,n_{q}
and
q\in\{\mathrm{D},\mathrm{U}\}
be the height-
0
crossings of
\mathcal{R}^{q}
which are discovered by the exploration and write
\widetilde{\eta}_{0}^{q}
(resp.
\widetilde{\eta}_{n_{q}+1}^{q}
) for the left (resp. right) side of
\mathcal{R}^{q}
. Each of these crossings will continue into
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{I}}
and either form a crossing of
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{I}}
or make an excursion into
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{I}}
from
\mathcal{R}^{q}
back to itself. We let
\widetilde{\eta}_{1},\dots,\widetilde{\eta}_{n}
be the aforementioned crossings of
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{I}}
, ordered from left to right,
\widetilde{\eta}_{n+2},\dots,\widetilde{\eta}_{n+m}
be the height-
0
level line excursions into
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{I}}
from either
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{D}}
or
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{U}}
back to itself, and let
\widetilde{\eta}_{0}
(resp.
\widetilde{\eta}_{n+1}
) be the left (resp. right) side of
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{I}}
. For each
0\leq i\leq n+1
we let
\eta_{i}=\varphi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}^{-1}(\widetilde{\eta}_{i})
. We let
\widetilde{K}
be the union of the exploration in
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{D}}
and
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{U}}
. For each
1\leq i\leq n+1
we let
\widetilde{U}_{i}
be the component of
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{in}}\setminus(\widetilde{K}\cup(\cup_{\ell=1}^{n+m}\widetilde{\eta}_{\ell}))
with
\widetilde{\eta}_{i-1}
and
\widetilde{\eta}_{i}
on its boundary (with the convention that
\widetilde{U}_{n+1+i}=\widetilde{U}_{i}
for each
i
),
U_{i}=\varphi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}^{-1}(\widetilde{U}_{i})
, and let
\mathcal{W}_{i}
be a Whitney square decomposition of
U_{i}
. We also let
\widetilde{U}
be the component of the complement in
\mathcal{R}
of the explorations in
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{D}}
,
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{U}}
which contains
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{I}}
. We say
Q\in\mathcal{W}_{i}
is
(M,a)
-good with respect to
Q_{i}\in\mathcal{W}_{i}
if
\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{U_{i}}(\mathrm{cen}(Q),\mathrm{cen}(Q_{i}))\leq M({\mathrm{len}}(Q)/\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}))^{-a}
where
{\mathrm{len}}(Q)
is the side length of
Q
.

Definition 7.1
.

Fix
M,a>0
. We say that
A_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}
is
(M,a)
-good if either
{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}=\infty
or
{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}<\infty
,
n\leq M
, and the following are true. For each
0\leq i\leq n
there is a segment
\eta_{i}(I_{i})
of
\eta_{i}
such that

(1)

\mu_{\eta_{i}}^{\mathrm{nat}}(\eta_{i}(I_{i}))\geq M^{-1}\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}})^{3/2}
;

(2)

\mu_{\eta_{i}}^{\mathrm{nat}}(\eta_{i}(I_{i})\cap Y)\leq M\mathrm{diam}(Y)^{3/2-a}
for every
Y
Borel;

(3)

There exists a square
Q_{i}\in\mathcal{W}_{i}
such that if
G_{i}^{-}
(resp.
G_{i}^{+}
) is the set of points
w\in\eta_{i-1}(I_{i-1})
(resp.
w\in\eta_{i}(I_{i})
) such that each square
Q\in\mathcal{W}_{i}
which is hit by the hyperbolic geodesic in
U_{i}
from
\mathrm{cen}(Q_{i})
to
w
is
(M,a)
-good with respect to
Q_{i}
, then

(a)

\mu_{\eta_{i}}^{\mathrm{nat}}(G_{i}^{-})/\mu_{\eta_{i}}^{\mathrm{nat}}(\eta(I_{i}))\geq 3/4
and
\mu_{\eta_{i}}^{\mathrm{nat}}(G_{i}^{+})/\mu_{\eta_{i}}^{\mathrm{nat}}(\eta(I_{i+1}))\geq 3/4
.

(b)

The number of elements of
\mathcal{W}_{i}
with side length
2^{-m}
which are hit by a hyperbolic geodesic in
U_{i}
from
\mathrm{cen}(Q_{i})
to a point in
G_{i}^{+}
(resp.
G_{i}^{-}
) is at most
M(2^{m}\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}))^{3/2+a}
.

Let
E_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}^{M,a}
be the event that
A_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}
is
(M,a)
-good.

Proposition 7.2
.

Suppose that
\eta
is an
{\rm SLE}_{4}
in
\mathbf{H}
from
0
to
\infty
and let
K\subseteq\mathbf{H}
be compact. For every
a\in(0,1)
there a.s. exists
n_{0}\in\mathbf{N}
and
M>1
so that for every
n\geq n_{0}
and
z\in(e^{-5n}\mathbf{Z}^{2})\cap K
there exists
m\geq n_{0}
with
(1-a^{2})n\leq m\leq n
so that
E_{z,m}^{M,a}
occurs.

In order to start to prove Proposition
7.2
, we will first in Section
7.2
work in the rectangle and analyze the crossings in this setting. We will then combine these results with our other estimates in Section
7.3
to complete the proof.

7.2.
Regularity statements in the rectangle

We begin by formulating a version of the conditions from Definition
7.1
for the crossings of the rectangle in place of the annulus. We will then show that each of these conditions is likely to occur provided we adjust the parameters appropriately. Afterwards, we will show that these conditions imply the corresponding conditions from Definition
7.1
(with the exception of the first crossing, which was handled earlier in Section
4
) provided the crossings of the rectangle are separated from each other and the boundary.

Fix
a,H_{0}>0
,
H\geq H_{0}
, and
\xi\in(0,H_{0}/10)
. Let
\mathcal{R}=(0,1)\times(0,H)
and let
\mathbf{P}_{H}
be the law of a GFF on
\mathcal{R}
with boundary conditions given by
\lambda
on
\partial^{\mathrm{L}}\mathcal{R}
,
-\lambda
on
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}
, and
0
on
\partial^{\mathrm{D}}\mathcal{R}
and
\partial^{\mathrm{U}}\mathcal{R}
. We perform the exploration in
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{D}}
,
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{U}}
as in Section
6
and let
\widetilde{U}
,
\widetilde{U}_{i}
, and
\widetilde{\eta}_{i}
be as in Section
7.1
. In particular,
\widetilde{\eta}_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq n
are the continuations of height-
0
crossings of the
\mathcal{R}^{q}
for
q\in\{\mathrm{D},\mathrm{U}\}
which cross
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{I}}
. We also let
\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{i}
be a Whitney square decomposition of
\widetilde{U}_{i}
for each
1\leq i\leq n+1
. Suppose that we have fixed
H\geq H_{0}
and
\xi\in(0,H_{0}/10)
. For each
1\leq i\leq n
, we let
\widetilde{x}_{i}
(resp.
\widetilde{y}_{i}
) be the starting (resp. ending) point of
\widetilde{\eta}_{i}
and we let
\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}=\sup\{t\geq 0:\widetilde{\eta}_{i}(t)\in B(\widetilde{x}_{i},\xi)\}
,
\widetilde{\tau}_{i}=\inf\{t\geq 0:\widetilde{\eta}_{i}(t)\in B(\widetilde{y}_{i},\xi)\}
, and
\widetilde{I}_{i}=[\widetilde{\sigma}_{i},\widetilde{\tau}_{i}]
. We will now argue that by adjusting the parameters it is likely that
n\leq M
and for each
1\leq i\leq n
each of the following conditions hold.

(I)

\mu_{\widetilde{\eta}_{i}}^{\mathrm{nat}}(\widetilde{\eta}_{i}(\widetilde{I}_{i}))\geq M^{-1}
;

(II)

\mu_{\widetilde{\eta}_{i}}^{\mathrm{nat}}(\widetilde{\eta}_{i}(\widetilde{I}_{i})\cap Y)\leq M\mathrm{diam}(Y)^{3/2-a}
for every
Y
Borel;

(III)

There exists a square
\widetilde{Q}_{i}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{i}
such that if
\widetilde{G}_{i}^{-}
(resp.
\widetilde{G}_{i}^{+}
) is the set of points on
w\in\widetilde{\eta}_{i-1}(\widetilde{I}_{i-1})
(resp.
w\in\widetilde{\eta}_{i}(\widetilde{I}_{i})
) such that each square
Q\in\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{i}
which is intersected by the hyperbolic geodesic from
\mathrm{cen}(\widetilde{Q}_{i})
to
w
is
(M,a)
-good with respect to
\widetilde{Q}_{i}
, then

(a)

\mu_{\widetilde{\eta}_{i-1}}^{\mathrm{nat}}(\widetilde{G}_{i}^{-})/\mu_{\widetilde{\eta}_{i-1}}^{\mathrm{nat}}(\widetilde{\eta}_{i-1}(\widetilde{I}_{i-1}))\geq 3/4
(if
i\geq 2
) and
\mu_{\widetilde{\eta}_{i}}^{\mathrm{nat}}(\widetilde{G}_{i}^{+})/\mu_{\widetilde{\eta}_{i}}^{\mathrm{nat}}(\widetilde{\eta}_{i}(\widetilde{I}_{i}))\geq 3/4
(if
i\leq n
).

(b)

The number of elements of
\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{i}
with side length
2^{-m}
which are hit by a hyperbolic geodesic in
\widetilde{U}_{i}
from
\mathrm{cen}(\widetilde{Q}_{i})
to a point in
\widetilde{G}_{i}^{+}
(if
i\leq n
) (resp.
\widetilde{G}_{i}^{-}
(if
i\geq 2
)) is at most
M2^{(3/2+a)m}
.

We begin by showing that the crossings are likely to be separated from each other.

Lemma 7.3
.

For each
H\geq H_{0}
and
\xi\in(0,H_{0}/10)
we have that

(7.1)

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}_{H}\!\left[\min_{i\neq\ell}\mathrm{dist}(\widetilde{\eta}_{i},\widetilde{\eta}_{\ell})\leq\epsilon\right]\to 0\quad\text{as}\quad\epsilon\to 0.

Proof.

Almost surely, these crossings will not intersect each other or hit
\partial^{\mathrm{L}}\mathcal{R}\cup\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}
. Consequently,
\min_{i\neq\ell}\mathrm{dist}(\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{q},\widetilde{\eta}_{\ell}^{q})
for
q\in\{\mathrm{D},\mathrm{U}\}
is an a.s. positive random variable, which immediately implies (
7.1
).
∎

In the following, we will first argue that (
I
) and (
II
) are likely to hold (Lemma
7.4
), then that (
IIIa
) is likely to hold (Lemma
7.5
), and finally that (
IIIb
) is likely to hold (Lemma
7.7
).

Lemma 7.4
.

Fix
p_{0}\in(0,1)
,
a,H_{0}>0
,
H\geq H_{0}
, and
\xi\in(0,H_{0}/10)
. Then there exists
M_{0}>1
depending only on
p_{0}
,
a
,
H
,
\xi
such that for every
M\geq M_{0}
both (
I
) and (
II
) hold with probability at least
1-p_{0}
under
\mathbf{P}_{H}
.

Proof.

Let
h\sim\mathbf{P}_{H}
and fix
N\in\mathbf{N}
. Assume that we have carried out the explorations in
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{U}}
and
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{D}}
, let
\widetilde{K}
be their union, let
\widetilde{U}
be the connected component of
\mathcal{R}\setminus\widetilde{K}
which contains
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{I}}
, and let
\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}
be a Whitney square decomposition of
\widetilde{U}
. Let
F_{1}
be the event that the number of crossings discovered by the exploration in
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{D}}
and
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{U}}
is at most
N
, the height of any such crossing is in absolute value at most
N
, and the distances between different crossings are at least
1/N
. Lemmas
5.6
,
6.3
, and
7.3
imply that we can choose
N
sufficiently large (depending only on
p_{0}
,
a
,
H
,
\xi
) so that
\mathbf{P}_{H}[F_{1}]\geq 1-p_{0}/100
.

Let
\widetilde{\eta}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{\eta}_{n}
be the continuations of the
0
-height crossings of
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{D}}
into
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{I}}
which connect with a
0
-height crossing of
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{U}}
and let
\widetilde{\eta}_{n+1},\ldots,\widetilde{\eta}_{n+m}
be the remainder of the
0
-height crossings of
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{D}}
or
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{U}}
which do not cross
\widetilde{U}
. For each
1\leq i\leq n
, we let
\widetilde{K}_{i}
be the union of
\widetilde{K}
and
\widetilde{\eta}_{\ell}
for
\ell\neq i
and we let
\widetilde{W}_{i}
be the component of
\mathcal{R}\setminus\widetilde{K}_{i}
which contains
\widetilde{\eta}_{i}
. Then the conditional law of
\widetilde{\eta}_{i}
given everything else is absolutely continuous with respect to that of a chordal
{\rm SLE}_{4}
in
\widetilde{W}_{i}
from
\widetilde{x}_{i}
to
\widetilde{y}_{i}
. Let
\widetilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{i}
be the Radon-Nikodym derivative between the conditional law of
\widetilde{\eta}_{i}
given everything else and that of a chordal
{\rm SLE}_{4}
in
\widetilde{W}_{i}
from
\widetilde{x}_{i}
to
\widetilde{y}_{i}
. We note that
\widetilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{i}
is an a.s. finite random variable. Let
F_{2}
be the event that
F_{1}
holds and
\widetilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{i}\leq N
for each
1\leq i\leq n
. Lemma
6.3
implies that we can take
N
sufficiently large (depending only on
p_{0}
,
a
,
H
,
\xi
) so that
\mathbf{P}_{H}[F_{2}]\geq 1-p_{0}/50
.

For each
1\leq i\leq n
we let
\widetilde{\phi}_{i}
be the unique conformal transformation
\mathbf{H}\to\widetilde{W}_{i}
which takes
0
to
\widetilde{x}_{i}
,
-1
to the leftmost point in
\partial\widetilde{W}_{i}
contained in the line
\{x+iH_{0}/2:x\in\mathbf{R}\}
, and
\infty
to
\widetilde{y}_{i}
and let
\widehat{\eta}_{i}=\widetilde{\phi}_{i}^{-1}(\widetilde{\eta}_{i})
. On
F_{2}
, we have that
\widehat{\eta}_{i}
is absolutely continuous with respect to an
{\rm SLE}_{4}
in
\mathbf{H}
from
0
to
\infty
with a Radon-Nikodym derivative which is bounded by
N
.

Fix a compact set
K\subseteq\mathbf{H}
. Let
F_{3}
be the event that
F_{2}
holds and
\widetilde{\phi}_{i}^{-1}(\widetilde{\eta}_{i}([\widetilde{\sigma}_{i},\widetilde{\tau}_{i}]))\subseteq K
for each
1\leq i\leq n
. We note that we can choose
K
to be sufficiently large so that
\mathbf{P}_{H}[F_{3}]\geq 1-p_{0}/25
. By Lemmas
3.2
and
6.3
there exists a constant
c_{0}>0
(depending only on
K
,
N
,
H
, and
\xi
) so that if
F_{4}
is the event that
F_{3}
holds and for every Borel set
Y\subseteq K
we have that
\mu_{\widehat{\eta}_{i}}^{\mathrm{nat}}(Y)\leq c_{0}\mathrm{diam}(Y)^{3/2-a}
then
\mathbf{P}[F_{4}]\geq 1-p_{0}/10
.

Note that

\mu_{\widetilde{\eta}_{i}}^{\mathrm{nat}}(Y)=\int_{\widetilde{\phi}_{i}^{-1}(Y)}|\widetilde{\phi}_{i}^{\prime}(w)|^{3/2}d\mu_{\widehat{\eta}_{i}}^{\mathrm{nat}}(w)\quad\text{for all}\quad Y\quad\text{Borel}.

Therefore applying
[
26
, Corollary 3.25]
we obtain that there exists
M_{0}>1
so that if
M\geq M_{0}
then (
II
) holds on
F_{4}
. If we let
F_{5}
be the event that
F_{4}
holds and (
I
) also holds then by increasing the value of
M_{0}
if necessary we have that
\mathbf{P}_{H}[F_{5}]\geq 1-p_{0}
, which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 7.5
.

Fix
p_{0}\in(0,1)
,
a>0
,
H_{0}>0
,
H\geq H_{0}
, and
\xi\in(0,H_{0}/10)
. Then there exists
M_{0}>1
depending only on
p_{0}
,
a
,
H
,
\xi
such that (
I
), (
II
), and (
IIIa
) hold for every
M\geq M_{0}
with probability at least
1-p_{0}
with respect to
\mathbf{P}_{H}
.

Before we proceed to the proof of Lemma
7.5
, we first record the following analog of Lemma
3.1
which is stated in the context of chordal (rather than two-sided whole-plane)
{\rm SLE}_{4}
.

Lemma 7.6
.

Suppose that
D\subseteq\mathbf{C}
is a simply connected domain,
x,y\in\partial D
are distinct, and
\eta
is an
{\rm SLE}_{4}
in
D
from
x
to
y
. Let
\sigma,\tau
be stopping times for
\eta
such that a.s.
0<\sigma<\tau
. For each
p_{0},\mathfrak{f}_{0}\in(0,1)
,
a>0
, there exists
M>1
so that the following is true. Let
D_{\mathrm{L}}
(resp.
D_{\mathrm{R}}
) be the component of
D\setminus\eta
which is to the left (resp. right) of
\eta
and let
\mathcal{W}^{\mathrm{L}}
(resp.
\mathcal{W}^{\mathrm{R}}
) be a Whitney square decomposition of
D_{\mathrm{L}}
(resp.
D_{\mathrm{R}}
). There exist squares
Q_{q}\in\mathcal{W}^{q}
for
q\in\{\mathrm{L},\mathrm{R}\}
so that the following is true. For
q\in\{\mathrm{L},\mathrm{R}\}
, let
G_{q,\sigma,\tau}
be the set of
r\in[\sigma,\tau]
such that every
Q\in\mathcal{W}^{q}
hit by the hyperbolic geodesic from
\mathrm{cen}(Q_{q})
to
\eta(r)
in
D_{q}
satisfies
\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{D_{q}}(\mathrm{cen}(Q_{q}),\mathrm{cen}(Q))\leq M{\mathrm{len}}(Q)^{-a}
. Then

\mathbf{P}[\mu_{\eta}^{\mathrm{nat}}(\eta(G_{q,\sigma,\tau}))\leq\mathfrak{f}_{0}\mu_{\eta}^{\mathrm{nat}}(\eta([\sigma,\tau]))]\leq p_{0}\quad\text{for}\quad q\in\{\mathrm{L},\mathrm{R}\}.

Transferring the two-sided whole-plane
{\rm SLE}_{4}
result from Lemma
3.1
to the setting of the first crossing of an
{\rm SLE}_{4}
across an annulus was the focus of Lemma
4.5
. Since we included all of the details of the proof that in case, we will be more brief in the present setting.

Proof of Lemma
7.6
.

We fix
\mathfrak{f}_{1}\in(0,1)
sufficiently close to
1
(to be chosen). Consider the two-sided whole-plane
{\rm SLE}_{4}
process
\eta^{w}
of Lemma
3.1
which we assume to have the natural parameterization and with time normalized so that
\eta^{w}(0)=0
. We let
\mathbf{C}_{\mathrm{L}}
(resp.
\mathbf{C}_{\mathrm{R}}
) denote the component of
\mathbf{C}\setminus\eta^{w}
which is to the left (resp. right) of
\eta^{w}
. Moreover, we let
\mathcal{W}^{\mathbf{C}_{\mathrm{L}}}
(resp.
\mathcal{W}^{\mathbf{C}_{\mathrm{R}}}
) be a Whitney square decomposition of
\mathbf{C}_{\mathrm{L}}
(resp.
\mathbf{C}_{\mathrm{R}}
). Let
\mathcal{W}^{\mathrm{L}}
(resp.
\mathcal{W}^{\mathrm{R}}
) be a Whitney square decomposition of the component
D_{\mathrm{L}}
(resp.
D_{\mathrm{R}}
) of
D\setminus\eta
which is to the left (resp. right) of
\eta
.
Fix
z
in the counterclockwise arc of
\partial D
from
x
to
y
which is distinct from
x,y
. Let
\phi:\mathbf{C}\setminus\eta^{w}((-\infty,0])\to D
be such that
\phi(0)=x
,
\phi(\infty)=y
, and the prime end associated with
\eta^{w}(-1)
on the left side of
\eta^{w}
is mapped to
z
. Then
\eta=\phi(\eta^{w}(\cdot))|_{[0,\infty)}
is a chordal
{\rm SLE}_{4}
in
D
from
x
to
y
.
Thus we can find constants
0<t<T
and
c_{1}>0
such that with probability at least
1-p_{0}/100
we have that
t\leq\sigma\leq\tau\leq T
and
\mu_{\eta}^{\mathrm{nat}}(\eta([\sigma,\tau]))\geq c_{1}
.

Next we claim that there exist constants
\widetilde{M}>1
large enough and
\delta\in(0,1)
small enough such that with probability at least
1-p_{0}/100
for
q\in\{\mathrm{L},\mathrm{R}\}
there exist squares
Q^{q}\in\mathcal{W}^{\mathbf{C}_{q}}
with
\mathrm{dist}(Q^{q},\eta^{w})\geq\delta
,
\mathrm{dist}(Q^{q},\eta^{w})\geq\delta
and
Q^{q}\subseteq B(0,1/\delta)
such that if
G_{q}
is the set of
r\in[t,T]
such that the hyperbolic geodesic in
\mathbf{C}_{q}
from
\mathrm{cen}(Q^{q})
to
\eta^{w}(r)
only intersects squares in
\mathbf{C}_{q}
which are
(\widetilde{M},a)
-good with respect to
Q^{q}
, then
\mathcal{L}_{1}(G_{q})\geq\mathfrak{f}_{1}(T-t)
. Indeed, first we note that
\eta^{w}
is a.s. a simple curve and it does not hit fixed points. Thus there exist
0<\delta<\xi<1
small enough such that with probability at least
1-p_{0}/300
we have that there exists
z^{q}\in\xi\mathbf{Z}^{2}\cap B(0,1/\xi)
so that
z^{q}\in\mathbf{C}_{q}
and
\mathrm{dist}(Q^{q},\eta^{w})\geq\delta,Q^{q}\subseteq B(0,1/\delta)
, where
Q^{q}
is the square in
\mathcal{W}^{\mathbf{C}_{q}}
such that
z^{q}\in Q^{q}
for
q\in\{\mathrm{L},\mathrm{R}\}
. Moreover arguing as in the proof of Lemma
3.1
, we obtain that there exists
\widetilde{M}>1
large such that with probability at least
1-p_{0}/300
the following holds. For each
z\in\xi\mathbf{Z}^{2}\cap B(0,1/\xi)
the statement of Lemma
3.1
holds when
1
,
[0,1]
, and
1-\delta
are replaced by
z
,
[t,T]
, and
\mathfrak{f}_{1}
respectively. The claim then follows by taking the intersection of the above events. By possibly taking
\delta
to be smaller we can also assume that every hyperbolic geodesic in
\mathbf{C}_{q}
starting from
\mathrm{cen}(Q^{q})
and ending at some point on
\eta^{w}([t,T])
lies in
B(0,1/\delta)\cap\{w\in\mathbf{C}:\mathrm{dist}(w,\eta^{w}((-\infty,0]))\geq\delta\}
and
\eta^{w}([0,T])\subseteq B(0,1/\xi)
. Let
F
be the intersection of the above events and note that
\mathbf{P}[F]\geq 1-p_{0}/100
. Moreover we can find a constant
b\in(0,1)
such that with probability at least
1-p_{0}/100
we have that
b\leq|\phi^{\prime}(w)|\leq b^{-1}
for each
w\in B(0,1/\delta)
such that
\mathrm{dist}(w,\eta^{w}((-\infty,0]))\geq\delta
. Suppose that we are working on the event that the following hold.

(i)

t\leq\sigma\leq\tau\leq T
,
\mu_{\eta}^{\mathrm{nat}}(\eta([\sigma,\tau]))\geq c_{1}
,

(ii)

F
occurs,

(iii)

b\leq|\phi^{\prime}(w)|\leq b^{-1}
for each
w\in B(0,1/\delta)
such that
\mathrm{dist}(w,\eta^{w}((-\infty,0]))\geq\delta
.

Note that the above event has probability at least
1-p_{0}/25
. First, we observe that

\displaystyle\mu_{\eta}^{\mathrm{nat}}(\phi(\eta^{w}([t,T]\setminus G_{q})))

\displaystyle=\int_{\eta^{w}([t,T]\setminus G_{q})}|\phi^{\prime}(w)|^{3/2}d\mu_{\eta}^{\mathrm{nat}}(w)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(1-\mathfrak{f}_{1})(T-t)}{c_{1}b^{3/2}}\mu_{\eta}^{\mathrm{nat}}(\eta([\sigma,\tau]))\leq(1-\mathfrak{f}_{0})\mu_{\eta}^{\mathrm{nat}}(\eta([\sigma,\tau]))

if we choose
\mathfrak{f}_{1}
sufficiently close to
1
.

For
q\in\{\mathrm{L},\mathrm{R}\}
we set
z_{q}=\mathrm{cen}(Q^{q})
and let
\widetilde{Q}^{q}\in\mathcal{W}^{q}
be the Whitney square such that
\phi(z_{q})\in\mathrm{cl}(\widetilde{Q}^{q})\setminus(\partial^{\mathrm{U}}\widetilde{Q}^{q}\cup\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\widetilde{Q}^{q})
, where
\partial^{\mathrm{U}}\widetilde{Q}^{q}
and
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\widetilde{Q}^{q}
denotes the upper and right boundary of
\widetilde{Q}^{q}
, respectively. Suppose that
\widetilde{Q}\in\mathcal{W}^{q}
is such that
\phi^{-1}(\widetilde{Q})
is hit by a hyperbolic geodesic
\gamma_{z_{q},\eta^{w}(r)}^{\mathbf{C}_{q}},r\in[\sigma,\tau]
, which only intersects squares in
\mathcal{W}^{\mathbf{C}_{q}}
which are
(\widetilde{M},a)
-good with respect to
Q^{q}
. Let
Q\in\mathcal{W}^{\mathbf{C}_{q}}
be such that
\phi(\mathrm{cen}(Q))\in\widetilde{Q}
. By (
2.6
) we have that

(7.2)

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{D_{q}}(\phi(z_{q}),\mathrm{cen}(\widetilde{Q}))

\displaystyle\asymp\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{D_{q}}(\phi(z_{q}),\phi(\mathrm{cen}(Q)))=\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\mathbf{C}_{q}}(z_{q},\mathrm{cen}(Q))

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{M}{\mathrm{len}}(Q)^{-a}\asymp\widetilde{M}|\phi^{\prime}(\mathrm{cen}(Q))|^{a}{\mathrm{len}}(\widetilde{Q})^{-a}\lesssim\widetilde{M}{\mathrm{len}}(\widetilde{Q})^{-a}

Finally, since
\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{D_{q}}(z_{q},\mathrm{cen}(\widetilde{Q}^{q}))\leq 1
, by applying Lemma
A.6
as in the proofs of of Lemmas
4.12
and
4.13
, we obtain that (
7.2
) still holds if we replace
\phi(z_{q})
with
\mathrm{cen}(\widetilde{Q}^{q})
. This completes the proof.
∎

Proof of Lemma
7.5
.

Assume that we have carried out the explorations in
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{U}}
and
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{D}}
, let
\widetilde{K}
be their union, let
\widetilde{U}
be the connected component of
\mathcal{R}\setminus\widetilde{K}
which contains
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{I}}
, and let
\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}
be a Whitney square decomposition of
\widetilde{U}
. Choose
N
large so that with probability at least
1-p_{0}/100
there are at most
N
crossings of either
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{D}}
or
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{U}}
. Let
\widetilde{\eta}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{\eta}_{n}
be the continuations of the
0
-height crossings of
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{D}}
into
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{I}}
which connect with a
0
-height crossing of
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{U}}
and let
\widetilde{\eta}_{n+1},\ldots,\widetilde{\eta}_{n+m}
be the remainder of the
0
-height crossings of
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{D}}
or
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{U}}
which do not cross
\widetilde{U}
. For each
1\leq i\leq n
, we let
\widetilde{K}_{i}
be the union of
\widetilde{K}
and
\widetilde{\eta}_{\ell}
for
\ell\neq i
and we let
\widetilde{W}_{i}
be the component of
\mathcal{R}\setminus\widetilde{K}_{i}
which contains
\widetilde{\eta}_{i}
. Then the conditional law of
\widetilde{\eta}_{i}
given everything else is absolutely continuous with respect to that of a chordal
{\rm SLE}_{4}
in
\widetilde{W}_{i}
. Let
\widetilde{U}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{U}_{n+1}
be the complementary components of
\widetilde{U}\setminus\cup_{i=1}^{n+m}\widetilde{\eta}_{i}
which have at least one of the
\widetilde{\eta}_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq n
on their boundary, ordered from left to right. Let
\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{i}
be a Whitney square decomposition of
\widetilde{U}_{i}
.

We assume that we have the setup of Lemma
7.4
and for each
1\leq i\leq n
we let
\widetilde{I}_{i}
be the segment as constructed in Lemma
7.4
. Lemma
7.6
implies that for each
a>0
there exists
M>1
large so that with probability at least
1-p_{0}/100
, for each
1\leq i\leq n
we can find squares
\widetilde{Q}_{i}^{\mathrm{L}}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{i}
and
\widetilde{Q}_{i}^{\mathrm{R}}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{i+1}
such that (
IIIa
) holds for the segment
\widetilde{\eta}_{i}(\widetilde{I}_{i})
and the domains
\widetilde{U}_{i}
and
\widetilde{U}_{i+1}
respectively. Let
\widetilde{G}_{\mathrm{L},i}
be the set of
r\in\widetilde{I}_{i}
such that every square in
Q\in\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{i}
which is hit by a hyperbolic geodesic from
\mathrm{cen}(\widetilde{Q}_{i}^{\mathrm{L}})
to
\widetilde{\eta}_{i}(r)
is
(M,a)
-good. Similarly, let
\widetilde{G}_{\mathrm{R},i}
be the set of
r\in\widetilde{I}_{i}
such that every square in
Q\in\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{i+1}
which is hit by a hyperbolic geodesic from
\mathrm{cen}(\widetilde{Q}_{i}^{\mathrm{R}})
to
\widetilde{\eta}_{i}(r)
is
(M,a)
-good. Then

\frac{\mu_{\widetilde{\eta}_{i}}^{\mathrm{nat}}(\widetilde{\eta}_{i}(\widetilde{G}_{\mathrm{L},i}))}{\mu_{\widetilde{\eta}_{i}}^{\mathrm{nat}}(\widetilde{\eta}_{i}(\widetilde{I}_{i}))}\geq\frac{3}{4}\quad\text{and}\quad\frac{\mu_{\widetilde{\eta}_{i}}^{\mathrm{nat}}(\widetilde{\eta}_{i}(\widetilde{G}_{\mathrm{R},i}))}{\mu_{\widetilde{\eta}_{i}}^{\mathrm{nat}}(\widetilde{\eta}_{i}(\widetilde{I}_{i}))}\geq\frac{3}{4}.

Moreover we can choose
M>1
large enough such that with probability at least
1-p_{0}/100
both (
I
) and (
II
) hold. Also, we can find
C>0
large such that with probability at least
1-p_{0}/100
we have that
\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\widetilde{U}_{i+1}}(\mathrm{cen}(\widetilde{Q}^{\mathrm{R}}_{i}),\mathrm{cen}(\widetilde{Q}^{\mathrm{L}}_{i+1}))\leq C
for each
1\leq i\leq n-1
. Fix
1\leq i\leq n-1
and
r\in\widetilde{G}_{\mathrm{R},i}
. Let
Q\in\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{i+1}
be such that
\gamma_{\widetilde{y}_{i},\widetilde{\eta}_{i}(r)}^{\widetilde{U}_{i+1}}\cap Q\neq\emptyset
where
\widetilde{y}_{i}=\mathrm{cen}(\widetilde{Q}^{\mathrm{L}}_{i+1})
. Then by applying Lemma
A.6
as in the proofs of of Lemmas
4.12
and
4.13
, we obtain that
\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\widetilde{U}_{i+1}}(\widetilde{y}_{i},\mathrm{cen}(Q))\lesssim M{\mathrm{len}}(Q)^{-a}
. This completes the proof of the lemma by considering the intersection of all of the above events.
∎

Finally, we shall see that we can choose the parameters so that with high probability, (
IIIb
) holds (in addition to (
I
), (
II
), and (
IIIa
)).

Lemma 7.7
.

Fix
p_{0}\in(0,1)
,
a,H_{0}>0
,
H\geq H_{0}
, and
\xi\in(0,H_{0}/10)
. There exists
M_{0}>0
depending only on
p_{0}
,
a
,
H_{0}
, and
\xi
such that with probability at least
1-p_{0}
with respect to
\mathbf{P}_{H}
we have that (
I
), (
II
), and (
III
) hold.

Proof.

Suppose that we have the setup and notation from the proof of Lemma
7.4
. We let
\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}^{0}=\sup\{t\geq 0:\widetilde{\eta}_{i}(t)\in B(\widetilde{x}_{i},\xi/2)\}
and
\widetilde{\tau}_{i}^{0}=\inf\{t\geq 0:\widetilde{\eta}_{i}(t)\in B(\widetilde{y}_{i},\xi/2)\}
and set
