\widetilde{I}^{0}_{i}=[\widetilde{\sigma}^{0}_{i},\widetilde{\tau}^{0}_{i}]
. Then
\widetilde{I}_{i}^{0}
contains
\widetilde{I}_{i}
. For each
\ell\in\mathbf{N}
we let
\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{i,\ell}
be the set of squares
\widetilde{Q}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{i}
such that
{\mathrm{len}}(\widetilde{Q})=2^{-\ell}
and
\mathrm{dist}(\widetilde{Q},\partial\widetilde{U}_{i})=\mathrm{dist}(\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{\eta}_{i}(\widetilde{I}^{0}_{i}))
.

Note that Lemmas
7.3
and
7.5
imply that we can choose
\widetilde{M}_{0}>1
,
N\in\mathbf{N}
, and
K\subseteq\mathbf{H}
compact depending only on
p_{0}
,
H
, and
\xi
such that with probability at least
1-p_{0}/100
we have that
n\leq N
,
\widetilde{\phi}^{-1}_{i}(\widetilde{\eta}_{i}(\widetilde{I}^{0}_{i}))\subseteq K
,
\mathrm{dist}(\widetilde{\eta}_{i}(\widetilde{I}^{0}_{i})
,
\partial\widetilde{W}_{i})\geq 1/N
for each
1\leq i\leq n
, and conditions (
I
), (
II
) and (
IIIa
) hold for the segments
\widetilde{\eta}_{i}(\widetilde{I}_{i})
. Let
F_{1}
be that event.

Fix
\widetilde{Q}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{i,\ell}
and let
w\in\widetilde{\eta}_{i}(\widetilde{I}^{0}_{i})
be such that
\mathrm{dist}(\widetilde{Q},\partial\widetilde{U}_{i})=\mathrm{dist}(\widetilde{Q},w)\lesssim 2^{-\ell}
. Note that we can find constants
c_{1},c_{2}>0
and
\ell_{0}\in\mathbf{N}
depending only on the above implicit constants such that
\widetilde{\phi}^{-1}_{i}(\widetilde{Q})\subseteq B(\widetilde{\phi}^{-1}_{i}(w),c_{1}2^{-\ell})
and
B(\widetilde{\phi}^{-1}_{i}(\mathrm{cen}(\widetilde{Q})),c_{2}2^{-\ell})\subseteq\widetilde{\phi}^{-1}_{i}(\widetilde{Q})
for each
\ell\geq\ell_{0}
. Thus by decreasing the value of
c_{2}
if necessary, we obtain that there exists
z\in c_{2}2^{-\ell}\mathbf{Z}^{2}
such that
z\in\widetilde{\phi}^{-1}_{i}(\widetilde{Q})
and
\mathrm{dist}(z,\widehat{\eta}_{i})\leq c_{1}2^{-\ell}
. Combining we obtain that
|\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{i,\ell}|\leq N_{i,\ell}
for each
\ell\geq\ell_{0}
, where
N_{i,\ell}
is the number of points
z\in c_{2}2^{-\ell}\mathbf{Z}^{2}\cap\mathbf{H}\cap B(0,2R)
such that
\widehat{\eta}_{i}\cap B(z,c_{1}2^{-\ell})\neq\emptyset
and
R>0
is chosen such that
K\subseteq B(0,R)
. Let
\gamma
be an
{\rm SLE}_{4}
in
\mathbf{H}
from
0
to
\infty
. Let
N_{m}
the number of points
z\in c_{2}2^{-m}\mathbf{Z}^{2}\cap\mathbf{H}\cap B(0,2R)
such that
\gamma\cap B(z,c_{1}2^{-m})\neq\emptyset
. Then
[
5
, Proposition 4]
implies that
\mathbf{E}[N_{m}]\lesssim 2^{3m/2}
and so
\mathbf{P}[N_{m}\geq 2^{(3/2+a)m}]\lesssim 2^{-am}
where the implicit constant depends only on
K
,
c_{1}
and
c_{2}
. This implies that
\mathbf{P}[N_{\ell}\leq 2^{(3/2+a)\ell}\ \forall\ell\geq m]\to 1
as
m\to\infty
. Note that the proof of Lemma
7.4
implies the
\mathbf{P}_{H}
-probability that the Radon-Nikodym derivative of the law of
\widehat{\eta}_{i}
with respect to that of
\gamma
is at most
N
tends to
1
as
N\to\infty
at rate which depends only on
H_{0}
and
\xi
. Thus we have that
\mathbf{P}_{H}[N_{i,\ell}\leq 2^{(3/2+a)\ell}\ \forall\ell\geq m]\to 1
as
m\to\infty
. Note also that
\max_{1\leq\ell\leq\ell_{0},1\leq i\leq n}|\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{i,\ell}|
is an a.s. finite random variable and so we can choose
M_{0}>1
large such that with probability at least
1-p_{0}/100
under
\mathbf{P}_{H}
we have that
|\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{i,\ell}|\leq M_{0}2^{(3/2+a)\ell}
for each
1\leq i\leq n
and each
\ell\in\mathbf{N}
. Let
F_{2}
be that event and note that
\mathbf{P}_{H}[F_{1}\cap F_{2}]\geq 1-p_{0}/50
. Suppose that we are working on
F_{1}\cap F_{2}
.

Now for each
1\leq i\leq n
, we let
\psi_{i}
be the unique conformal transformation mapping
\mathbf{D}
onto
\widetilde{U}_{i}
such that
\psi_{i}(0)=\mathrm{cen}(\widetilde{Q}_{i})
and
\psi^{\prime}_{i}(0)>0
. Furthermore, we let
\widetilde{\theta}_{i,0}\in[0,2\pi)
and
\widetilde{\theta}_{i,1}\in(\widetilde{\theta}_{i,0},\widetilde{\theta}_{i,0}+2\pi)
be such that let
\psi^{-1}_{i}(\widetilde{\eta}_{i}(\widetilde{I}_{i}))=\{e^{i\theta}:\theta\in[\widetilde{\theta}_{i,0},\widetilde{\theta}_{i,1}]\}
and
S_{i}=\psi_{i}(\{re^{i\theta}:r\in[0,1],\ \theta\in[\widetilde{\theta}_{i,0},\widetilde{\theta}_{i,1}]\})
. Since
\min_{1\leq i\leq n}\mathrm{dist}(S_{i},\partial\widetilde{U}_{i}\setminus\widetilde{\eta}_{i}(\widetilde{I}^{0}_{i}))>0
a.s., it follows that there exists
\epsilon>0
depending only on the above constants such that with probability at least
1-p_{0}/100
under
\mathbf{P}_{H}
we have that
\mathrm{dist}(S_{i},\partial\widetilde{U}_{i}\setminus\widetilde{\eta}_{i}(\widetilde{I}^{0}_{i}))\geq\epsilon
for each
1\leq i\leq n
. Let
F_{3}
be that event and suppose that we are working on
F_{1}\cap F_{2}\cap F_{3}
. Let
\widetilde{Q}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{i}
be such that
\mathrm{dist}(\widetilde{Q},\partial\widetilde{U}_{i})<\mathrm{dist}(\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{\eta}_{i}(\widetilde{I}^{0}_{i}))
and
\gamma_{\mathrm{cen}(\widetilde{Q}_{i}),w}^{\widetilde{U}_{i}}\cap\widetilde{Q}\neq\emptyset
for some
w\in\widetilde{\eta}_{i}(\widetilde{I}_{i})
. Then we have that
\mathrm{diam}(\widetilde{Q})\geq\epsilon/100
. Note that the number of
\widetilde{Q}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{i}
such that
\mathrm{diam}(\widetilde{Q})\geq\epsilon/100
is at most
O(\epsilon^{-2})
where the implicit constant depends only on
H_{0}
and
\xi
. Therefore, possibly by increasing the value of
M_{0}
, we obtain that (
IIIb
) holds if
F_{1}\cap F_{2}\cap F_{3}
occurs, which completes the proof.
∎

7.3.
Conclusion of the proof

Now we fix
p_{0}\in(0,1)
,
\delta_{0},H_{0}>0
,
a\in(0,1/2)
, and let
b\in(1,2)
,
\xi_{1}\in(0,(e^{-b}-e^{-2})/2)
, and
\xi_{0}\in(0,H_{0}/2)
be as in (
i
) of Lemma
4.3
. We then let
\delta_{1},\delta_{2}\in(0,1)
,
M_{0}>1
be as in Lemma
4.5
for the above choices of
p_{0},\delta_{0},a,b
and
\xi_{2}=3\xi_{1}/2
. We also let
\widetilde{\xi}_{0}\in(0,H_{0}/2)
be as in (
ii
) of Lemma
4.3
for
\widetilde{\xi}_{1}=\delta_{2}
. We fix
\xi\in(0,H_{0}/10)
such that
\xi<\xi_{0}/3
and perform the explorations in
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{U}}
and
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{D}}
and define
({{\mathbf{k}}}_{j})_{j\geq 1}
as in Section
6
.

Lemma 7.8
.

Suppose that we have the above setup. Then for all
\delta_{1}\in(0,1)
sufficiently small there exists
M>1
sufficiently large and
\delta_{2}\in(0,1)
sufficiently small such that

\mathbf{P}[(E_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}^{M,a})^{c}\,|\,\mathcal{F}_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}]{\bf 1}_{{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}<\infty}\leq p_{0}{\bf 1}_{{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}<\infty}\quad\text{a.s.}

Proof.

We are going to show that the conditions of Definition
7.1
are likely to hold by using that the conditions in the rectangle are likely to hold and the definition of
{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}
implies that on
{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}<\infty
the first crossing of
A_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}
by
\eta
is well-behaved. Let
\phi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}=\varphi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}^{-1}
so that
\phi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}
conformally maps
\mathcal{R}_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}
to
A_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}^{*}
with
\partial^{\mathrm{L}}\mathcal{R}_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}
taken to
\eta^{\mathrm{R}}([\sigma_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}},\tau_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}])
and
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}
taken to
\eta^{\mathrm{L}}([\sigma_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}},\tau_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}])
.

Step 1. First crossing.

First, by the definition of
{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}
on
{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}<\infty
we have that the events
F_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}^{1}
(Lemma
4.3
),
F_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}^{2}
(Lemma
4.4
), and
C_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}
(Lemma
4.5
) occur. This implies that the following is true.

There exists an interval
I_{0}\subseteq[\sigma_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}},\tau_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}]
and points
z_{\mathrm{L}},z_{\mathrm{R}}\in A^{*}_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}
such that
\mathrm{dist}(z_{\mathrm{L}},\eta^{\mathrm{L}}(I_{0}))\leq\delta_{1}\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}})
,
\mathrm{dist}(z_{\mathrm{R}},\eta^{\mathrm{R}}(I_{0}))\leq\delta_{1}\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}})
,
\mathrm{dist}(\{z_{\mathrm{L}},z_{\mathrm{R}}\},\partial A^{*}_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}})\geq\delta_{2}\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}})
and such that for every simply connected domain
W\subseteq A^{*}_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}
containing the component of
B(y,\delta_{1}^{1/4}\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}))\setminus\eta([0,\tau_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}])
which contains
z_{\mathrm{R}}
the following holds (and the same with
z_{\mathrm{L}}
in place of
z_{\mathrm{R}}
). Let
\mathcal{W}
be a Whitney square decomposition of
W
. Then the hyperbolic geodesic in
W
from
z_{\mathrm{R}}
(resp.
z_{\mathrm{L}}
) to
w\in\eta^{\mathrm{R}}(I_{0})
(resp.
w\in\eta^{\mathrm{L}}(I_{0})
) passes only through squares which are
(M,a)
-good with respect to
z_{\mathrm{R}}
(resp.
z_{\mathrm{L}}
) for at least
\frac{3}{4}
-fraction of points with respect to
\mu_{\eta}^{\mathrm{nat}}(dw)/\mu_{\eta}^{\mathrm{nat}}(I_{0})
. Moreover conditions (
1
) and (
2
) hold

Step 2a. Condition (
1
).
We note that since
\eta_{i}=\phi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}(\widetilde{\eta}_{i})
, we have that

(7.3)

\displaystyle\mu_{\eta_{i}}^{\mathrm{nat}}(Y)=\int_{\varphi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}(Y)}|\phi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}^{\prime}(w)|^{3/2}d\mu_{\widetilde{\eta}_{i}}^{\mathrm{nat}}(w)\quad\text{for all}\quad Y\quad\text{Borel}.

Moreover, we note that by choosing
\epsilon>0
small enough and
M>1
large enough, we have that with probability at least
1-p_{0}/100
, all of the crossings
\widetilde{\eta}_{i}
are separated from each other and from
\partial^{\mathrm{L}}\mathcal{R}_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}
and
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}
by at least
\epsilon
(Lemmas
6.3
and
7.3
) and
\mu_{\widetilde{\eta}_{i}}^{\mathrm{nat}}(\widetilde{\eta}_{i}(\widetilde{I}_{i}))\geq M^{-1}
for all
i
(Lemmas
6.3
and
7.4
). By the definition of
{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}
, we have that
\inf_{\sigma_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}\leq t\leq\tau_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}}S_{t}\geq\delta_{0}
. Assume that we are on this event.

Let
\mathcal{R}=(0,1)\times(0,H_{0})\subseteq\mathcal{R}_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}
. For each
\epsilon>0
, we let
\mathcal{R}^{\epsilon}
be the set of points in
\mathcal{R}
at distance at least
\epsilon/2
from
\partial\mathcal{R}
. Then a Brownian motion starting from
w\in\mathcal{R}^{\epsilon}
has probability bounded from below (depending only on
H_{0}
,
\epsilon
) of exiting
\mathcal{R}
in any of the four boundary arcs. Set
\widetilde{w}=\psi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}(\phi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}(w))\in B(0,e^{-1})\setminus\mathrm{cl}(B(0,e^{-2}))
(recall Section
4.1
). We claim that the probability that a Brownian motion starting from
\widetilde{w}
exits
B(0,e^{-1})\setminus\mathrm{cl}(B(0,e^{-2}))
in
\partial B(0,e^{-1})
(resp.
\partial B(0,e^{-2})
) is bounded from below by a positive constant which depends only on
H_{0}
,
\epsilon
, and
b
. Indeed, first we note that condition (
i
) of Lemma
4.3
implies that
(\varphi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}\circ\psi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}^{-1})(\partial B(0,e^{-b}-\xi_{1}))\subseteq\mathcal{R}
is a crossing from
\partial^{\mathrm{L}}\mathcal{R}
to
\partial^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{R}
and so the probability that a Brownian motion starting from
\widetilde{w}
hits
\partial B(0,e^{-b}-\xi_{1})
before exiting
B(0,e^{-1})\setminus\mathrm{cl}(B(0,e^{-2}))
is bounded from below by a positive constant depending only on
H_{0}
and
\epsilon
. The claim then follows by combining with the Markov property and the fact that for any point on
\partial B(0,e^{-b}-\xi_{1})
the probability that a Brownian motion starting from that point exits
B(0,e^{-1})\setminus\mathrm{cl}(B(0,e^{-2}))
on
\partial B(0,e^{-1})
(resp.
\partial B(0,e^{-2})
) is bounded from below by a positive constant depending only on
b
and
\xi_{1}
which is uniform in the location of the starting point. Thus we can find
q\in(0,(e^{-1}-e^{-2})/2)
depending only on
H_{0}
,
\epsilon
,
b
and
\xi_{1}
such that
\widetilde{w}\in B(0,e^{-1}-q)\setminus\mathrm{cl}(B(0,e^{-2}+q))
. Moreover we have that
|(\psi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}^{-1})^{\prime}(\widetilde{w})|\asymp\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}})
where the implicit constants are universal. Also, the Koebe-
\tfrac{1}{4}
theorem combined with the Beurling’s estimate imply that
|(\psi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}^{-1})^{\prime}(\widetilde{w})|\asymp\mathrm{dist}(\phi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}(w),\partial A^{*}_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}})
and so
\mathrm{dist}(\phi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}(w),\partial A^{*}_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}})\asymp\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}})
. It follows that

(7.4)

|\phi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}^{\prime}(w)|\asymp\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}})\quad\text{for all}\quad w\in\mathcal{R}^{\epsilon}

where the implicit constants depend only on
H_{0}
,
\epsilon
,
b
and
\xi_{1}
. Consequently, since
\widetilde{\eta}_{i}\subseteq\mathcal{R}^{\epsilon}
, it follows from (
7.3
), (
7.4
) that
\mu_{\eta_{i}}^{\mathrm{nat}}(\eta_{i}(I_{i}))\gtrsim M^{-1}\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}})^{3/2}
where the implicit constant depends only on
H_{0}
,
\epsilon
,
b
and
\xi_{1}
and
\eta_{i}(I_{i})=\phi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}(\widetilde{\eta}_{i}(\widetilde{I}_{i}))
.

Step 2b. Condition (
2
).
By Lemmas
6.3
, and
7.4
we can assume that in addition to the conditions of Step 2a we have that (
II
) also holds with probability at least
1-p_{0}/100
. Suppose that
Y\subseteq A_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}
is a Borel set such that
\varphi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}(Y)\subseteq\mathcal{R}^{\epsilon}
. Note by (
7.4
) that
\mathrm{diam}(\varphi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}(Y))\asymp\mathrm{diam}(Y)/\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}})
where the implicit constants depend only on
H_{0}
,
\epsilon
,
b
and
\xi_{1}
. By (
7.3
), (
7.4
), it follows that

\displaystyle\mu_{\eta_{i}}^{\mathrm{nat}}(\eta_{i}(I_{i})\cap Y)

\displaystyle=\int_{\widetilde{\eta}_{i}(\widetilde{I}_{i})\cap\varphi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}(Y)}|\phi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}^{\prime}(w)|^{3/2}d\mu_{\widetilde{\eta}_{i}}^{\mathrm{nat}}(w)

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}})^{3/2}\mu_{\widetilde{\eta}_{i}}^{\mathrm{nat}}(\widetilde{\eta}_{i}(\widetilde{I}_{i})\cap\varphi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}(Y))

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}})^{3/2-a}M\mathrm{diam}(\varphi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}(Y))^{3/2-a}\lesssim M\mathrm{diam}(Y)^{3/2-a}

where the implicit constants depend only on
H_{0}
,
\epsilon
,
b
and
\xi_{1}
.

Step 2c. Condition (
3a
).
We will first handle the squares in the domains
U_{2},\dots,U_{n}
, that is, the domains which do not have
\eta([\sigma_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}},\tau_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}])
as part of their boundaries. Combining Lemmas
6.3
and
7.7
, we obtain that with probability at least
1-p_{0}/100
condition (
IIIa
) holds for
\widetilde{U}_{i}
for each
2\leq i\leq n
for some large constant
\widetilde{M}>1
and with
3/4
replaced by any fixed
\mathfrak{f}\in(0,1)
which depends on the aforementioned constants except for
\widetilde{M}
. Let
G_{i}^{-}=\phi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}(\widetilde{G}_{i}^{-})
and
G_{i}^{+}=\phi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}(\widetilde{G}_{i}^{+})
. If we choose
\mathfrak{f}\in(0,1)
sufficiently close to
1
we have that

\displaystyle\frac{\mu_{\eta_{i}}^{\mathrm{nat}}(\eta_{i}(I_{i})\setminus G_{i}^{-})}{\mu_{\eta_{i}}^{\mathrm{nat}}(\eta_{i}(I_{i}))}=\frac{\int_{\widetilde{\eta}_{i}(\widetilde{I}_{i})\setminus\widetilde{G}_{i}^{-}}|\phi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}^{\prime}(w)|^{3/2}d\mu_{\widetilde{\eta}_{i}}^{\mathrm{nat}}(w)}{\int_{\widetilde{\eta}_{i}(\widetilde{I}_{i})}|\phi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}^{\prime}(w)|^{3/2}d\mu_{\widetilde{\eta}_{i}}^{\mathrm{nat}}(w)}\lesssim\frac{\mu_{\widetilde{\eta}_{i}}^{\mathrm{nat}}(\widetilde{\eta}_{i}(\widetilde{I}_{i})\setminus\widetilde{G}_{i}^{-})}{\mu_{\widetilde{\eta}_{i}}^{\mathrm{nat}}(\widetilde{\eta}_{i}(\widetilde{I}_{i}))}\lesssim 1-\mathfrak{f}<\frac{1}{4}

and similarly for
G_{i}^{+}
. We claim that condition (
3a
) holds for
U_{i}
for each
2\leq i\leq n
, by possibly taking
\widetilde{M}
to be larger. Indeed, we fix
w\in G_{i}^{-}
and set
\widetilde{z}_{i}=\mathrm{cen}(\widetilde{Q}_{i})
. Let
Q\in\mathcal{W}_{i}
be such that
\gamma_{\widehat{z}_{i},w}^{U_{i}}(t)\in Q
for some
t\geq 0
, where
\widehat{z}_{i}=\phi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}(\widetilde{z}_{i})
. Let also
Q_{i}\in\mathcal{W}_{i}
be such that
\widehat{z}_{i}\in\mathrm{cl}(Q_{i})\setminus(\partial^{\mathrm{R}}Q_{i}\cup\partial^{\mathrm{U}}Q_{i})
and set
z_{i}=\mathrm{cen}(Q_{i})
. We pick
\widetilde{Q}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{i}
such that
\gamma^{\widetilde{U}_{i}}_{\widetilde{z}_{i},\varphi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}(w)}(t)\in\widetilde{Q}
. Since
\varphi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}(w)\in\widetilde{G}_{i}^{-}
, we have that
\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{U_{i}}(\widehat{z}_{i},\mathrm{cen}(Q))\lesssim\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\widetilde{U}_{i}}(\widetilde{z}_{i},\mathrm{cen}(\widetilde{Q}))\lesssim\widetilde{M}{\mathrm{len}}(\widetilde{Q})^{-a}
. Note that by (
2.6
),

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathrm{len}}(Q)}{{\mathrm{len}}(\widetilde{Q})}\asymp|\phi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}^{\prime}(\mathrm{cen}(\widetilde{Q}))|\asymp\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}})

since
\widetilde{U}_{i}\subseteq\mathcal{R}^{\epsilon}
and so

(7.5)

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{U_{i}}(\widehat{z}_{i},\mathrm{cen}(Q))\lesssim\widetilde{M}({\mathrm{len}}(Q)/\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}))^{-a}.

By applying Lemma
A.6
as in the proofs of Lemmas
4.12
and
4.13
, we have that (
7.5
) still holds if we replace
\widehat{z}_{i}
with
z_{i}
. This proves the claim.

We now turn to the case of the domain
U_{1}
, which has part of
\eta^{\mathrm{R}}([\sigma_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}},\tau_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}])
as part of its boundary. Let
\mathcal{W}_{1}
be a Whitney square decomposition of
U_{1}
. We claim that there exists
Q_{1}\in\mathcal{W}_{1}
such that the hyperbolic geodesics in
U_{1}
from
z_{1}=\mathrm{cen}(Q_{1})
satisfy (
7.5
) where
\widehat{z}_{i}
is replaced by
z_{1}
and
w\in G_{1}^{-}
for
\delta_{1}
sufficiently small and
M>1
sufficiently large. Here
G_{1}^{-}
is the set of
w\in\eta^{\mathrm{R}}(I_{0})
such that
\gamma_{z_{1},w}^{U_{1}}
intersects only
(M,a)
-good squares in
\mathcal{W}_{1}
. Note that condition (
i
) of Lemma
4.3
implies that
\varphi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}(z_{\mathrm{R}})\in(0,1)\times(\xi_{0},H_{0}-\xi_{0})
. Hence combining with the Beurling estimate we obtain that
\varphi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}(z_{\mathrm{R}})\in U_{1}
for
\delta_{1}>0
sufficiently small. A similar argument implies that the image under
\varphi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}
of the component of
B(z_{\mathrm{R}},\delta_{1}^{1/4}\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}))\setminus\eta([0,\tau_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}])
containing
z_{\mathrm{R}}
is a subset of
\widetilde{U}_{1}
, which implies that the component of
B(z_{\mathrm{R}},\delta_{1}^{1/4}\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}))\setminus\eta([0,\tau_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}])
containing
z_{\mathrm{R}}
is a subset of
U_{1}
. It follows that
\mu_{\eta}^{\mathrm{nat}}(G^{\mathrm{R}})/\mu_{\eta}^{\mathrm{nat}}(I_{0})\geq\frac{3}{4}
, where
G^{\mathrm{R}}
is the set of points
w\in\eta^{\mathrm{R}}(I_{0})
such that
\gamma_{z_{\mathrm{R}},w}^{U_{1}}
intersects only
(M,a)
-good squares in
\mathcal{W}_{1}
. Note that Lemma
4.3
implies that
\varphi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}(z_{\mathrm{R}})\in(\widetilde{\xi}_{0},1-\widetilde{\xi}_{0})\times(\widetilde{\xi}_{0},H_{0}-\widetilde{\xi}_{0})
and so we can find
\zeta>0
small enough depending only on the above implicit constants such that
\mathrm{dist}(\varphi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}(z_{\mathrm{R}}),\partial\widetilde{U}_{1})\geq\zeta
. By possibly taking
\zeta
to be smaller and applying Lemma
6.3
, we can find a constant
C<\infty
such that with probability at least
1-p_{0}/100
, we have that
\mathrm{dist}(\widetilde{Q}_{i},\partial\widetilde{U}_{i})\geq\zeta
and
\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\widetilde{U}_{i}}(x_{1},x_{2})\leq C
for each
x_{1},x_{2}\in\widetilde{U}_{i}
such that
\mathrm{dist}(\{x_{1},x_{2}\},\partial\widetilde{U}_{i})\geq\zeta
, for each
1\leq i\leq n
. Suppose that we are working on that event. Then we have that
\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{U_{1}}(z_{\mathrm{R}},z_{1})=\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\widetilde{U}_{1}}(\varphi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}(z_{\mathrm{R}}),\varphi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}(z_{1}))\leq C
and so applying Lemma
A.6
as in the proofs of Lemmas
4.12
and
4.13
, we have that
\gamma_{z_{1},w}^{U_{1}}
intersects only
(M,a)
-good squares in
\mathcal{W}_{1}
for each
w\in G^{\mathrm{R}}
(possibly by taking
M
to larger). A similar argument works for the domain
U_{n+1}
.

Step 2d. Condition (
3b
).

First, we note that if we consider
M>1
large enough, then with probability at least
1-p_{0}/100
condition (
IIIb
) is satisfied for
\widetilde{G}_{i}^{+}
(resp.
\widetilde{G}_{i}^{-}
) for each
1\leq i\leq n
(resp.
2\leq i\leq n+1
). Fix
1\leq i\leq n
and let
Q\in\mathcal{W}_{i}
be such that
{\mathrm{len}}(Q)=2^{-m}
,
m\in\mathbf{Z}
, and
\gamma_{\widehat{z}_{i},w}^{U_{i}}\cap Q\neq\emptyset
for some
w\in G_{i}^{+}
, where
\widehat{z}_{i}
is as in Step 2c. Let also
\widetilde{Q}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{i}
be such that
\widetilde{Q}\cap\varphi_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}(Q)\neq\emptyset
. Then (
2.6
) and (
7.4
) imply that there exists
C_{1}>1
depending only on
\epsilon
,
H_{0}
and
b
such that
C_{1}^{-1}2^{-m}\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}})^{-1}\leq{\mathrm{len}}(\widetilde{Q})\leq C_{1}2^{-m}\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}})^{-1}
. Hence combining with Lemma
A.2
, we obtain that the number of
Q\in\mathcal{W}_{i}
such that
{\mathrm{len}}(Q)=2^{-m}
and
\gamma_{\widehat{z}_{i},w}^{U_{i}}\cap Q\neq\emptyset
for some
w\in G_{i}^{+}
is at most a constant times
M2^{(3/2+a)m}\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}})^{3/2+a}
. We claim that the same holds if we replace
\widehat{z}_{i}
with
z_{i}
and possibly by taking
M
to be larger. Indeed, since
\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{U_{i}}(z_{i},\widetilde{z}_{i})\leq 1
, Lemma
A.6
implies that there exists a universal constant
C<\infty
such that
\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{U_{i}}(\gamma_{z_{i},w}^{U_{i}}(t),\gamma_{\widehat{z}_{i},w}^{U_{i}}(t))\leq C
for each
t\geq 0
. This implies that there exists a universal constant
\widetilde{C}>1
such that for each
Q\in\mathcal{W}_{i}
with
{\mathrm{len}}(Q)=2^{-m}
and
\gamma_{z_{i},w}^{U_{i}}\cap Q\neq\emptyset
for some
w\in G_{i}^{+}
, there exists
\widehat{Q}\in\mathcal{W}_{i}
with
\widetilde{C}^{-1}2^{-m}\leq{\mathrm{len}}(Q)\leq\widetilde{C}2^{-m}
,
\gamma_{\widehat{z}_{i},w}^{U_{i}}\cap\widetilde{Q}\neq\emptyset
and
B_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{U_{i}}(\mathrm{cen}(\widehat{Q}),\widetilde{C})\cap Q\neq\emptyset
for some
w\in G_{i}^{+}
. The claim then follows by combining with Lemma
A.1
. A similar argument holds for
G_{i}^{-}
and
2\leq i\leq n+1
.

It remains to show that condition (
3b
) holds for
G_{n+1}^{+}
and
G_{1}^{-}
. We will show that it holds for
G_{1}^{-}
. A similar argument works for
G_{n+1}^{+}
. Note that
\eta(I_{0})\subseteq B(z_{\mathrm{R}},\delta_{1}^{1/2}\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}))
and the component of
B(z_{\mathrm{R}},\delta_{1}^{1/4}\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}))\setminus\eta([0,\tau_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}])
containing
z_{\mathrm{R}}
is a subset of
U_{1}
. Hence we have that
\gamma_{z_{\mathrm{R}},w}^{U_{1}}\subseteq B(z_{\mathrm{R}},\delta_{1}^{1/3}\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}))
for each
w\in\eta(I_{0})
, if
\delta_{1}
is sufficiently small. This implies that
\mathrm{dist}(Q,\partial U_{1})=\mathrm{dist}(Q,\eta([\sigma_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}},\tau_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}]))
and so
Q
is contained in the
2^{-m+3}
-neighborhood of
\eta([\sigma_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}},\tau_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}])
for every square
Q\in\mathcal{W}_{1}
such that
{\mathrm{len}}(Q)=2^{-m}
and
\gamma_{y,w}^{U_{1}}\cap Q\neq\emptyset
for some
w\in G_{1}^{-}
. Let
N_{m}
be the number of such squares. Then, since the event
F_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}}^{2}
occurs, we obtain that
2^{-2m}N_{m}\lesssim M2^{m(a-1/2)}\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}})^{3/2+a}
and so
N_{m}\lesssim M2^{(3/2+a)m}\mathrm{diam}(A_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j+1}})^{3/2+a}
. The proof is then complete by applying Lemma
A.6
as in the previous paragraph.
∎

Proof of Proposition
7.2
.

Step 1. Density of scales at which
{\rm SLE}
regularity events hold.
Fix
a\in(0,1)
. For each
z\in\mathbf{H}
and
j\in\mathbf{N}
, let
D_{z,j}=\{\tau_{z,j}<\infty\}
,
G_{z,j}^{1}=\{\inf_{\tau_{z,j-1}\leq t\leq\tau_{z,j}}S_{t}\geq\delta_{0}\}
,
G_{z,j}^{2}=\{H_{z,j}\geq H_{0}\}
, and
let
G_{z,j}^{3}
be the event that condition (
i
) of Lemma
4.3
holds. Let also
G_{z,j}^{4}
be the event that the event
C_{z,j}
of Lemma
4.5
occurs,
G_{z,j}^{5}
be the event that condition (
ii
) of Lemma
4.3
holds and let
G_{z,j}^{6}
be the event that the event
F_{z,j}^{2}
in the statement of Lemma
4.4
occurs. We also set
G_{z,j}=\cap_{i=1}^{6}G_{z,j}^{i}
. Fix
K\subseteq\mathbf{H}
compact. For each
k\in\mathbf{N}
, we let
\mathcal{D}_{k}
be the set of squares of side length
e^{-5k}
and with corners in
(e^{-5k}\mathbf{Z})^{2}+(\frac{e^{-5k}}{2},\frac{e^{-5k}}{2})
which intersect
K
. Fix
Q\in\mathcal{D}_{k}
and let
z=\mathrm{cen}(Q)
. Then we have that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}[D_{z,\ell+1},G_{z,\ell}^{c}\,|\,\mathcal{G}_{z,\ell-1}]{\bf 1}_{D_{z,\ell-1}}\leq

\displaystyle\big(\mathbf{P}[D_{z,\ell+1},(G_{z,\ell}^{1})^{c}\,|\,\mathcal{G}_{z,\ell-1}]+\mathbf{P}[D_{z,\ell+1},G_{z,\ell}^{1},(G_{z,\ell}^{2})^{c}\,|\,\mathcal{G}_{z,\ell-1}]

(7.6)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\mathbf{P}[D_{z,\ell+1},G_{z,\ell}^{1},G_{z,\ell}^{2},\cup_{i=3}^{6}(G_{z,\ell}^{i})^{c}\,|\,\mathcal{G}_{z,\ell-1}]\big){\bf 1}_{D_{z,\ell-1}}.

Fix
q_{0}>0
. By Lemma
4.1
we can make
\delta_{0}>0
sufficiently small so that

(7.7)

\mathbf{P}[D_{z,\ell+1},(G_{z,\ell}^{1})^{c}\,|\,\mathcal{G}_{z,\ell-1}]{\bf 1}_{D_{z,\ell-1}}\leq q_{0}/100.

For the above choice of
\delta_{0}
, Lemma
4.2
implies that we can make
H_{0}>0
sufficiently small so that

(7.8)

\mathbf{P}[D_{z,\ell+1},G_{z,\ell}^{1},(G_{z,\ell}^{2})^{c}\,|\,\mathcal{G}_{z,\ell-1}]{\bf 1}_{D_{z,\ell-1}}\leq q_{0}/100.

Also part (
i
) of Lemma
4.3
implies that for the above choice of
H_{0}
and
\delta_{0}
, we can find
b\in(1,2)
,
\xi_{1}\in(0,(e^{-b}-e^{-2})/2)
and
\xi_{0}\in(0,H_{0}/2)
(depending only on
H_{0}
,
\delta_{0}
, and
q_{0}
) such that

(7.9)

\mathbf{P}[D_{z,\ell+1},G_{z,\ell}^{1},G_{z,\ell}^{2},(G_{z,\ell}^{3})^{c}\,|\,\mathcal{G}_{z,\ell-1}]{\bf 1}_{D_{z,\ell-1}}\leq q_{0}/100.

Moreover Lemma
4.5
implies that for the above choice of
H_{0}
,
\delta_{0}
,
a
,
b
, and
\xi_{2}=3\xi_{1}/2
, we have that for
\delta_{1}\in(0,1)
sufficiently small (depending only on
H_{0}
,
\delta_{0}
,
a
,
b
,
\xi_{1}
, and
q_{0}
), we can find
M_{0}>1
sufficiently large and
\delta_{2}\in(0,1)
sufficiently small such that

(7.10)

\mathbf{P}[D_{z,\ell+1},G_{z,\ell}^{1},G_{z,\ell}^{2},(G_{z,\ell}^{4})^{c}\,|\,\mathcal{G}_{z,\ell-1}]{\bf 1}_{D_{z,\ell-1}}\leq q_{0}/100.

Similarly part (
ii
) of Lemma
4.3
implies that for the above choice of
\delta_{0},H_{0},\delta_{1},b
and
\xi_{1}
with
\widetilde{\xi}_{1}=\delta_{2}
, we can find
\widetilde{\xi}_{0}\in(0,H_{0}/2)
such that

(7.11)

\mathbf{P}[D_{z,\ell+1},G_{z,\ell}^{1},G_{z,\ell}^{2},(G_{z,\ell}^{5})^{c}\,|\,\mathcal{G}_{z,\ell-1}]{\bf 1}_{D_{z,\ell-1}}\leq q_{0}/100.

Finally Lemma
4.4
implies that by possibly taking
M_{0}
to be larger, we have that

(7.12)

\mathbf{P}[D_{z,\ell+1},G_{z,\ell}^{1},G_{z,\ell}^{2},(G_{z,\ell}^{6})^{c}\,|\,\mathcal{G}_{z,\ell-1}]{\bf 1}_{D_{z,\ell-1}}\leq q_{0}/100.

Overall, (
7.6
) and (
7.7
)–(
7.12
) imply that the number of the even values of
(1-a^{2}/2)k\leq\ell\leq k
for which
D_{z,\ell+1}\cap G_{z,\ell}^{c}
occurs is stochastically dominated from above by a binomial random variable with parameters
n=\lfloor a^{2}k/4\rfloor
and
p=q_{0}
. Let
F_{z,k}
be the event that the number of even values of
(1-a^{2}/2)k\leq j\leq k
for which
D_{z,j+1}\cap G_{z,j}^{c}
occurs is at least
a^{2}k/8
. Then
[
34
, Lemma 2.6]
implies that by making
q_{0}>0
sufficiently close to zero, we have that
\mathbf{P}[F_{z,k}]\leq e^{-12k}
. Therefore by the Borel-Cantelli lemma there a.s. exists
k_{0}\in\mathbf{N}
so that
k\geq k_{0}
implies that if
Q\in\mathcal{D}_{k}
,
z=\mathrm{cen}(Q)
, and
D_{z,k}
occurs then the number of
(1-a^{2}/2)k\leq j\leq k
so that
G_{z,j}
occurs is at least
a^{2}k/8
.

Step 2. Density of good scales at which the regularity of crossings hold.
Fix
p_{0}\in(0,1)
. Then Lemma
7.8
implies that by possibly taking
\delta_{1}\in(0,1)
to be smaller and keeping all of the aforementioned parameters fixed, we can choose
M>1
sufficiently large and
\delta_{2}\in(0,1)
sufficiently small such that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\!\left[(E_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}^{M,a})^{c}\,|\,\mathcal{F}_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j-1}}\right]{\bf 1}_{{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j-1}<\infty}\leq p_{0}.

We recall the convention that
(E_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}^{M,a})^{c}=\emptyset
if
{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}=\infty
. We then have for
n,N\in\mathbf{N}
that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\!\left[\bigcap_{N\leq j\leq N+n}(E_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}^{M,a})^{c}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbf{E}\!\left[\mathbf{P}\!\left[(E_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{N+n}}^{M,a})^{c}\,|\,\mathcal{F}_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{N+n-1}}\right]{\bf 1}_{\left\{\bigcap_{N\leq j\leq N+n-1}(E_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}^{M,a})^{c}\right\}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq p_{0}\mathbf{P}\!\left[\bigcap_{N\leq j\leq N+n-1}(E_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}^{M,a})^{c}\right]\leq\dots\leq p_{0}^{n}.

Fix
\delta>0
(to be chosen) and assume that
0<p_{0}<\exp(-13/\delta)
. Applying the above for
n=\lfloor k\delta\rfloor
and
k\in\mathbf{N}
large enough we have that

(7.13)

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\!\left[\bigcap_{N\leq j\leq N+\lfloor\delta k\rfloor}(E_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}^{M,a})^{c}\right]\leq e^{-12k}\quad\text{for each}\,N\in\mathbf{N}.

By the Borel-Cantelli lemma, there a.s. exists
n_{0}\in\mathbf{N}
so that
n\geq n_{0}
implies that if
Q\in\mathcal{D}_{n}
and
z=\mathrm{cen}(Q)
then for each
1\leq N\leq n
, there exists
j\in\mathbf{N}
such that
N\leq j\leq N+\lfloor\delta n\rfloor
and
E_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}^{M,a}
occurs.

Step 3. Gap sizes between good scales.
Using the notation of Section
6.2
, we note that we can write

(7.14)

{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{j}({{\mathbf{k}}}_{\ell-1}^{*}+\Delta_{\ell}+4)

where
\Delta_{\ell}={{\mathbf{k}}}_{\ell}-({{\mathbf{k}}}_{\ell-1}^{*}+{{\mathbf{k}}}_{\ell-1}+4)
gives the gap size between the scale
{{\mathbf{k}}}_{\ell-1}+{{\mathbf{k}}}_{\ell-1}^{*}+4
and the next scale so that the event
G_{z,j}
occurs. We take the convention that
{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}^{*}=0
whenever
{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}=\infty
. Lemma
6.4
implies that there exist constants
c_{0}>1
and
c_{1}>0
(depending only on
H_{0}
,
\xi_{0}
, and
\xi
) so that

(7.15)

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}[{{\mathbf{k}}}_{\ell}^{*}\geq t\,|\,\mathcal{F}_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{\ell-1}}]{\bf 1}_{{{\mathbf{k}}}_{\ell-1}<\infty}\leq c_{0}\exp(-c_{0}^{-1}e^{c_{1}t})\quad\text{for each}\quad t\geq 0.

Fix
\theta>1
. By (
7.15
) we have that

(7.16)

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\!\left[e^{\theta{{\mathbf{k}}}_{\ell}^{*}}\,|\,\mathcal{F}_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{\ell-1}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq e^{c_{1}^{-1}\theta\log(c_{0}\theta)}+c_{0}\int_{c_{1}^{-1}\log(c_{0}\theta)}^{\infty}\exp(\theta t-c_{0}^{-1}e^{c_{1}t})dt.

It is easy to see that there exists a constant
c_{2}>1
(depending only on
c_{0}
and
c_{1}
) so that (
7.16
) is at most
c_{2}e^{c_{2}\theta\log\theta}
. We consequently have for any
N\in\mathbf{N}
that

\displaystyle\ \mathbf{P}\!\left[\sum_{j=1}^{\lfloor\delta n\rfloor}{{\mathbf{k}}}_{N+j}^{*}\geq a^{2}n/16\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}\!\left[e^{\sum_{j=1}^{\lfloor\delta n\rfloor}\theta{{\mathbf{k}}}_{N+j}^{*}}\geq e^{\theta a^{2}n/16}\right]\leq e^{-\theta a^{2}n/16}\mathbf{E}\!\left[e^{\sum_{j=1}^{\lfloor\delta n\rfloor}\theta{{\mathbf{k}}}_{N+j}^{*}}\right]

\displaystyle=e^{-\theta a^{2}n/16}\mathbf{E}\!\left[e^{\sum_{j=1}^{\lfloor\delta n\rfloor-1}\theta{{\mathbf{k}}}_{N+j}^{*}}\mathbf{E}\!\left[e^{\theta{{\mathbf{k}}}_{N+\lfloor\delta n\rfloor}^{*}}\,|\,\mathcal{F}_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{N+\lfloor\delta n\rfloor-1}}\right]\right]

\displaystyle\leq c_{2}e^{c_{2}\theta\log\theta-\theta a^{2}n/16}\mathbf{E}\!\left[e^{\sum_{j=1}^{\lfloor\delta n\rfloor-1}\theta{{\mathbf{k}}}_{N+j}^{*}}\right]

(7.17)

\displaystyle\leq\dots\leq c_{2}^{\delta n}e^{n\theta(c_{2}\delta\log\theta-a^{2}/16)}.

Choosing
\theta>1
large enough (depending only on
c_{2}
and
a
) so that
\theta a^{2}/32>12+\log(c_{2})
and then
\delta\in(0,a^{2}/144)
small enough so that
c_{2}\delta\log\theta<a^{2}/32
, we thus have that

(7.18)

\mathbf{P}\!\left[\sum_{j=1}^{\lfloor\delta n\rfloor}{{\mathbf{k}}}_{N+j}^{*}\geq a^{2}n/16\right]\leq e^{-12n}\quad\text{for each}\quad n,N\in\mathbf{N}.

Note that
\delta
is independent of
\delta_{1}
and so we can first choose
\delta
so that (
7.18
) holds and then
p_{0}\in(0,e^{-13/\delta})
so that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\!\left[(E_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}^{M,a})^{c}\,|\,\mathcal{F}_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j-1}}\right]{\bf 1}_{{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j-1}<\infty}\leq p_{0}.

for
\delta_{1}
small enough,
M
large enough and
\delta_{2}
small enough. Moreover, we note for
q>0
large enough (depending only on
c_{0}
and
c_{1}
) and
n\in\mathbf{N}
large enough by (
7.15
) and a union bound that

(7.19)

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}[\exists 1\leq j\leq n:{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}^{*}\geq q\log n]

\displaystyle\leq nc_{0}\exp(-c_{0}^{-1}n^{c_{1}q})\leq e^{-12n}.

It thus follows from (
7.18
), (
7.19
) and the Borel-Cantelli lemma that the following is true. There a.s. exists
n_{1}\in\mathbf{N}
so that
n\geq n_{1}
implies that for every
Q\in\mathcal{D}_{n}
with
z=\mathrm{cen}(Q)
we have
{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}^{*}\leq q\log n
for all
1\leq j\leq n
and
\sum_{j=1}^{\lfloor\delta n\rfloor}{{\mathbf{k}}}_{N+j}^{*}\leq a^{2}n/16
for all
1\leq N\leq n
.

Step 4. Conclusion of the proof.
Assume that
n\geq\max(n_{0},n_{1},k_{0})
. Fix
Q\in\mathcal{D}_{n}
,
z=\mathrm{cen}(Q)
and suppose that
\tau_{z,n}<\infty
. Let
J=\min\{j\in\mathbf{N}:{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}\geq n(1-a^{2})\}
; note that
J\leq n/5
as
{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}-{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j-1}\geq{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j-1}^{*}+4\geq 5
whenever
{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}<\infty
for every
j\in\mathbf{N}
. As
n\geq n_{1}
and
J\leq n/5
we have that

{{\mathbf{k}}}_{J-1}^{*}\leq q\log n\quad\text{and}\quad\sum_{j=1}^{\lfloor\delta n\rfloor}{{\mathbf{k}}}_{J+j-1}^{*}\leq a^{2}n/16.

Let
g_{1}
be the first
k\geq(1-a^{2})n
such that the event
G_{z,k}
occurs. For each
j\geq 2
, we inductively let
g_{j}
be the first
k>g_{j-1}
such that
G_{z,k}
occurs. Let

\widetilde{{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{j}=\sum_{\ell=0}^{j-1}({{\mathbf{k}}}_{J+\ell-1}^{*}+4).

We claim that

(7.20)

\displaystyle g_{\widetilde{{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{j}}\geq{{\mathbf{k}}}_{J+j-1}\quad\text{for each}\quad j\in\mathbf{N}.

We are going to prove (
7.20
) by induction on
j\in\mathbf{N}
. By definition,
g_{\widetilde{{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{1}}
is the
({{\mathbf{k}}}_{J-1}^{*}+4)
th scale
i
after
(1-a^{2})n
such that
G_{z,i}
occurs while
{{\mathbf{k}}}_{J}
is the first
i
after
{{\mathbf{k}}}_{J-1}+{{\mathbf{k}}}_{J-1}^{*}+4
such that
G_{z,i}
occurs. Since
{{\mathbf{k}}}_{J-1}\leq(1-a^{2})n
we have that
g_{\widetilde{{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{1}}\geq{{\mathbf{k}}}_{J}
. Suppose there exists
j\in\mathbf{N}
such that
g_{\widetilde{{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{i}}\geq{{\mathbf{k}}}_{J+i-1}
for each
1\leq i\leq j
. Then we have that
g_{\widetilde{{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{j+1}}
is the
({{\mathbf{k}}}_{J+j-1}^{*}+4)
th scale
i
after
g_{\widetilde{{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{j}}\geq{{\mathbf{k}}}_{J+j-1}
so that
G_{z,i}
occurs. On the other hand,
{{\mathbf{k}}}_{J+j}
is the first
i
after
{{\mathbf{k}}}_{J+j-1}^{*}+4
steps after
{{\mathbf{k}}}_{J+j-1}
so that
G_{z,i}
occurs. Thus it is easy to see that
g_{\widetilde{{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{j+1}}\geq{{\mathbf{k}}}_{J+j}
hence (
7.20
) holds. We thus have that

\displaystyle{{\mathbf{k}}}_{J+\lfloor\delta n\rfloor}

\displaystyle\leq g_{\widetilde{{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\lfloor\delta n\rfloor+1}}\quad\text{(by~\eqref{eqn:g_k_lbd})}

\displaystyle\leq g_{(a^{2}/16+8\delta)n+q\log n}\quad\text{(as $n\geq n_{1}$)}

\displaystyle\leq n\quad\text{(as $n\geq k_{0}$)}.

Note that
J\leq n/5<n
and so since
n\geq n_{0}
, we obtain that there exists
j\in\mathbf{N}
such that
J\leq j\leq J+\lfloor\delta n\rfloor
and
E_{z,{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}}^{M,a}
occurs. But then
{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}\geq{{\mathbf{k}}}_{J}\geq(1-a^{2})n
and
{{\mathbf{k}}}_{j}\leq{{\mathbf{k}}}_{J+\lfloor\delta n\rfloor}\leq n
. The proof is then complete since for each
z\in(e^{-5n}\mathbf{Z}^{2})\cap K
, there exists
Q\in\mathcal{D}_{n}
such that
z=\mathrm{cen}(Q)
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
1.1
.

We will show that
\eta
satisfies the conditions of Theorem
8.1
a.s., which completes the proof. We fix
a\in(0,1)
small enough such that
\tfrac{3}{2}\in(10a,2-10a)
. Then Proposition
7.2
implies that the following holds a.s. For every compact set
K\subseteq\mathbf{H}
, there exist
\widetilde{n}_{0}\in\mathbf{N}
and
M>1
such that for each
\widetilde{n}\geq\widetilde{n}_{0}
and
z\in(e^{-5(\widetilde{n}-1)}\mathbf{Z}^{2})\cap K
, there exists
\widetilde{m}\geq\widetilde{n}_{0}
with
(1-a^{2})(\widetilde{n}-1)\leq\widetilde{m}\leq\widetilde{n}-1
so that
E_{z,\widetilde{m}}^{M,a}
occurs.

For
m\in\mathbf{N}
we consider the family of sets
\mathcal{A}_{m}=\{A_{z,\widetilde{m}}:z\in\mathbf{H},m=\lfloor(4\widetilde{m}-\log(c_{1}/2))/\log(2)\rfloor+1\}

where
c_{1}\in(0,1)
is small enough (to be chosen and depending only on
a
) such that
B(z,c_{1}e^{-4m})
lies in the bounded component of
\mathbf{C}\setminus A_{z,m}
for each
z\in\mathbf{H}
such that
\tau_{z,m}<\infty
. Fix
K\subseteq\mathbf{H}
compact and
\widetilde{n}_{0}\in\mathbf{N}
as above. Let
n_{0}
be large (to be chosen) and
n\geq n_{0}
. Set
\widetilde{n}=\lfloor(\log(2)n+\log(c_{1}/2))/4\rfloor
. Then by picking
n_{0}
large enough, we have that
\widetilde{n}\geq\widetilde{n}_{0}
. Fix
z\in K\cap\eta
and let
w\in(e^{-5(\widetilde{n}-1)}\mathbf{Z}^{2})\cap K
be such that
|z-w|\leq 2e^{-5(\widetilde{n}-1)}
. Then there exists
(1-a^{2})(\widetilde{n}-1)\leq\widetilde{m}\leq\widetilde{n}-1
such that
E_{w,\widetilde{m}}^{M,a}
occurs. Set
m=\lfloor(4\widetilde{m}-\log(c_{1}/2))/\log(2)\rfloor+1
and note that
A_{w,\widetilde{m}}\in\mathcal{A}_{m}
. Also, by possibly taking
n_{0}
to be larger, we have that
B(z,2^{-n})
lies in the bounded component of
\mathbf{C}\setminus A_{w,\widetilde{m}}
. Moreover we have that
(1-a^{2})n\leq m\leq n
if
c_{1}
is sufficiently close to
0
(depending only on
a
). Furthermore we have that
\mathrm{diam}(A_{w,\widetilde{m}})\geq 2c_{1}e^{-4\widetilde{m}}\geq 4\cdot 2^{-m}
and
\mathrm{diam}(A_{w,\widetilde{m}})\leq 2c_{2}e^{-4\widetilde{m}}\leq 4c_{2}2^{-m}/c_{1}
, where
c_{2}
is a universal constant such that
A_{x,k}\subseteq B(x,c_{2}e^{-4k})
for every
x\in\mathbf{H}
such that
\tau_{x,k}<\infty
. Thus it follows that
\mathrm{diam}(A_{w,\widetilde{m}})\asymp 2^{-m}
where the implicit constants depend only on
c_{1}
and
c_{2}
. Then it is clear that conditions (
I
) and (
II
) are satisfied with
d_{i}=\tfrac{3}{2}
possibly by taking
M
to be larger. The proof is then complete by using that
\overline{\dim}_{\mathrm{M}}(K\cap\eta)\leq\dim_{\mathrm{M}}(\eta)=\tfrac{3}{2}
a.s.
[
47
,
5
]
, where for a set
A
,
\overline{\dim}_{\mathrm{M}}(A)
denotes the upper Minkowski dimension of
A
.
∎

8.
Removability theorem

8.1.
Setup and statement

Figure 7.
Illustration of the assumptions for Theorem
8.1
. Shown is a topological annulus
A\in\mathcal{A}_{k}
where
\partial^{\mathrm{in}}A
(resp.
\partial^{\mathrm{out}}A
) denotes its inner (resp. outer) boundary. The sets
U_{1},\ldots,U_{m}
(with
m=6
) from (
I
) are shown in green. The main assumption is (
II
), which controls the behavior of
\partial U_{i}\cap\partial U_{i+1}
for each
1\leq i\leq m
(where
U_{m+1}=U_{1}
).

Suppose that
X\subseteq\mathbf{C}
and that there exists
a\in(0,1)
such that for every
K\subseteq X
compact there exists
M>1
such that the following is true. Let
\overline{\dim}_{\mathrm{M}}(K)
be the upper Minkowksi dimension of
K
. Then we have that
\overline{\dim}_{\mathrm{M}}(K)<2
and
0<a<(2-\overline{\dim}_{\mathrm{M}}(K))/5
.

There exists a family
\mathcal{A}=\cup_{k=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{A}_{k}
of open sets such that every
A\in\mathcal{A}_{k}
has the topology of an annulus with
\mathrm{diam}(A)\leq M2^{-k}
. For such
A
we let
\partial^{\mathrm{in}}A
(resp.
\partial^{\mathrm{out}}A
) be the boundary of the bounded (resp. unbounded) component of
\mathbf{C}\setminus A
.

Moreover there exists
n_{0}\in\mathbf{N}
so that for all
n\geq n_{0}
and
z\in K
there exists
k\in\mathbf{N}
with
(1-a^{2})n\leq k\leq n
and
A\in\mathcal{A}_{k}
so that
B(z,2^{-n})
is contained in the bounded component of
\mathbf{C}\setminus A
and the following are true (see Figure
7
).

(I)

There exist
1\leq m\leq M
and pairwise disjoint, open, and simply connected subsets
U_{1},\ldots,U_{m}
of
A\setminus X
such that for each
1\leq i\leq m
there exists
I_{i}\subseteq\partial U_{i}\cap\partial U_{i+1}
closed (with the convention
U_{m+1}=U_{1}
and
U_{0}=U_{m}
) such that every loop
\gamma\colon\mathbf{S}^{1}\to\mathrm{cl}(\cup_{i=1}^{m}U_{i})
which hits each of the
I_{i}
exactly once and does not otherwise hit
\partial U_{i}\cap\partial U_{i+1}
disconnects
\partial^{\mathrm{in}}A
from
\partial^{\mathrm{out}}A
.

(II)

There exists
d_{i}\in(10a,2-10a)
and a positive and finite measure
\mu_{i}
supported on
I_{i}
so that the following properties hold. Let
\overline{\mu}_{i}
be
\mu_{i}
normalized to be a probability measure.

(a)

We have that
\mu_{i}(I_{i})\geq M^{-1}2^{-d_{i}k}
.

(b)

For every
Y\subseteq I_{i}
Borel we have that
\mu_{i}(Y)\leq M\mathrm{diam}(Y)^{d_{i}-a}
.

(c)

For each
1\leq i\leq m
let
\mathcal{W}_{i}
be a Whitney square decomposition of
U_{i}
. There exists a square
Q_{i}\in\mathcal{W}_{i}
such that the following is true. Let
G_{i}^{-}
be the set of
w\in I_{i-1}
(where
I_{0}=I_{m}
) so that if
\gamma_{i,w}
is the hyperbolic geodesic in
U_{i}
, from
\mathrm{cen}(Q_{i})
to
w
then for every
Q\in\mathcal{W}_{i}
hit by
\gamma_{i,w}
we have that
\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{U_{i}}(\mathrm{cen}(Q_{i}),\mathrm{cen}(Q))\leq M(2^{k}{\mathrm{len}}(Q))^{-a}
. We also let
G_{i}^{+}
be defined in the same way but with
I_{i}
in place of
I_{i-1}
. Then

(i)

\overline{\mu}_{i-1}(G_{i}^{-})\geq 3/4
and
\overline{\mu}_{i}(G_{i}^{+})\geq 3/4
and

(ii)

The number of elements of
\mathcal{W}_{i}
with side length
2^{-j}
which are hit by a hyperbolic geodesic in
U_{i}
from
\mathrm{cen}(Q_{i})
to a point in
G_{i}^{+}
(resp.
G_{i}^{-}
) is at most
M2^{(d_{i}+a)(j-k)}
(resp.
M2^{(d_{i-1}+a)(j-k)}
).

Theorem 8.1
.

Suppose that
D\subseteq\mathbf{C}
is open and
X\subseteq D
is closed in
D
and satisfies the above assumptions. If
f\colon D\to\mathbf{C}
is a homeomorphism onto its image and conformal on
D\setminus X
then
f
is conformal on
D
.

8.2.
Main lemma

The main step in the proof of Theorem
8.1
is the following lemma.

Lemma 8.2
.

Suppose that we have the setup described in Section
8.1
and Theorem
8.1
. There exists a finite and positive constant
M_{0}
depending only on
a
and
M
so that the following is true. Let
f\colon D\to\mathbf{C}
be a homeomorphism which is conformal on
D\setminus X
. Suppose that
A\in\mathcal{A}_{k}
satisfies (
I
) and (
II
) above. There exists a path
\gamma
in
A
which disconnects
\partial^{\mathrm{in}}A
from
\partial^{\mathrm{out}}A
such that

\displaystyle\mathrm{diam}(f(\gamma))\leq M_{0}\left(2^{(3a-1)k}+2^{(1-a)k}\int_{A\setminus X}|f^{\prime}(u)|^{2}du\right).

Proof.

This proof is in part inspired by that of
[
19
, Theorem 2]
and calculations will be similar in Step 2.

Step 1. An integral bound.
Fix
1\leq i\leq m
. Let
\mathcal{G}_{i}^{+}
(resp.
\mathcal{G}_{i}^{-}
) be the set of squares in
\mathcal{W}_{i}
which are hit by a hyperbolic geodesic in
U_{i}
from
z_{i}=\mathrm{cen}(Q_{i})
to a point of
G_{i}^{+}
(resp.
G_{i}^{-}
). For each
Q\in\mathcal{W}_{i}
, let
\mathrm{SH}_{i}(Q)
be the shadow cast on
\partial U_{i}
by the hyperbolic geodesics
\gamma_{i,w}
from
z_{i}
to boundary points
w\in\partial U_{i}
which pass through
Q
and let
\mathrm{SH}_{i}^{+}(Q)=\mathrm{SH}_{i}(Q)\cap G_{i}^{+}
and
\mathrm{SH}_{i}^{-}(Q)=\mathrm{SH}_{i}(Q)\cap G_{i}^{-}
. Moreover, we set
s_{i}^{+}(Q)=\mathrm{diam}(\mathrm{SH}_{i}^{+}(Q))
and
s_{i}^{-}(Q)=\mathrm{diam}(\mathrm{SH}_{i}^{-}(Q))
. We fix
w\in G_{i}^{+}
. By Lemma
A.3
, there exists a constant
c_{0}>0
so that

(8.1)

\mathrm{length}(f(\gamma_{i,w}))\leq c_{0}\sum_{Q\in\gamma_{i,w}}{\mathrm{len}}(Q)|f^{\prime}|(Q).

By integrating (
8.1
) over
I_{i}
against
\overline{\mu}_{i}
, we see that

\displaystyle\int\mathrm{length}(f(\gamma_{i,w})){\bf 1}_{G_{i}^{+}}(w)d\overline{\mu}_{i}(w)

\displaystyle\lesssim\int\sum_{Q\in\gamma_{i,w}}{\mathrm{len}}(Q)|f^{\prime}|(Q)d\overline{\mu}_{i}(w)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{G}_{i}^{+}}\overline{\mu}_{i}(\mathrm{SH}_{i}^{+}(Q)){\mathrm{len}}(Q)|f^{\prime}|(Q)

(8.2)

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-k(1-a)}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{G}_{i}^{+}}\overline{\mu}_{i}(\mathrm{SH}_{i}^{+}(Q))^{2}+2^{k(1-a)}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{G}_{i}^{+}}{\mathrm{len}}(Q)^{2}|f^{\prime}|(Q)^{2}.

By (
IIa
) we have that
\mu_{i}(I_{i})\geq M^{-1}2^{-d_{i}k}
hence
\overline{\mu}_{i}(\mathrm{SH}_{i}^{+}(Q))\leq M2^{d_{i}k}\mu_{i}(\mathrm{SH}_{i}^{+}(Q))
. Thus (
8.2
) is at most a (
M
-dependent) constant times

\displaystyle\frac{2^{-k(1-a)}}{2^{-2d_{i}k}}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{G}_{i}^{+}}\mu_{i}(\mathrm{SH}_{i}^{+}(Q))^{2}+2^{k(1-a)}\int_{A\setminus X}|f^{\prime}(u)|^{2}du

(8.3)

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{(2d_{i}-1+a)k}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{G}_{i}^{+}}\mu_{i}(\mathrm{SH}_{i}^{+}(Q))^{2}+2^{k(1-a)}\int_{A\setminus X}|f^{\prime}(u)|^{2}du.

We similarly have that

\displaystyle\int\mathrm{length}(f(\gamma_{i,w})){\bf 1}_{G_{i}^{-}}(w)d\overline{\mu}_{i-1}(w)\lesssim 2^{(2d_{i-1}-1+a)k}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{G}_{i}^{-}}\mu_{i-1}(\mathrm{SH}_{i}^{-}(Q))^{2}+2^{(1-a)k}\int_{A\setminus X}|f^{\prime}(u)|^{2}du.

Note that the implicit constants depend only on
M
. This leaves us to deal with the first term in (
8.3
) (and analogously the first term on the right hand side of the above).

Step 2. Bounding
\mu_{i}(\mathrm{SH}_{i}^{+}(Q))
.
By (
IIb
), we have that
\mu_{i}(\mathrm{SH}_{i}^{+}(Q))\leq Ms_{i}^{+}(Q)^{d_{i}-a}
. Therefore the first term in (
8.3
) is at most a constant (depending only on
M
) times

\displaystyle 2^{(2d_{i}-1+a)k}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{G}_{i}^{+}}s_{i}^{+}(Q)^{2(d_{i}-a)}.

We shall now bound this term. Fix
Q\in\mathcal{G}_{i}^{+}
such that
s_{i}^{+}(Q)>0
and
w_{1}=w_{1}(Q)
and
w_{2}=w_{2}(Q)
such that
w_{1},w_{2}\in\mathrm{SH}_{i}^{+}(Q)
and
|w_{2}-w_{1}|\geq s_{i}^{+}(Q)/2
. Then by the definition of
\mathrm{SH}_{i}^{+}(Q)
, the hyperbolic geodesics
\gamma_{i,w_{1}}
and
\gamma_{i,w_{2}}
intersect only squares in
\mathcal{G}_{i}^{+}
. Let
(Q_{Q}^{j,w_{1}})_{j\geq 0}
(resp.
(Q_{Q}^{j,w_{2}})_{j\geq 0}
) be the sequence of squares in
\mathcal{G}_{i}^{+}
, starting from
Q_{i}
which
\gamma_{i,w_{1}}
(resp.
\gamma_{i,w_{2}}
) hits. Then there exist
j_{1},j_{2}\in\mathbf{N}
such that
Q=Q_{Q}^{j_{1},w_{1}}=Q_{Q}^{j_{2},w_{2}}
. Writing
(\widehat{Q}_{Q}^{j})_{j\geq 0}\coloneqq(Q_{Q}^{j+j_{1},w_{1}})_{j\geq 0}
and
(\widecheck{Q}_{Q}^{j})_{j\geq 0}\coloneqq(Q_{Q}^{j+j_{2},w_{2}})_{j\geq 0}
, we obtain a bi-infinite sequence in
\mathcal{G}_{i}^{+}
connecting
w_{1}
and
w_{2}
. Since
\mathrm{diam}(Q^{\prime})=\sqrt{2}{\mathrm{len}}(Q^{\prime})
we have by (
2.4
) that

(8.4)

\displaystyle s_{i}^{+}(Q)\lesssim\sum_{j\geq 1}{\mathrm{len}}(\widehat{Q}_{Q}^{j})+\sum_{j\geq 1}{\mathrm{len}}(\widecheck{Q}_{Q}^{j}),

where the implicit constant is universal. We will now consider the cases
d_{i}-a<1
and
d_{i}-a\geq 1
separately, first focusing on the latter, and will show in both cases that

(8.5)

\displaystyle 2^{(2d_{i}-1+a)k}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{G}_{i}^{+}}s_{i}^{+}(Q)^{2(d_{i}-a)}\lesssim 2^{(3a-1)k}.

Step 2(i).
d_{i}-a\geq 1
.
As
s_{i}^{+}(Q)\leq\mathrm{diam}(A)\lesssim 2^{-k}
we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{G}_{i}^{+}}s_{i}^{+}(Q)^{2(d_{i}-a)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{G}_{i}^{+}}s_{i}^{+}(Q)^{2(d_{i}-a-1)}s_{i}^{+}(Q)^{2}\lesssim 2^{-2(d_{i}-a-1)k}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{G}_{i}^{+}}s_{i}^{+}(Q)^{2}

(8.6)

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-2(d_{i}-a-1)k}\left(\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{G}_{i}^{+}}\left(\sum_{j\geq 1}{\mathrm{len}}(\widehat{Q}_{Q}^{j})\right)^{2}+\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{G}_{i}^{+}}\left(\sum_{j\geq 1}{\mathrm{len}}(\widecheck{Q}_{Q}^{j})\right)^{2}\right)\quad\text{(by~\eqref{eqn:good_shadow_diam_bound})}.

We will only bound the first term in (
8.6
); the other term in (
8.6
) is bounded in an analogous manner. See also
[
19
, Page 275]
for a similar calculation.

For
Q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{W}_{i}
, we let

\displaystyle q_{i}(Q^{\prime})=\frac{1}{{\mathrm{len}}(Q^{\prime})^{2}}\int_{Q^{\prime}}\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{U_{i}}(z,z_{i})d\mathcal{L}_{2}(z).

We assume that
(\widehat{Q}_{Q}^{j})
is ordered according to when the squares are hit by
\gamma_{i,w_{1}}
after it first hits
Q
(breaking ties using any fixed convention).
It follows from Lemma
A.5
that

(8.7)

q_{i}(\widehat{Q}_{Q}^{j})\asymp q_{i}(Q)+j\quad\text{for all}\quad j\in\mathbf{N}_{0}.

Consequently,

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{G}_{i}^{+}}\left(\sum_{j\geq 1}{\mathrm{len}}(\widehat{Q}_{Q}^{j})\right)^{2}=

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{G}_{i}^{+}}\left(\sum_{j\geq 1}{\mathrm{len}}(\widehat{Q}_{Q}^{j})q_{i}(\widehat{Q}_{Q}^{j})^{3/4}q_{i}(\widehat{Q}_{Q}^{j})^{-3/4}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{G}_{i}^{+}}\left(\sum_{j\geq 1}{\mathrm{len}}(\widehat{Q}_{Q}^{j})^{2}q_{i}(\widehat{Q}_{Q}^{j})^{3/2}\right)\left(\sum_{j\geq 1}q_{i}(\widehat{Q}_{Q}^{j})^{-3/2}\right)\quad\text{(by Cauchy-Schwarz)}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{G}_{i}^{+}}\left(\sum_{j\geq 1}{\mathrm{len}}(\widehat{Q}_{Q}^{j})^{2}q_{i}(\widehat{Q}_{Q}^{j})^{3/2}\right)\left(\sum_{j\geq 1}(q_{i}(Q)+j)^{-3/2}\right)\quad\text{(by~\eqref{eqn:q_i_universal_bound})}

(8.8)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{G}_{i}^{+}}\left(\sum_{j\geq 1}{\mathrm{len}}(\widehat{Q}_{Q}^{j})^{2}q_{i}(\widehat{Q}_{Q}^{j})^{3/2}\right)(q_{i}(Q)+1)^{-1/2}.

For
\widetilde{Q}\in\mathcal{G}_{i}^{+}
, we let
\mathcal{Q}_{i,\widetilde{Q}}^{+}
be the set of
Q\in\mathcal{G}_{i}^{+}
such that
\widetilde{Q}=\widehat{Q}_{Q}^{j}
for some
j\in\mathbf{N}_{0}
. In other words,
\mathcal{Q}_{i,\widetilde{Q}}^{+}
is the set of
Q\in\mathcal{G}_{i}^{+}
such that the sequence of squares
(\widehat{Q}_{Q}^{j})
contains
\widetilde{Q}
. Then (
8.8
) is at most a constant times

(8.9)

\displaystyle\sum_{Q,\widetilde{Q}\in\mathcal{G}_{i}^{+}}{\mathrm{len}}(\widetilde{Q})^{2}q_{i}(\widetilde{Q})^{3/2}(q_{i}(Q)+1)^{-1/2}{\bf 1}_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{i,\widetilde{Q}}^{+}}=\sum_{\widetilde{Q}\in\mathcal{G}_{i}^{+}}{\mathrm{len}}(\widetilde{Q})^{2}q_{i}(\widetilde{Q})^{3/2}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{i,\widetilde{Q}}^{+}}(q_{i}(Q)+1)^{-1/2}.

We claim that

(8.10)

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{i,\widetilde{Q}}^{+}}(q_{i}(Q)+1)^{-1/2}\lesssim\sum_{j=1}^{q_{i}(\widetilde{Q})}j^{-1/2}\lesssim q_{i}(\widetilde{Q})^{1/2}

for every
\widetilde{Q}\in\mathcal{G}_{i}^{+}
distinct from
Q_{i}
, where the implicit constants are universal. Indeed, first we let
\widetilde{z}=\mathrm{cen}(\widetilde{Q})
. Lemma
A.5
implies that there exists a universal constant
C>1
such that

\displaystyle C^{-1}(q_{i}(Q)+j)\leq q_{i}(\widetilde{Q})\leq C(q_{i}(Q)+j)\quad\text{for all}\quad Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{i,\widetilde{Q}}^{+}\quad\text{with}\quad\widetilde{Q}=\widehat{Q}_{Q}^{j}.

Recalling from Section
2.1
that
\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{U_{i}}(z_{i},z)\asymp d_{\mathcal{W}_{i}}(z_{i},\mathrm{cen}(Q))
for all
z\in Q
, where the implicit constant is universal, we have that
\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{U_{i}}(z_{i},z)\lesssim q_{i}(Q)
for all
z\in Q
. Hence by taking
C
sufficiently large, we can assume that
\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{U_{i}}(z_{i},z)\leq Cq_{i}(Q)
for all
z\in Q
. Set

(8.11)

n=\lfloor Cq_{i}(\widetilde{Q})\rfloor+1

and for
1\leq j\leq n
, we let
\mathcal{F}_{j,\widetilde{Q}}^{i}
be the set of squares
Q
in
\mathcal{W}_{i}
for which a hyperbolic geodesic (parameterized by hyperbolic length) from
z_{i}
to some point of
\widetilde{Q}
passes through
Q
at some time
t\in[j-1,j)
. Then we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{i,\widetilde{Q}}^{+}}(q_{i}(Q)+1)^{-1/2}\leq\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{F}_{j,\widetilde{Q}}^{i}}(q_{i}(Q)+1)^{-1/2}\lesssim\sum_{j=1}^{n}|\mathcal{F}_{j,\widetilde{Q}}^{i}|j^{-1/2}

where the implicit constants are universal. Combining this with Lemma
A.4
completes the proof of (
8.10
).

Combining (
8.9
) with (
8.10
) we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{G}_{i}^{+}}\left(\sum_{j\geq 1}{\mathrm{len}}(\widehat{Q}_{Q}^{j})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\widetilde{Q}\in\mathcal{G}_{i}^{+}}{\mathrm{len}}(\widetilde{Q})^{2}q_{i}(\widetilde{Q})^{3/2}q_{i}(\widetilde{Q})^{1/2}=\sum_{\widetilde{Q}\in\mathcal{G}_{i}^{+}}{\mathrm{len}}(\widetilde{Q})^{2}q_{i}(\widetilde{Q})^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\widetilde{Q}\in\mathcal{G}_{i}^{+}}{\mathrm{len}}(\widetilde{Q})^{2}\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{U_{i}}(\widetilde{Q},z_{i})^{2}.

For the last inequality we used that
q_{i}(\widetilde{Q})\lesssim\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{U_{i}}(\widetilde{Q},z_{i})
for every
\widetilde{Q}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{i}
and some universal implicit constant.

Note that
\mathrm{diam}(Q)\leq\mathrm{diam}(A)\leq M2^{-k}\leq 2^{-k_{0}}
where
k_{0}=\lfloor k-\log_{2}(M)\rfloor
.
For
j\geq k_{0}
, we let
\mathcal{N}_{i,j}^{+}
be the family of squares in
\mathcal{G}_{i}^{+}
of side length
2^{-j}
. By (
II(c)ii
), we have that
|\mathcal{N}_{i,j}^{+}|\leq M2^{(d_{i}+a)(j-k)}
. Using that
d_{i}+3a-2<a
in the final inequality, we thus have

\displaystyle\sum_{\widetilde{Q}\in\mathcal{G}_{i}^{+}}{\mathrm{len}}(\widetilde{Q})^{2}\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{U_{i}}(\widetilde{Q},z_{i})^{2}=\sum_{j\geq k_{0}}\sum_{\widetilde{Q}\in\mathcal{N}_{i,j}^{+}}{\mathrm{len}}(\widetilde{Q})^{2}\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{U_{i}}(\widetilde{Q},z_{i})^{2}

(8.12)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j\geq k_{0}}\underbrace{2^{-2j}}_{{\mathrm{len}}(\widetilde{Q})}\cdot\underbrace{2^{(d_{i}+a)(j-k)}}_{|\mathcal{N}_{i,j}^{+}|}\cdot\underbrace{2^{2a(j-k)}}_{\widetilde{Q}\in\mathcal{G}_{i}^{+}}\lesssim 2^{-2k}\sum_{j\geq k_{0}}2^{(d_{i}+3a-2)(j-k)}\lesssim 2^{-2k}.

Combining the above and repeating the same argument for
(\widecheck{Q}_{Q}^{j})
, it follows that

\displaystyle 2^{(2d_{i}-1+a)k}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{G}_{i}^{+}}s_{i}^{+}(Q)^{2(d_{i}-a)}\lesssim 2^{(2d_{i}-1+a)k}\cdot\underbrace{2^{-2(d_{i}-a-1)k}}_{\eqref{eqn:sum_shadow_cubed_bound}}\cdot\underbrace{2^{-2k}}_{\eqref{eqn:wt_q_gi_plus_ubd}}\leq 2^{(3a-1)k}\quad\text{if}\quad d_{i}-a\geq 1.

That is, we have proved (
8.5
) in the case
d_{i}-a\geq 1
.

Step 2(ii).
0<d_{i}-a<1
.
We have that

(8.13)

\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{G}_{i}^{+}}s_{i}^{+}(Q)^{2(d_{i}-a)}\lesssim\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{G}_{i}^{+}}\left(\left(\sum_{j\geq 1}{\mathrm{len}}(\widehat{Q}_{Q}^{j})\right)^{2(d_{i}-a)}+\left(\sum_{j\geq 1}{\mathrm{len}}(\widecheck{Q}_{Q}^{j})\right)^{2(d_{i}-a)}\right).

Again, we will only bound the first term in (
8.13
). We have that

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{G}_{i}^{+}}\left(\sum_{j\geq 1}{\mathrm{len}}(\widehat{Q}_{Q}^{j})\right)^{2(d_{i}-a)}=\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{G}_{i}^{+}}\left(\sum_{j\geq 1}{\mathrm{len}}(\widehat{Q}_{Q}^{j})q_{i}(\widehat{Q}_{Q}^{j})^{3/4}q_{i}(\widehat{Q}_{Q}^{j})^{-3/4}\right)^{2(d_{i}-a)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{G}_{i}^{+}}\left(\sum_{j\geq 1}{\mathrm{len}}(\widehat{Q}_{Q}^{j})^{2}q_{i}(\widehat{Q}_{Q}^{j})^{3/2}\right)^{d_{i}-a}\left(\sum_{j\geq 1}q_{i}(\widehat{Q}_{Q}^{j})^{-3/2}\right)^{d_{i}-a}\quad\text{(by Cauchy-Schwarz)}

(8.14)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{G}_{i}^{+}}\left(\sum_{j\geq 1}{\mathrm{len}}(\widehat{Q}_{Q}^{j})^{2}q_{i}(\widehat{Q}_{Q}^{j})^{3/2}\right)^{d_{i}-a}\left(\sum_{j\geq 1}(q_{i}(Q)+j)^{-3/2}\right)^{d_{i}-a}\quad\text{(by~\eqref{eqn:q_i_universal_bound})}

Since
(x_{1}+\cdots+x_{n})^{p}\leq x_{1}^{p}+\cdots+x_{n}^{p}
for all
n\in\mathbf{N}
,
p\in(0,1)
and
x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\geq 0
, we have that (
8.14
) is at most a constant times

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{G}_{i}^{+}}\left(\sum_{j\geq 1}{\mathrm{len}}(\widehat{Q}_{Q}^{j})^{2(d_{i}-a)}q_{i}(\widehat{Q}_{Q}^{j})^{3(d_{i}-a)/2}\right)(q_{i}(Q)+1)^{-(d_{i}-a)/2}

(8.15)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\widetilde{Q}\in\mathcal{G}_{i}^{+}}{\mathrm{len}}(\widetilde{Q})^{2(d_{i}-a)}q_{i}(\widetilde{Q})^{3(d_{i}-a)/2}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{i,\widetilde{Q}}^{+}}(q_{i}(Q)+1)^{-(d_{i}-a)/2}.

Arguing as in the case
d_{i}-a\geq 1
and with
n
as in (
8.11
), we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{i,\widetilde{Q}}^{+}}(q_{i}(Q)+1)^{-(d_{i}-a)/2}\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{F}_{j,\widetilde{Q}}^{i}}(q_{i}(Q)+j)^{-(d_{i}-a)/2}\lesssim\sum_{j=1}^{n}|\mathcal{F}_{j,\widetilde{Q}}^{i}|j^{-(d_{i}-a)/2}

(8.16)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n}j^{-(d_{i}-a)/2}\lesssim q_{i}(\widetilde{Q})^{1-(d_{i}-a)/2}.

Combining (
8.15
) with (
8.16
), we obtain that

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{G}_{i}^{+}}\left(\sum_{j\geq 1}{\mathrm{len}}(\widehat{Q}_{Q}^{j})\right)^{2(d_{i}-a)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\widetilde{Q}\in\mathcal{G}_{i}^{+}}{\mathrm{len}}(\widetilde{Q})^{2(d_{i}-a)}q_{i}(\widetilde{Q})^{d_{i}-a+1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\widetilde{Q}\in\mathcal{G}_{i}^{+}}{\mathrm{len}}(\widetilde{Q})^{2(d_{i}-a)}\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{U_{i}}(\widetilde{Q},z_{i})^{d_{i}-a+1}.

Therefore if
0<d_{i}-a<1
, we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{\widetilde{Q}\in\mathcal{G}_{i}^{+}}{\mathrm{len}}(\widetilde{Q})^{2(d_{i}-a)}\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{U_{i}}(z_{i},\widetilde{Q})^{d_{i}-a+1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\geq k_{0}}\sum_{\widetilde{Q}\in\mathcal{N}^{+}_{i,j}}{\mathrm{len}}(\widetilde{Q})^{2(d_{i}-a)}\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{U_{i}}(z_{i},\widetilde{Q})^{d_{i}-a+1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j\geq k_{0}}\underbrace{2^{-2j(d_{i}-a)}}_{{\mathrm{len}}(\widetilde{Q})}\underbrace{2^{(j-k)(d_{i}+a)}}_{|\mathcal{N}_{i,j}^{+}|}\underbrace{2^{(d_{i}-a+1)a(j-k)}}_{\widetilde{Q}\in\mathcal{G}_{i}^{+}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-2(d_{i}-a)k}\sum_{j\geq k_{0}}2^{(j-k)(d_{i}+a+a(d_{i}-a+1)-2(d_{i}-a))}\lesssim 2^{-2(d_{i}-a)k}

since
d_{i}+a+a(d_{i}-a+1)-2d_{i}+2a\leq-d_{i}+5a<-a
and the implicit constant depends only on
M
and
a
. Altogether, we have shown that

\displaystyle 2^{(2d_{i}-1+a)k}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{G}_{i}^{+}}s_{i}^{+}(Q)^{2(d_{i}-a)}\lesssim 2^{(2d_{i}-1+a)k}\cdot 2^{-2(d_{i}-a)k}=2^{(3a-1)k}\quad\text{if}\quad 0<d_{i}-a<1.

That is, we have proved (
8.5
) in the case
0<d_{i}-a<1
.

Step 3. Combining the above bounds.
Finally, combining the bounds of Steps 1 and 2, we obtain that there exists
\widetilde{M}_{0}>0
depending only on
M
and
a
so that

(8.17)

\int\text{length}(f(\gamma_{i,w})){\bf 1}_{G_{i}^{+}}(w)d\overline{\mu}_{i}(w)\leq\widetilde{M}_{0}\left(2^{-(1-3a)k}+2^{k(1-a)}\int_{A\setminus X}|f^{\prime}(w)|^{2}d\mathcal{L}_{2}(w)\right).

A similar bound holds when we replace
G_{i}^{+}
by
G_{i}^{-}
and
\overline{\mu}_{i}
by
\overline{\mu}_{i-1}
. By applying Markov’s inequality, we obtain that the fraction of points in
I_{i}
(with respect to
\overline{\mu}_{i}
) which satisfy (
8.17
) but with
\widetilde{M}_{0}
replaced by
16\widetilde{M}_{0}
is at least
11/16
and similarly for the set
I_{i-1}
and the measure
\overline{\mu}_{i-1}
. By also applying the above reasoning to the domain
U_{i+1}
, we can find
w\in I_{i}
and paths
\gamma_{i,w}
and
\gamma_{i+1,w}
in
U_{i}
and
U_{i+1}
respectively which connect
w
to
z_{i}
and
z_{i+1}
respectively and such that

\displaystyle\text{length}(f(\gamma_{j,w}))\leq 16\widetilde{M}_{0}\left(2^{-(1-3a)k}+2^{k(1-a)}\int_{A\setminus X}|f^{\prime}(w)|^{2}d\mathcal{L}_{2}(w)\right)\quad\text{for}\quad j\in\{i,i+1\}.

By repeating the above procedure for all
i
, and concatenating the paths
\gamma_{i,w}
, we obtain a path satisfying the desired bound with
M_{0}=16M\widetilde{M}_{0}
.
∎

8.3.
Proof of Theorem
8.1

Step 1. Setup.
Let
f\colon D\to\mathbf{C}
be a homeomorphism onto its image which is conformal on
D\setminus X
. We will first show that
f
satisfies the ACL property on
D
and then deduce that
f
is conformal on
D
.

We fix a rectangle
F=[x_{1},x_{2}]\times[y_{1},y_{2}]\subseteq D
and assume that
K\subseteq X
is compact and contains
F\cap X
. Let
M,a
be the constants for
K
as described before the statement of Theorem
8.1
. We note that the assumption
0<a<(2-\overline{\dim}_{\mathrm{M}}(K))/5
implies in particular that
a\in(0,2/5)
.

For
t\in[y_{1},y_{2}]
we let
L_{t}=\{s+it:s\in\mathbf{R}\}
. Since
f
is conformal on
D\setminus X
, with
\mathrm{J}_{f}
the Jacobian of
f
, we have that
\int_{E}\mathrm{J}_{f}(z)d\mathcal{L}_{2}(z)=\mathcal{L}_{2}(f(E))
for every Borel set
E\subseteq D\setminus X
. Note that
\mathcal{L}_{2}(X)=0
. Indeed, since
X
is closed in
D
, it follows that there exists an increasing sequence of compact sets
(K_{n})_{n\geq 1}
so that
X=\cup_{n\geq 1}K_{n}
. By our assumptions, each
K_{n}
has upper Minkowski dimension strictly less than
2
hence
\mathcal{L}_{2}(K_{n})=0
. Therefore
\mathcal{L}_{2}(X)=0
as a countable union of sets of Lebesgue measure
0
. Moreover, since
\mathcal{L}_{2}(X)=0
, we obtain that
\mathrm{J}_{f}
is defined Lebesgue a.e. and
\int_{E}\mathrm{J}_{f}(z)d\mathcal{L}_{2}(z)\leq\mathcal{L}_{2}(f(E))
for every Borel set
E\subseteq D
. In particular,
\int_{F}\mathrm{J}_{f}(z)d\mathcal{L}_{2}(z)<\infty
since
\mathcal{L}_{2}(f(F))<\infty
as
f
is continuous, and since
|f^{\prime}|^{2}=\mathrm{J}_{f}
Lebesgue a.e., it follows that there exists
\widetilde{M}
finite such that

(8.18)

\displaystyle\int_{F}|f^{\prime}(w)|^{2}d\mathcal{L}_{2}(w)\leq\widetilde{M}.

Next, we fix
\xi\in(0,1)
small and for
N\in\mathbf{N}
and
t\in[y_{1},y_{2}]
we consider the following property:

(P_{t,N})

There exists
\delta>0
such that for every collection
((r_{i},s_{i}))_{i}
of pairwise disjoint intervals in
L_{t}\cap F
such that
\sum_{i}|s_{i}-r_{i}|<\delta
we have that
\sum_{i}|f(s_{i})-f(r_{i})|<\frac{1}{N}
.

We will show that the Lebesgue measure of the set of
t\in[y_{1},y_{2}]
such that
(P_{t,N})
holds for every
N\in\mathbf{N}
is at least
(1-\xi)(y_{2}-y_{1})
.

Step 2. Typical lines are good.

Step 2a. Bound on the number of intersections of
K
with a typical line.
For
k\in\mathbf{N}
,
t\in[y_{1},y_{2}]
, we partition the line
L_{t}\cap F
into segments of length
2^{-k}
with dyadic endpoints. We assume (without loss of generality) that
x_{2}-x_{1}=2^{n^{\prime}}
for some
n^{\prime}\in\mathbf{Z}
. We also let
I_{1,k}^{t},\dots,I_{n_{t}(k),k}^{t}
be the subfamily of the above intervals that
K
intersects and we set
I_{j,k}^{t}=[a_{j,k}^{t},b_{j,k}^{t}]
for
j=1,\dots,n_{t}(k)
. Since the upper Minkowski dimension of
K
is at most
2-5a
, it follows that
\limsup_{s\to\infty}\mathrm{Cont}_{2-4a}(K;s)=0
. In particular, there exists a finite constant
c_{1}
such that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{2}(\{z\in\mathbf{C}:\mathrm{dist}(z,K)\leq r\})\leq c_{1}r^{4a}\quad\text{for all}\quad r\in(0,1).

Then we have for all
k\in\mathbf{N}
that

\displaystyle\int_{y_{1}}^{y_{2}}2^{-k}n_{t}(k)d\mathcal{L}_{1}(t)\leq\mathcal{L}_{2}(\{z\in\mathbf{C}:\mathrm{dist}(z,K)\leq 2^{-k}\})\leq c_{1}2^{-4ak}.

Fix
c_{2}>0
(independent of
k
) and set
T_{k}=\{t\in[y_{1},y_{2}]:n_{t}(k)>c_{2}2^{(1-4a)k}\}
. Then, taking
c_{2}
sufficiently large (but still independent of
k
), Markov’s inequality implies that
\mathcal{L}_{1}(T_{k})\leq c_{1}/c_{2}\leq\xi(y_{2}-y_{1})/3
for all
k\in\mathbf{N}
.

Step 2b. Bound on the integral of
|f^{\prime}|^{2}
on a neighborhood of a typical line.
By Fubini’s theorem, we have for a constant
\widetilde{c}>0
that

(8.19)

\displaystyle\int_{y_{1}}^{y_{2}}\int_{B(L_{t},M2^{1-(1-a^{2})k})\cap F}|f^{\prime}(w)|^{2}d\mathcal{L}_{2}(w)dt\leq\widetilde{c}\widetilde{M}2^{-(1-a^{2})k}

Consequently, by taking
c_{3}
large (independently of
k
) the Lebesgue measure of the set of
t\in[y_{1},y_{2}]
such that

(8.20)

\int_{B(L_{t},M2^{1-(1-a^{2})k})}|f^{\prime}(w)|^{2}d\mathcal{L}_{2}(w)\leq c_{3}2^{-(1-a/2)k}

holds is at least
(y_{2}-y_{1})(1-\xi/3)
. Thus, the (one-dimensional) Lebesgue measure of the intersection of
[y_{1},y_{2}]\setminus T_{k}
with the set of
t\in[y_{1},y_{2}]
such that (
8.20
) holds is at least
(1-2\xi/3)(y_{2}-y_{1})
for all
k
sufficiently large.

Step 2c. Bound on the integral of
|f^{\prime}|^{2}
on a typical line.
We similarly have for all
c_{4}>0
sufficiently large (depending only on
\widetilde{M}
and
\xi
) that

\mathcal{L}_{1}\left(\left\{t\in[y_{1},y_{2}]:\int_{L_{t}\cap F}|f^{\prime}(w)|^{2}d\mathcal{L}_{1}(w)\geq c_{4}\right\}\right)\leq\frac{\widetilde{M}}{c_{4}}<\frac{\xi}{3}(y_{2}-y_{1}).

Step 2d. All three properties hold on a typical line.
Combining the above, we obtain that the one-dimensional Lebesgue measure of
t\in[y_{1},y_{2}]
such that the following hold is at least
(1-\xi)(y_{2}-y_{1})
:

(A)

n_{t}(k)\leq c_{2}2^{(1-4a)k}
,

(B)

\int_{B(L_{t},M2^{1-(1-a^{2})k})}|f^{\prime}(w)|^{2}d\mathcal{L}_{2}(w)\leq c_{3}2^{-(1-a/2)k}
, and

(C)

\int_{L_{t}\cap F}|f^{\prime}(w)|^{2}d\mathcal{L}_{1}(w)\leq c_{4}
.

Step 3. Bounding sums of over subintervals.
We fix
t\in[y_{1},y_{2}]
and for
1\leq j\leq n_{t}(k)
we fix
z_{j,k}^{t}\in K\cap I_{j,k}^{t}
. Then there exists
k_{j}\in\mathbf{N}
such that
(1-a^{2})k\leq k_{j}\leq k
and
A(z_{j,k}^{t},k_{j})\in\mathcal{A}_{k_{j}}
and the properties of
K
stated right above Theorem
8.1
hold for
A(z_{j,k}^{t},k_{j})
and
B(z_{j,k}^{t},2^{-k})
is contained in the bounded component of
\mathbf{C}\setminus A(z_{j,k}^{t},k_{j})
. Note that
z_{j,k}^{t}\in L_{t}\cap F
and so
A(z_{j,k}^{t},k_{j})\subseteq B(L_{t},M2^{1-(1-a^{2})k})
and
\partial^{\mathrm{in}}A(z_{j,k}^{t},k_{j})
surrounds
I_{j,k}^{t}
provided
k
is sufficiently large. Throughout this step, we assume that (
A
), (
B
), and (
C
) hold.

Next, we fix a collection
([r_{i},s_{i}])_{i}
of pairwise disjoint (except possibly at their endpoints) subintervals of
L_{t}\cap F
such that
\sum_{i}|s_{i}-r_{i}|<\delta
and
|s_{i}-r_{i}|\geq 2^{-k}
for all
i
. Here,
\delta>0
is small but fixed (to be chosen independently of
k
and depending only on
N
,
c_{1},\ldots,c_{4}
, and the implicit constants
M
,
a
of the properties of
K
). Note that each interval
I_{j,k}^{t}
can contain at most one of the
[r_{i},s_{i}]
. By subdividing each of the
[r_{i},s_{i}]
if necessary (which only makes the sum
\sum_{i}|f(s_{i})-f(r_{i})|
larger), we can further assume that each
[r_{i},s_{i}]
is either contained in one of the intervals
I_{j,k}^{t}
or is disjoint from all of them, except possibly at its endpoints. After performing this subdivision, each
I_{j,k}^{t}
can contain at most two of the
[r_{i},s_{i}]
. Moreover, if we have an interval
[r_{i},s_{i}]
which does not intersect one of the
I_{j,k}^{t}
, then clearly
|f(s_{i})-f(r_{i})|\leq\int_{[r_{i},s_{i}]}|f^{\prime}(w)|d\mathcal{L}_{1}(w)
. On the other hand, if we have an interval
[r_{i},s_{i}]
which is contained in one of the
I_{j,k}^{t}
and
\gamma_{j,k}
is the path in
A(z_{j,k}^{t},k_{j})
from Lemma
8.2
then since
\gamma_{j,k}
surrounds
I_{j,k}^{t}
, we have that
|f(s_{i})-f(r_{i})|\leq\mathrm{diam}(f(\gamma_{j,k}))
. Hence, it follows that

(8.21)

\sum_{i}|f(s_{i})-f(r_{i})|\leq\int_{\cup_{i}[r_{i},s_{i}]}|f^{\prime}(w)|d\mathcal{L}_{1}(w)+2\sum_{j=1}^{n_{t}(k)}\mathrm{diam}(f(\gamma_{j,k})).

By Lemma
8.2
, we have that

\displaystyle\mathrm{diam}(f(\gamma_{j,k}))

\displaystyle\leq M_{0}\left(2^{-(1-3a)k_{j}}+2^{(1-a)k_{j}}\int_{A(z_{j,k}^{t},k_{j})}|f^{\prime}(w)|^{2}d\mathcal{L}_{2}(w)\right)

\displaystyle\leq M_{0}\left(2^{-(1-a^{2})(1-3a)k}+2^{(1-a)k}\int_{A(z_{j,k}^{t},k_{j})}|f^{\prime}(w)|^{2}d\mathcal{L}_{2}(w)\right)

for all
j
. The Cauchy-Schwarz inequality together with (
C
) implies that the first term in the right-hand side of (
8.21
) is at most
\delta^{1/2}\left(\int_{L_{t}\cap F}|f^{\prime}(w)|^{2}d\mathcal{L}_{1}(w)\right)^{1/2}\leq\delta^{1/2}c_{4}^{1/2}
. Together with (
A
), this implies that

\displaystyle\sum_{i}|f(s_{i})-f(r_{i})|

\displaystyle\leq c_{4}^{1/2}\delta^{1/2}+2M_{0}\left(n_{t}(k)2^{-(1-a^{2})(1-3a)k}+2^{(1-a)k}\int\sum_{j=1}^{n_{t}(k)}{\bf 1}_{A(z_{j,k}^{t},k_{j})}(w)|f^{\prime}(w)|^{2}d\mathcal{L}_{2}(w)\right)

\displaystyle\leq c_{4}^{1/2}\delta^{1/2}+2M_{0}\left(c_{2}2^{-(a-a^{2}+3a^{3})k}+2^{(1-a)k}\int\sum_{j=1}^{n_{t}(k)}{\bf 1}_{A(z_{j,k}^{t},k_{j})}(w)|f^{\prime}(w)|^{2}d\mathcal{L}_{2}(w)\right).

Note also that if we pick a point
z\in L_{t}\cap F
, then
\sum_{j=1}^{n_{t}(k)}{\bf 1}_{A(z_{j,k}^{t},k_{j})}(z)
is equal to the number of annuli
A(z_{j,k}^{t},k_{j})
which contain
z
. We fix such an annulus
A(z_{i,k}^{t},k_{i})
. Noting that
A(z_{j,k}^{t},k_{j})\subseteq B(z_{j,k}^{t},M2^{-(1-a^{2})k})
and that the balls
B(z_{i,k}^{t},M2^{-(1-a^{2})k}),B(z_{j,k}^{t},M2^{-(1-a^{2})k})
intersect only if
|z_{i,k}^{t}-z_{j,k}^{t}|\leq M2^{1-(1-a^{2})k}
, we have that
\sum_{j=1}^{n_{t}(k)}{\bf 1}_{A(z_{j,k}^{t},k_{j})}(z)
is at most the number of
j
such that
B(z_{j,k}^{t},M2^{-(1-a^{2})k})
intersects
B(z_{i,k}^{t},M2^{-(1-a^{2})k})
. This, in turn, is at most
M2^{1+a^{2}k}+1\leq M2^{2+a^{2}k}
. It thus follows that

\displaystyle\sum_{i}|f(s_{i})-f(r_{i})|\lesssim\delta^{1/2}+2^{-(a-a^{2}+3a^{3})k}+2^{(1-a+a^{2})k}\int_{\cup_{j=1}^{n_{t}(k)}A(z_{j,k}^{t},k_{j})}|f^{\prime}(w)|^{2}d\mathcal{L}_{2}(w).

Since each set
A(z_{j,k}^{t},k_{j})
is contained in
B(L_{t},M2^{1-(1-a^{2})k})
we have by (
B
) that

\displaystyle\sum_{i}|f(s_{i})-f(r_{i})|

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta^{1/2}+2^{-(a-a^{2}+3a^{3})k}+2^{(1-a+a^{2})k}\int_{B(L_{t},M2^{1-(1-a^{2})k})}|f^{\prime}(w)|^{2}d\mathcal{L}_{2}(w)

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta^{1/2}+2^{-(a-a^{2}+3a^{3})k}+2^{(a^{2}-a/2)k}<\frac{1}{N}

for all
k\in\mathbf{N}
sufficiently large and
\delta>0
is chosen small and independent of
k
. Note also that the implicit constant in the above inequality is independent of
N
and
k
. Since
a\in(0,2/5)
, it follows that both exponents are negative.

Step 4. Deducing that
f
is ACL and hence conformal.
Let
G_{\delta,k,N}
be the set of
t\in[y_{1},y_{2}]
such that
(P_{t,N})
holds for
\delta
and intervals
([r_{i},s_{i}])_{i}
such that
|s_{i}-r_{i}|\geq 2^{-k}
for all
i
. The above implies that for each
N\in\mathbf{N}
and
\xi\in(0,1)
there exists
k_{0}=k_{0}(N)\in\mathbf{N}
and
\delta_{0}=\delta_{0}(N,\xi)>0
(i.e.,
\delta_{0}
depends on
N
,
\xi
but
k_{0}
depends only on
N
) such that for all
k\geq k_{0}
and
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
we have that
\mathcal{L}_{1}(G_{\delta,k,N})\geq(1-\xi)(y_{2}-y_{1})
. This implies that for all
k\geq k_{0}
we have that
\mathcal{L}_{1}(\cup_{r\in\mathbf{Q}_{+}}G_{r,k,N})=y_{2}-y_{1}
. As
\cup_{r\in\mathbf{Q}_{+}}G_{r,k,N}
is decreasing in
k
, we have that
\mathcal{L}_{1}(\cap_{k=1}^{\infty}\cup_{r\in\mathbf{Q}_{+}}G_{r,k,N})=y_{2}-y_{1}
. Note that if
(P_{t,N})
holds for every
N
, i.e.,
t\in\cap_{N=1}^{\infty}\cap_{k=1}^{\infty}\cup_{r\in\mathbf{Q}_{+}}G_{r,k,N}
then
f
is absolutely continuous on
L_{t}\cap F
. Thus, as
\mathcal{L}_{1}(\cap_{N=1}^{\infty}\cap_{k=1}^{\infty}\cup_{r\in\mathbf{Q}_{+}}G_{r,k,N})=y_{2}-y_{1}
, we see that
f
is absolutely continuous on
L_{t}\cap F
for Lebesgue a.e.
t\in[y_{1},y_{2}]
. Finally, by repeating the same argument for lines of the form
\{t+is:s\in\mathbf{R}\}
for
t\in[x_{1},x_{2}]
, we obtain that Lebesgue a.e.,
f
is absolutely continuous on segments of the form
\{t+is:s\in\mathbf{R}\}
for
t\in[x_{1},x_{2}]
or
\{it+s:s\in\mathbf{R}\}
for
t\in[y_{1},y_{2}]
. It follows that
f
has the ACL property on
D
. Moreover, since
\mathcal{L}_{2}(X)=0
and
f
is
1
-quasiconformal on
D\setminus X
, it follows that
f
is
1
-quasiconformal on
D
and so conformal on
D
as well. This completes the proof of the theorem.
∎

Appendix A
Whitney squares and the hyperbolic metric

In this appendix, we will collect several facts about Whitney squares and the hyperbolic metric which are used in this article.

Lemma A.1
.

Let
D
be a simply connected domain and
\mathcal{W}
a Whitney square decomposition of
D
. For
z\in D
and
r>0
, let
B_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{D}(z,r)\coloneqq\{w\in D:\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{D}(z,w)<r\}
. Then, for each
r>0
, there exists a positive constant
M(r)
such that for all
z\in D
,
B_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{D}(z,r)
intersects at most
M(r)
squares in
\mathcal{W}
. Moreover,
M(r)
is uniform in the choice of simply connected domain and Whitney square decomposition.

Proof.

Fix
a\in D
and let
\phi
be the unique conformal transformation mapping
\mathbf{D}
onto
D
such that
\phi(0)=a
and
\phi^{\prime}(0)>0
. Then the conformal invariance of the hyperbolic metric implies that
B_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{D}(a,r)=\phi(B_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\mathbf{D}}(0,r))
. Also we have that
B_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\mathbf{D}}(0,r)=B(0,a(r))
where
a(r)=\frac{e^{2r}-1}{e^{2r}+1}
(see
[
11
]
) and so
B_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{D}=\phi(B(0,a(r)))
. Moreover
[
26
, Theorem 3.21]
implies that
B_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{D}(a,r)\subseteq B(a,\frac{a(r)|\phi^{\prime}(0)|}{(1-a(r))^{2}})
. In particular we have that

(A.1)

\mathcal{L}_{2}(B_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{D}(a,r))\lesssim|\phi^{\prime}(0)|^{2}

where the implicit constant depends only on
r
. Let
Q\in\mathcal{W}
be such that
Q\subseteq B_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{D}(a,r)
. Then the Koebe-
\frac{1}{4}
theorem combined with
[
26
, Theorem 3.21]
implies that
\mathrm{dist}(\mathrm{cen}(Q),\partial D)\gtrsim|\phi^{\prime}(0)|
and so
\mathcal{L}_{2}(Q)=\ell(Q)^{2}\gtrsim|\phi^{\prime}(0)|^{2}
where the implicit constant depends only on
r
. Fix
M\in\mathbf{N}
and suppose that
Q_{j}\subseteq B_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{D}(a,r)
for
Q_{j}\in\mathcal{W}
and
1\leq j\leq M
are pairwise disjoint. By (
A.1
) we have that
\mathcal{L}_{2}(\cup_{j=1}^{M}Q_{j})=\sum_{j=1}^{M}\ell(Q_{j})^{2}\lesssim|\phi^{\prime}(0)|^{2}
and so
M\lesssim 1
where the implicit constant depends only on
r
. It follows that there exists
\widetilde{M}(r)>0
depending only on
r
such that for each
a\in D
, the hyperbolic ball
B_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{D}(a,r)
can contain at most
\widetilde{M}(r)
squares in
\mathcal{W}
. Note for each
Q\in\mathcal{W}
and
z,w\in Q
we have that
\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{D}(z,w)\leq 1
(which follows from (
2.1
) and the fact that the same holds for the quasihyperbolic diameter). The claim of the lemma then follows by setting
M(r)=\widetilde{M}(r+1)
since
Q\subseteq B_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{D}(a,r+1)
for every
Q\in\mathcal{W}
such that
Q\cap B_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{D}(a,r)\neq\emptyset
.
∎

Lemma
A.1
immediately implies the following.

Lemma A.2
.

There exists a universal constant
M>0
such that the following holds. Let
D,\widetilde{D}
be simply connected domains,
\varphi:D\to\widetilde{D}
conformal and let
\mathcal{W}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}
be a Whitney square decomposition of
D
and
\widetilde{D}
, respectively. Then, for any
Q\in\mathcal{W}
,
\varphi(Q)
intersects at most
M
Whitney squares in
\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}
and for any
\widetilde{Q}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}
,
\varphi^{-1}(\widetilde{Q})
intersects at most
M
Whitney squares in
\mathcal{W}
.

Proof.

This follows from Lemma
A.1
, since the hyperbolic diameter of any Whitney square is at most
1
.
∎

Lemma A.3
.

There exists a constant
c_{0}>0
so that the following is true. Suppose that
U,V\subseteq\mathbf{C}
are simply connected domains,
f\colon U\to V
is a conformal transformation, and
\mathcal{W}
is a Whitney square decomposition of
U
. Then for each
z\in U
and
w\in\partial U
we have that

(A.2)

\mathrm{length}(f(\gamma_{z,w}))\leq c_{0}\sum_{Q\in\gamma_{z,w}}{\mathrm{len}}(Q)|f^{\prime}|(Q),

where
\gamma_{z,w}
denotes the hyperbolic geodesic in
U
connecting
z
to
w
, by
Q\in\gamma_{z,w}
we mean that
\gamma_{z,w}
intersects the square
Q
, and
|f^{\prime}|(Q)={\mathrm{len}}(Q)^{-2}\int_{Q}|f^{\prime}(u)|du
.

Proof.

Suppose that
Q\in\gamma_{z,w}
. We parameterize
\gamma_{z,w}
by
[0,1]
and we set

\displaystyle s=\inf\{r\geq 0:\gamma_{z,w}(r)\in Q\}\quad\text{and}\quad t=\sup\{r\geq s:\gamma_{z,w}(r)\in Q\}.

Since
\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{U}(x,y)\leq 1
for all
x,y\in Q
, it follows for
r\in[s,t]
that

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{U}(\mathrm{cen}(Q),\gamma_{z,w}(r))

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{U}(\mathrm{cen}(Q),\gamma_{z,w}(s))+\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{U}(\gamma_{z,w}(s),\gamma_{z,w}(r))

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{U}(\mathrm{cen}(Q),\gamma_{z,w}(s))+\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{U}(\gamma_{z,w}(s),\gamma_{z,w}(t))\leq 2

as
\gamma_{z,w}
is a geodesic. Thus
\gamma_{z,w}([s,t])\subseteq\mathrm{cl}(B_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{U}(\mathrm{cen}(Q),2))
where
B_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{U}(z,r)=\{w\in U:\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{U}(z,w)<r\}
denotes the hyperbolic ball of radius
r
around
z
in
U
. Let
\phi:\mathbf{D}\rightarrow U
be the unique conformal transformation such that
\phi(0)=\mathrm{cen}(Q)
and
\phi^{\prime}(0)>0
. Note that
B_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\mathbf{D}}(0,2)=B(0,a)
where
a=\frac{e^{4}-1}{e^{4}+1}
(see
[
11
]
) and so the conformal invariance of the hyperbolic metric implies that if
\widetilde{\gamma}=\phi^{-1}(\gamma_{z,w}|_{[s,t]})
, then
\widetilde{\gamma}\subseteq B(0,a)
. Moreover, we have that

\displaystyle 1\geq\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\mathbf{D}}(\widetilde{\gamma}(s),\widetilde{\gamma}(t))=\int_{s}^{t}\frac{|\widetilde{\gamma}^{\prime}(r)|}{1-|\widetilde{\gamma}(r)|^{2}}dr\geq\text{length}(\widetilde{\gamma})

and so
\mathrm{length}(\widetilde{\gamma})\leq 1
. We let
Q_{1},\dots,Q_{k}\in\mathcal{W}
be such that
Q_{j}\cap B_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{U}(\mathrm{cen}(Q),2)\neq\emptyset
for each
1\leq j\leq k
and
B_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{U}(\mathrm{cen}(Q),2)\subseteq\bigcup_{j=1}^{m}\mathrm{cl}(Q_{j})
. Note that Lemma
A.1
implies that
m\leq M_{0}
for some universal constant
M_{0}
. Since for every square
Q_{j}
we can find a subfamily of squares of the above family which is a chain and connects
Q
to
Q_{j}
, it follows from (
2.4
) that
{\mathrm{len}}(Q_{j})\asymp{\mathrm{len}}(Q)
, where the implicit constants are universal. Consequently,
\mathrm{dist}(Q_{j},\partial U)\lesssim{\mathrm{len}}(Q_{j})\lesssim{\mathrm{len}}(Q)
for all
1\leq j\leq k
and so
\mathrm{dist}(\gamma_{z,w}(r),\partial U)\lesssim{\mathrm{len}}(Q)
for all
r\in[s,t]
. By the Koebe-
1/4
theorem, this implies that
|(\phi^{-1})^{\prime}(\gamma_{z,w}(r))|\gtrsim{\mathrm{len}}(Q)^{-1}
for all
r\in[s,t]
. Combining with the above, we obtain that
1\geq\mathrm{length}(\widetilde{\gamma})\gtrsim\mathrm{length}(\gamma_{z,w}([s,t])){\mathrm{len}}(Q)^{-1}
which implies that
\mathrm{length}(\gamma_{z,w}([s,t]))\lesssim{\mathrm{len}}(Q)
where the implicit constants are universal. Finally, by (
2.5
) it follows that
\mathrm{length}(f(\gamma_{z,w}\cap Q))\leq c_{0}{\mathrm{len}}(Q)|f^{\prime}|(Q)
for some universal constant
c_{0}>0
. The claim then follows by summing over
Q\in\gamma_{z,w}
.
∎

Lemma A.4
.

There exists a constant
c_{0}>0
so that the following is true. Suppose that
U\subseteq\mathbf{C}
is a simply connected domain,
\mathcal{W}
is a Whitney square decomposition of
U
,
Q_{0},Q_{1}\in\mathcal{W}
, and for
j\in\mathbf{N}
we let
\mathcal{F}_{j}
be the set of
\widetilde{Q}\in\mathcal{W}
for which a hyperbolic geodesic (parameterized by hyperbolic length) from
\mathrm{cen}(Q_{0})
to some point in
Q_{1}
passes through at some time
t\in[j-1,j)
. Then
|\mathcal{F}_{j}|\leq c_{0}
.

Proof.

Let
\phi\colon U\to\mathbf{D}
be the unique conformal transformation such that
\phi(\mathrm{cen}(Q_{0}))=0
and
\phi^{\prime}(\mathrm{cen}(Q_{0}))>0
. Fix
j\in\mathbf{N}
and suppose that
\widetilde{Q}\in\mathcal{F}_{j}
. We divide
\widetilde{Q}
into squares
\widetilde{Q}_{k}
for
1\leq k\leq 4
with sides parallel to the coordinate axes and equal side length
