{\mathrm{len}}(\widetilde{Q}_{k})={\mathrm{len}}(\widetilde{Q})/2
. Note that

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}(\mathrm{cen}(\widetilde{Q}_{k}),\partial U)\geq\frac{{\mathrm{len}}(\widetilde{Q}_{k})}{2}+\mathrm{dist}(\widetilde{Q},\partial U)\geq\left(1+\frac{1}{4\sqrt{2}}\right)\!\mathrm{diam}(\widetilde{Q}),

so that for
w\in\widetilde{Q}_{k}
, we have that

\displaystyle|w-\mathrm{cen}(\widetilde{Q}_{k})|\leq\frac{\mathrm{diam}(\widetilde{Q}_{k})}{2}=\frac{\mathrm{diam}(\widetilde{Q})}{4}\leq r_{0}\mathrm{dist}(\mathrm{cen}(\widetilde{Q}_{k}),\partial U)\quad\text{where}\quad r_{0}=\frac{\sqrt{2}}{1+4\sqrt{2}}\in(0,1).

Hence,
\widetilde{Q}_{k}\subseteq B(\mathrm{cen}(\widetilde{Q}_{k}),r_{0}\mathrm{dist}(\mathrm{cen}(\widetilde{Q}_{k}),\partial U))
and thus, by
[
26
, Corollary 3.25]
we have that

\displaystyle|\phi(w)-\phi(\mathrm{cen}(\widetilde{Q}_{k}))|\leq\frac{4|w-\mathrm{cen}(\widetilde{Q}_{k})|}{1-r_{0}^{2}}\frac{\mathrm{dist}(\phi(\mathrm{cen}(\widetilde{Q}_{k})),\partial\mathbf{D})}{\mathrm{dist}(\mathrm{cen}(\widetilde{Q}_{k}),\partial U)}\leq\frac{4r_{0}}{1-r_{0}^{2}}\mathrm{dist}(\phi(\mathrm{cen}(\widetilde{Q}_{k})),\partial\mathbf{D})

for all
w\in B(\mathrm{cen}(\widetilde{Q}_{k}),r_{0}\mathrm{dist}(\mathrm{cen}(\widetilde{Q}_{k}),\partial U))
. It follows that
\phi(\widetilde{Q}_{k})\subseteq B(\phi(\mathrm{cen}(\widetilde{Q}_{k})),pd)
where
p=\frac{4r_{0}}{1-r_{0}^{2}}
and
d=\mathrm{dist}(\phi(\mathrm{cen}(\widetilde{Q}_{k})),\partial\mathbf{D})
. Note that
p\in(0,1)
.

For
x,y\in\mathbf{D}
we let
\widehat{\gamma}_{x,y}
be the hyperbolic geodesic in
\mathbf{D}
from
x
to
y
parameterized by hyperbolic length and such that
\widehat{\gamma}_{x,y}(0)=x
. Let
\widehat{z}_{k}=\phi(\mathrm{cen}(\widetilde{Q}_{k}))
. Fix
j\in\mathbf{N}
and suppose that there exists
t\in[j-1,j)
and
\widehat{z}\in B(\widehat{z}_{k},pd)
such that
\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\mathbf{D}}(0,\widehat{z})\geq t
. Note that
\widehat{\gamma}_{0,\widehat{z}}
is the line segment connecting
0
to
\widehat{z}
. Let also
\widehat{w}\in B(\widehat{z}_{k},pd)
be such that
\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\mathbf{D}}(0,\widehat{w})\geq s
for some
s\in[j-1,j)
. Then we have that

\displaystyle|\widehat{\gamma}_{0,\widehat{z}}(j-1)-\widehat{\gamma}_{0,\widehat{w}}(j-1)|\leq|\widehat{z}-\widehat{w}|\leq|\widehat{z}-\widehat{z}_{k}|+|\widehat{z}_{k}-\widehat{w}|\leq 2pd.

Moreover, for all
w\in B(\widehat{z}_{k},pd)
it holds that
\mathrm{dist}(w,\partial\mathbf{D})\geq(1-p)d
and so
\mathrm{dist}(x,\partial\mathbf{D})\geq(1-p)d
for all
x\in I=[\widehat{\gamma}_{0,\widehat{z}}(j-1),\widehat{\gamma}_{0,\widehat{w}}(j-1)]
. Thus, it follows that

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\mathbf{D}}(\widehat{\gamma}_{0,\widehat{z}}(j-1),\widehat{\gamma}_{0,\widehat{w}}(j-1))\leq\int_{I}\frac{|dz|}{1-|z|^{2}}\leq\frac{2p}{1-p}.

Moreover,
\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\mathbf{D}}(\widehat{\gamma}_{0,\widehat{w}}(j-1),\widehat{\gamma}_{0,\widehat{w}}(r))\leq 1
and so
\widehat{\gamma}_{0,\widehat{w}}(r)\in B_{k}=B_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\mathbf{D}}(\widehat{\gamma}_{0,\widehat{z}}(j-1),\frac{1+p}{1-p})
for all
r\in[j-1,j)
.

By the conformal invariance of the hyperbolic metric we obtain the following. Fix
w\in Q_{1}
, pick some
t\in[j-1,j)
and let
\widetilde{Q}\in\mathcal{W}
be such that
\gamma_{\mathrm{cen}(Q_{0}),w}(t)\in\widetilde{Q}
. Then it holds that
\gamma_{\mathrm{cen}(Q_{0}),w}(t)\in\phi^{-1}(B_{k})
for some
1\leq k\leq 4
. In particular,
\widetilde{Q}\cap\phi^{-1}(B_{k})\neq\emptyset
. By Lemma
A.1
, we obtain that there exists some universal constant
M_{0}>0
such that for each
1\leq k\leq 4
,
\phi^{-1}(B_{k})
intersects at most
M_{0}
squares in
\mathcal{W}
, and so
|\mathcal{F}_{j}|\leq 4M_{0}
.
∎

Lemma A.5
.

Suppose that
U\subseteq\mathbf{C}
is a simply connected domain and
\mathcal{W}
is a Whitney square decomposition of
U
. Suppose that
Q_{0}\in\mathcal{W}
and for
Q\in\mathcal{W}
we let

\displaystyle q(Q)=\frac{1}{{\mathrm{len}}(Q)^{2}}\int_{Q}\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{U}(z,\mathrm{cen}(Q_{0}))d\mathcal{L}_{2}(z).

Suppose that
w\in\partial U
and
\gamma
is the hyperbolic geodesic from
\mathrm{cen}(Q_{0})
to
w
. Suppose that
Q\in\mathcal{W}
is hit by
\gamma
and let
(\widehat{Q}_{j})
be the sequence of squares hit by
\gamma
starting after it first hits
Q
and ordered according to when they are first hit by
\gamma
(breaking ties using any fixed convention). Then we have that

(A.3)

q(\widehat{Q}_{j})\asymp q(Q)+j\quad\text{for all}\quad j\in\mathbf{N}_{0}

with universal implicit constants.

Proof.

We will first prove that
q(\widehat{Q}_{j})\lesssim q(Q)+j
for all
j\in\mathbf{N}_{0}
. Let
\widehat{Q}
be the square with the same center as
Q
but with half the side length. Since
\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{U}(z,\mathrm{cen}(Q_{0}))\gtrsim d_{\mathcal{W}}(\mathrm{cen}(Q),\mathrm{cen}(Q_{0}))
for all
z\in\widehat{Q}
where the implicit constant is universal, it follows that

(A.4)

q(Q)\geq\frac{1}{{\mathrm{len}}(Q)^{2}}\int_{\widehat{Q}}\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{U}(z,\mathrm{cen}(Q_{0}))d\mathcal{L}_{2}(z)\gtrsim d_{\mathcal{W}}(\mathrm{cen}(Q),\mathrm{cen}(Q_{0})).

By the definition of
(\widehat{Q}_{k})
we can find
s<t
such that
\gamma(s)\in Q
,
\gamma(t)\in\widehat{Q}_{j}
,
\gamma([s,t])\subseteq\mathrm{cl}(\bigcup_{k=0}^{j}\widehat{Q}_{k})
and
\gamma([s,t])\cap\widehat{Q}_{k}\neq\emptyset
for all
1\leq k\leq j
. Let
\varphi:\mathbf{D}\rightarrow U
be the conformal transformation such that
\varphi(0)=\gamma(s)
and
a=\varphi^{-1}(\gamma(t))\in(0,1)
. Let
\mathcal{W}^{\prime}
be a fixed Whitney square decomposition of
\mathbf{D}
and let
(Q_{k}^{\prime})_{0\leq k\leq l}
be a chain of minimal length of adjacent squares in
\mathcal{W}^{\prime}
such that
0\in Q_{0}^{\prime}
and
a\in Q_{l}^{\prime}
. Note that
\varphi([0,a])=\gamma([s,t])
since
\gamma|_{[s,t]}
is the hyperbolic geodesic in
U
from
\gamma(s)
to
\gamma(t)
, and consequently
[0,a]\subseteq\mathrm{cl}(\bigcup_{k=0}^{j}\varphi^{-1}(\widehat{Q}_{k}))
. Moreover for all
0\leq k\leq j
there exists
z_{k}\in\varphi^{-1}(\widehat{Q}_{k})
such that
\varphi^{-1}(\widehat{Q}_{k})\subseteq B_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\mathbf{D}}(z_{k},2)
and so
[0,a]\subseteq\bigcup_{k=0}^{j}B_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\mathbf{D}}(z_{k},2)
. Note that Lemma
A.1
implies that
B_{\text{hyp}}^{\mathbf{D}}(z_{k},2)
can intersect at most
M_{0}
squares in
\mathcal{W}^{\prime}
for some universal constant
M_{0}
and hence
l\lesssim j
. Suppose that
l\geq 1
. Then
(\varphi(Q_{k}^{\prime}))_{0\leq k\leq l}
is a connected family of sets connecting
Q
to
\widehat{Q}_{j}
and by Lemma
A.2
, each
\varphi(Q_{k}^{\prime})
can intersect at most
\widetilde{M}_{0}
squares in
\mathcal{W}
for some universal constant
\widetilde{M}_{0}
. This implies that
d_{\mathcal{W}}(\mathrm{cen}(Q),\mathrm{cen}(\widehat{Q}_{j}))\lesssim l\lesssim j
and so by (
A.4
) we have that for all
z\in\widehat{Q}_{j}
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{U}(z,\mathrm{cen}(Q_{0}))\lesssim d_{\mathcal{W}}(\mathrm{cen}(Q_{0}),\mathrm{cen}(\widehat{Q}_{j}))\lesssim d_{\mathcal{W}}(\mathrm{cen}(Q_{0}),\mathrm{cen}(Q))+d_{\mathcal{W}}(\mathrm{cen}(Q),\mathrm{cen}(\widehat{Q}_{j}))\lesssim q(Q)+j

where the implicit constants are universal and so this gives
q(\widehat{Q}_{j})\lesssim q(Q)+j
for all
j\in\mathbf{N}_{0}
in the case that
l\geq 1
. When
l=0
, we have that
\{\gamma(s),\gamma(t)\}\subseteq\varphi(Q_{0}^{\prime})
and
\varphi(Q_{0}^{\prime})
can be covered by at most
\widetilde{M}_{0}
squares in
\mathcal{W}
. Therefore
d_{\mathcal{W}}(\mathrm{cen}(Q),\mathrm{cen}(\widehat{Q}_{j}))\lesssim 1\lesssim j
and similarly we obtain
q(\widehat{Q}_{j})\lesssim q(Q)+j
for all
j\in\mathbf{N}_{0}
.

We now turn to proving that
q(\widehat{Q}_{j})\gtrsim q(Q)+j
for all
j\in\mathbf{N}_{0}
. Let
j_{1}\in\mathbf{N}
be such that
Q
is the
j_{1}
th square in
\mathcal{W}
that
\gamma
intersects when the squares in
\mathcal{W}
are ordered according to when they are first hit by
\gamma
. We note that the analysis of the previous paragraph implies that
q(\widehat{Q}_{j})\gtrsim j+j_{1}
where the implicit constant is universal. Moreover, the collection of squares in
\mathcal{W}
which
\gamma
intersects up until it first hits
Q
is connected and connects
Q_{0}
to
Q
and thus we can find a subcollection of those squares which is a chain of squares connecting
Q_{0}
to
Q
. This implies that

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{U}(z,\mathrm{cen}(Q_{0}))\lesssim d_{\mathcal{W}}(\mathrm{cen}(Q_{0}),\mathrm{cen}(Q))\lesssim j_{1}\quad\text{for all}\quad z\in Q,

and hence
q(Q)\lesssim j_{1}
, where the implicit constant is universal. Combining, this completes the proof of (
A.3
).
∎

Recall that the inradius of a set
D\subseteq\mathbf{H}
with
0\in\partial D
is defined by
\mathrm{inrad}(D)=\sup\{r>0:B(0,r)\cap\mathbf{H}\subseteq D\}
. Furthermore, recall the notation
\gamma_{z,w}^{D}
in Section
2.1
.

Lemma A.6
.

Fix
C>0
and
r_{0}\geq 2
. Let
\widetilde{D}\subseteq D\subseteq\mathbf{C}
be simply connected domains,
z_{0},w_{0}\in\widetilde{D}
, and let
\zeta
be a prime end of
\partial D
which is also in
\partial\widetilde{D}
. Let
\phi:D\to\mathbf{H}
be the unique conformal map sending
\zeta
to
0
and
w_{0}
to
i
and let
r=\mathrm{inrad}(\phi(\widetilde{D}))
. Assume that
r\geq r_{0}
and

(A.5)

\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\widetilde{D}}(z_{0},w_{0})\leq C.

There exists a constant
c_{0}>0
depending only on
C
,
r_{0}
such that

(A.6)

\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\widetilde{D}}(\gamma_{z_{0},\zeta}^{\widetilde{D}}(t),\gamma_{w_{0},\zeta}^{D}(t))\leq c_{0}\quad\text{for all}\quad t\geq 0.

In order to prove Lemma
A.6
, we will first state and prove the following infinite length version.

Lemma A.7
.

Assume that we have the same setup as in Lemma
A.6
. Then there exists a constant
\widetilde{C}>0
depending only on
r_{0}
and
C
such that

(A.7)

\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\widetilde{D}}(\gamma_{z_{0},\zeta}^{\widetilde{D}}(t),\gamma_{w_{0},\zeta}^{D}(\mathbf{R}_{+}))\leq\widetilde{C}e^{-t}.

Proof.

By conformal invariance of hyperbolic distance, may assume that
D=\mathbf{H}
,
w_{0}=i
and
\zeta=0
. Note that the unit speed hyperbolic geodesic from
i
to
0
is
\gamma_{i,0}^{\mathbf{H}}(t)=ie^{-t}
,
t\geq 0
. Moreover, since the unique conformal automorphism
f
of
\mathbf{H}
which fixes
0
and sends
i
to
z_{0}=x_{0}+iy_{0}
is given by

\displaystyle f(z)=\frac{|z_{0}|^{2}z}{x_{0}z+y_{0}},

it follows that the unit speed hyperbolic geodesic from
z_{0}
to
0
is given by

(A.8)

\displaystyle\gamma_{z_{0},0}^{\mathbf{H}}(t)=\frac{|z_{0}|^{2}x_{0}e^{-2t}+i|z_{0}|^{2}y_{0}e^{-t}}{x_{0}^{2}e^{-2t}+y_{0}^{2}}.

Note here, that the distance to the hyperbolic geodesic
\gamma_{i,0}^{\mathbf{H}}(t)=ie^{-t}
,
t\in\mathbf{R}
, shrinks much faster than that to the boundary, due to the difference in exponents. Indeed, we have that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{Re}(\gamma_{z_{0},0}^{\mathbf{H}}(t))}{\mathrm{Im}(\gamma_{z_{0},0}^{\mathbf{H}}(t))}=\frac{x_{0}}{y_{0}}e^{-t}.

Consequently, by (
2.1
),

(A.9)

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\mathbf{H}}(\gamma_{z_{0},0}^{\mathbf{H}}(t),\gamma_{i,0}^{\mathbf{H}}(\mathbf{R}))

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{qh}}^{\mathbf{H}}(\gamma_{z_{0},0}^{\mathbf{H}}(t),\gamma_{i,0}^{\mathbf{H}}(\mathbf{R}))=\inf_{P:\gamma_{z_{0},0}^{\mathbf{H}}(t)\to i\mathbf{R}}\int_{P}\frac{|dz|}{|\mathrm{Im}(z)|}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{|\mathrm{Re}(\gamma_{z_{0},0}^{\mathbf{H}}(t))|}{\mathrm{Im}(\gamma_{z_{0},0}^{\mathbf{H}}(t))}=\frac{|x_{0}|}{y_{0}}e^{-t}.

By mapping
z_{0}
and
i
to
\mathbf{D}
with the map
\psi(z)=(i-z)/(i+z)
it is easy to see that the condition
\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\mathbf{H}}(z_{0},i)\leq C
gives a lower bound on
y_{0}
and an upper bound on
|z_{0}|
.

Let
\widetilde{D}^{*}
be the reflection of
\widetilde{D}
across
(-r,r)
and let
\varphi:\widetilde{D}^{*}\to\mathbf{C}
be the unique conformal map satisfying
\varphi(\widetilde{D})=\mathbf{H}
,
\varphi(0)=0
,
\varphi^{\prime}(0)=1
and
\varphi(-r_{0})=-s
and
\varphi(r_{0})=s
for some
s>0
. The proof idea is the following. We know that we have the desired exponential convergence of the image
\gamma_{\widetilde{z}_{0},0}^{\mathbf{H}}=\varphi(\gamma_{z_{0},0}^{\widetilde{D}})
to the geodesic
\gamma_{i,0}^{\mathbf{H}}
, so in order to check that
\gamma_{z_{0},0}^{\widetilde{D}}
converges towards
\gamma_{i,0}^{\mathbf{H}}
as well, conformally map it with
\varphi
and then check that
\varphi
does not perturb
\gamma_{i,0}^{\mathbf{H}}
too much. Indeed, this is the case as
\varphi
will locally look like the identity close to
0
.

We now formalize the ideas. We let
H^{*}=\varphi(\widetilde{D}^{*})
and note that
\varphi_{r}(z)\coloneqq\varphi(rz)
is a conformal map defined on
\mathbf{D}
and
\varphi_{r}:\mathbf{D}\to\varphi_{r}(\mathbf{D})\subseteq H^{*}
,
\varphi_{r}(0)=0
and
\varphi_{r}^{\prime}(0)=r
. Thus, writing
\widetilde{\varphi}(z)=\tfrac{1}{r}\varphi_{r}(z)
, then (considering the restriction to
\mathbf{D}
)
\widetilde{\varphi}:\mathbf{D}\to\widetilde{\varphi}(\mathbf{D})\subseteq\tfrac{1}{r}H^{*}
is conformal and
\widetilde{\varphi}(0)=0
and
\widetilde{\varphi}^{\prime}(0)=1
. By
[
26
, Proposition 3.26]
there is a universal constant
C_{*}
such that whenever
|z|\leq 1/2
, then
|\widetilde{\varphi}(z)-z|\leq C_{*}|z|^{2}
(in fact, the optimal value is
C_{*}=6
). In particular, when
|z|\leq r/2
,

(A.10)

\displaystyle|\varphi(z)-z|\leq C_{*}\frac{|z|^{2}}{r}.

It follows that for
s>\max(0,\log(2C^{*}/r))
, we have
\mathrm{Im}(\varphi(ie^{-s}))\geq e^{-s}/2
and consequently that

(A.11)

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\mathbf{H}}(\gamma_{i,0}^{\mathbf{H}}(s),\varphi(\gamma_{i,0}^{\mathbf{H}}(s)))\leq\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{qh}}^{\mathbf{H}}(\gamma_{i,0}^{\mathbf{H}}(s),\varphi(\gamma_{i,0}^{\mathbf{H}}(s)))\leq\frac{|ie^{-s}-\varphi(ie^{-s})|}{e^{-s}/2}\leq\frac{2C_{*}e^{-s}}{r}.

We let
T_{t}=\arg\,\min\{s\in\mathbf{R}:\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\mathbf{H}}(\gamma_{\widetilde{z}_{0},0}^{\mathbf{H}}(t),\gamma_{i,0}^{\mathbf{H}}(s))\}
and
\widetilde{z}_{t}=\widetilde{x}_{t}+i\widetilde{y}_{t}\coloneqq\gamma_{\widetilde{z}_{0},0}^{\mathbf{H}}(t)=\varphi(\gamma_{z_{0},0}^{\widetilde{D}}(t))
and note that by conformal invariance and the triangle inequality

(A.12)

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\widetilde{D}}(\gamma_{z_{0},0}^{\widetilde{D}}(t),\gamma_{i,0}^{\mathbf{H}}(\mathbf{R}))

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\mathbf{H}}(\gamma_{\widetilde{z}_{0},0}^{\mathbf{H}}(t),\gamma_{i,0}^{\mathbf{H}}(T_{t}))+\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\mathbf{H}}(\gamma_{i,0}^{\mathbf{H}}(T_{t}),\varphi(\gamma_{i,0}^{\mathbf{H}}(T_{t}))).

Moreover, we have by the distortion theorem (
[
26
, Theorem 3.21]
) that there is a universal constant
C^{\prime}>0
such that
\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\mathbf{H}}(\varphi(i),i)\leq C^{\prime}
. Thus, as above, the bound
\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\mathbf{H}}(\varphi(z_{0}),i)\leq\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\widetilde{D}}(z_{0},i)+\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\mathbf{H}}(\varphi(i),i)\leq C+C^{\prime}
provides an upper bound on
|\widetilde{z}_{0}|
and a lower bound on
\widetilde{y}_{0}
which depend only on
C
. By (
A.9
) the first term on the right-hand side of (
A.12
) is at most
\tfrac{|\widetilde{x}_{0}|}{\widetilde{y}_{0}}e^{-t}
. Thus, what remains is to handle the second term.

We begin by noting that

\displaystyle\log\frac{e^{-T_{t}}}{\widetilde{y}_{t}}=\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\mathbf{H}}(\gamma_{i,0}^{\mathbf{H}}(T_{t}),i\widetilde{y}_{t})\leq\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\mathbf{H}}(\gamma_{i,0}^{\mathbf{H}}(T_{t}),\widetilde{z}_{t})\leq\frac{|\widetilde{x}_{0}|}{\widetilde{y}_{0}}e^{-t}.

It follows that

(A.13)

\displaystyle T_{t}\geq\log(1/\widetilde{y}_{t})-\frac{|\widetilde{x}_{0}|}{\widetilde{y}_{0}}e^{-t}.

Thus, by the above mentioned bounds on
|\widetilde{x}_{0}|
and
\widetilde{y}_{0}
, the fact that
\widetilde{y}_{t}\asymp\tfrac{|z_{0}|^{2}}{\widetilde{y}_{0}}e^{-t}
where the implicit constant depends only on
C
(recall (
A.8
)) and (
A.11
), it follows that there exist constants
t_{0}\geq 0
and
C_{0}>0
depending only on
r_{0}
and
C
such that for
t\geq t_{0}
,
T_{t}\geq\max(0,\log(2C_{*}/r))
and

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\mathbf{H}}(\gamma_{i,0}^{\mathbf{H}}(T_{t}),\varphi(\gamma_{i,0}^{\mathbf{H}}(T_{t})))\leq C_{0}e^{-t}.

Thus, the proof is done for
t\geq t_{0}
, since then
\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\widetilde{D}}(\gamma_{z_{0},0}^{\widetilde{D}}(t),\gamma_{i,0}^{\mathbf{H}}(\mathbf{R}))=\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\widetilde{D}}(\gamma_{z_{0},0}^{\widetilde{D}}(t),\gamma_{i,0}^{\mathbf{H}}(\mathbf{R}_{+}))
.

Finally, we note that if
0\leq t\leq t_{0}
, then

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\widetilde{D}}(\gamma_{z_{0},0}^{\widetilde{D}}(t),\gamma_{i,0}^{\mathbf{H}}(\mathbf{R}_{+}))\leq\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\widetilde{D}}(\gamma_{z_{0},0}^{\widetilde{D}}(t),i)\leq\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\widetilde{D}}(z_{0},i)+t\leq C+t_{0},

which finishes the proof.
∎

Proof of Lemma
A.6
.

By applying the conformal map
\phi
, we can assume without loss of generality that
D=\mathbf{H}
,
w_{0}=i
, and
\zeta=0
. By Lemma
A.7
there exists
\widetilde{C}>0
depending only on
C,r_{0}
such that for each
t\geq 0

\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\widetilde{D}}(\gamma_{\widetilde{z}_{0},0}^{\widetilde{D}}(t),\gamma_{i,0}^{\mathbf{H}}(\mathbf{R}_{+}))\leq\widetilde{C}e^{-t}
. Fix
t\geq 0
and let
s\geq 0
be such that
\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\widetilde{D}}(\gamma_{\widetilde{z}_{0},0}^{\widetilde{D}}(t),\gamma_{i,0}^{\mathbf{H}}(s))\leq 2\widetilde{C}e^{-t}\leq 2\widetilde{C}
. Then by (
2.1
) and the fact that
\mathrm{dist}(\gamma_{i,0}^{\mathbf{H}}(u),\partial\widetilde{D})=\mathrm{dist}(\gamma_{i,0}^{\mathbf{H}}(u),\partial\mathbf{H})
for all
u>0
, we have that

(A.14)

\displaystyle t=\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\widetilde{D}}(z_{0},\gamma_{z_{0},0}^{\widetilde{D}}(t))

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\widetilde{D}}(z_{0},i)+\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{qh}}^{\widetilde{D}}(i,\gamma_{i,0}^{\mathbf{H}}(s))+\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\widetilde{D}}(\gamma_{i,0}^{\mathbf{H}}(s),\gamma_{z_{0},0}^{\widetilde{D}}(t))\leq C+s+2\widetilde{C}.

Since
\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{D_{2}}(z_{1},w_{1})\leq\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{D_{1}}(z_{1},w_{1})
whenever
D_{1}\subseteq D_{2}\subseteq\mathbf{C}

and
z_{1},w_{1}\in D_{1}
, it follows that

\displaystyle s

\displaystyle=\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\mathbf{H}}(i,\gamma_{i,0}^{\mathbf{H}}(s))\leq\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\widetilde{D}}(i,\gamma_{i,0}^{\mathbf{H}}(s))

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\widetilde{D}}(i,z_{0})+\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\widetilde{D}}(z_{0},\gamma_{z_{0},0}^{\widetilde{D}}(t))+\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\widetilde{D}}(\gamma_{z_{0},0}^{\widetilde{D}}(t),\gamma_{i,0}^{\mathbf{H}}(s))

(A.15)

\displaystyle\leq C+t+2\widetilde{C}.

Thus (
A.14
) and (
A.15
) imply that there exists a constant
c_{1}>0
depending only on
C,r_{0}
such that
|t-s|\leq c_{1}
. Finally, since
\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\widetilde{D}}(\gamma_{i,0}^{\mathbf{H}}(s),\gamma_{i,0}^{\mathbf{H}}(t))\leq\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{qh}}^{\widetilde{D}}(\gamma_{i,0}^{\mathbf{H}}(s),\gamma_{i,0}^{\mathbf{H}}(t))=|t-s|
we have that

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\widetilde{D}}(\gamma_{z_{0},0}^{\widetilde{D}}(t),\gamma_{i,0}^{\mathbf{H}}(t))

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\widetilde{D}}(\gamma_{z_{0},0}^{\widetilde{D}}(t),\gamma_{i,0}^{\mathbf{H}}(s))+\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{hyp}}^{\widetilde{D}}(\gamma_{i,0}^{\mathbf{H}}(s),\gamma_{i,0}^{\mathbf{H}}(t))\leq 2\widetilde{C}+c_{1}.

∎
